# Review record — 2026-11-01 codebase review

Status: EVIDENCE, written while the review ran. The durable record of the
2026-11-01 whole-codebase review: what was read, what was found, what was
split, what was fixed, and what is recommended. Companion to
`docs/design/SPLIT_COMMON.md`, `docs/design/SPLIT_COMPOSER.md` and
`docs/design/SPLIT_PERCEPTION.md`.

Every `file:line` cited by the audits is **as of commit 84f3de23** (the state
before the first split). The three splits moved most of `agents/common.py`,
`agents/composer.py` and `agents/perception.py` into siblings, so a line
number in those three files is a citation into the pre-split revision, not
an address in the tree.

## 1. Method

- **Backup first.** The parked layout-round work was committed (84f3de23) and
  the branch pushed; it had been 98 commits ahead of the remote.
- **Baseline before any change.** Full suite on `.venv`: 19,635 passed, 4
  xfailed, 0 failed, 281 s at `-n 6`. The offline real-pipeline driver
  (`tools/story_drive.py` on a scratch database) ran 23 turns with 0 errors in
  12 s; it was re-run after every split and is the between-changes engine
  smoke.
- **Reading.** 39 read-only audit lanes, one per subsystem, each given the
  same brief (what the project treats as a defect, the evidence standard, the
  registers it must not re-report) and a list of questions specific to its
  files. Every lane read its files in full and wrote a report; the reports are
  in this directory, unedited except for the scratch paths. Two
  information-gathering lanes digested the UNBUILT registers and measured the
  test suite (`tests_health_raw.json`).
- **Splitting.** One planner per large module produced a plan JSON; one tool
  (`tools/split_module.py`, landed by the tooling lane) applied it: a verbatim
  partition of every top-level statement, free-name analysis through
  `symtable` with annotations collected separately, a sibling DAG check, a
  facade with explicit re-exports of every former name, and a post-write
  re-import that compares the facade's namespace with the original's. The
  anatomy input came from `tools/module_anatomy.py`.
- **Fixing.** Seventeen fix lanes with disjoint file ownership, each writing a
  failing test before its fix and a log of what it fixed, skipped and
  requested; the language-runtime lane ran first because the others call the
  helpers it landed (`fold_key`, the corrected `name_boundary_pattern`,
  `split_sentences`).

## 3. Classes that recur across lanes

These are the shapes that appeared in five or more reports; each is a rule
the engine lacks rather than a defect in one file.

1. **English-only text assumptions in a language-pack engine.** `\b` word
   boundaries, `.split()` word counts, `[a-z0-9]` slugs and English word lists
   appear in every package. Measured consequences: a Japanese room name folds
   to the empty string (`normalize_room_id`), two kana-named owners' shed
   jackets collapse onto one object, a Japanese featured-resident history
   always fails its word-count gate, the disguise tripwire never fires, rumour
   degradation does nothing, a deaf card still hears, a Japanese question is
   never detected. The shared matcher `name_boundary_pattern` itself put no
   boundary at a kana edge. The fix is one set of script-aware helpers in
   `language_runtime` and the call sites routed through them.
2. **Substring matching where a boundary was meant.** Room names in goal
   text ("beginning" names the Inn), planned rooms ("the spinning mill" lands
   in the `inn`), beat-ending cues ("center" matches "enter": 52 of 153 live
   beat-ending acts matched inside a longer word), salience cues ("liar" in
   "familiar"), room-exposure words ("wood" in "wooden": 20 of 666 live rooms
   read falsely open), the entrance rank ("Great Hall" ranks as a hall).
3. **Two representations free to disagree.** The `world_entities`
   projection is skipped by the World Browser, `world_put`, the commit's own
   mints and the attire folds; `room_registry` is written without the scene by
   `plant_structure`; a body's whereabouts live in sixteen scene stores with
   writers that update fewer than all of them; the FTS mirror, the embedding
   model key, summaries and the rows they summarise each have one writer that
   forgets the other.
4. **Writes outside `transaction()`.** Plot clocks (`fire_due_clocks`),
   `publish_package`, mandates on four threads, artifact wording, background
   promotion, the charter's `save_registry`, World Browser read-modify-write,
   `planned_entities` marked `rendered` during preparation — each reproduced
   as a lost or doubled write.
5. **Module-level "last call" state read by the next caller.**
   `providers.last_reasoning` is not cleared between calls and is handed to
   the next mind in the interaction loop; the embedding coalescer serves queued
   callers on the leader's abort; the dressing caches outlive a deleted chat.
6. **Guards that fire on, or miss, valid output.** Facade monkeypatches that
   went inert and stayed green; `assert`-by-absence tests; a schema reachability
   test satisfied by a class name in a comment; the facade rule reading only
   absolute imports; the patch-target rule blind to string-form patches.

## 4. The splits

See the three plan notes. Lines before → after: `agents/common.py` 12,971 →
facade + 16 siblings (170-1,253 lines); `agents/composer.py` 7,691 → facade +
12 (315-1,279); `agents/perception.py` 9,323 → facade + 12 (357-1,470). Each
split was committed alone, with its repoints, and the full suite was green
after each (one pinned test and one doc line needed the new module names).
What the repoints taught: between a move and its repoints, 8 (common) and 23
(perception) tests failed and several more passed vacuously; ten of the
common repoints and all five module-source pins were invisible to
`project_check`, and only the splitter's repoint table found them.

The registers, `Design.md`, `AGENTS.md` and the guides were reconciled against

## 6. Recommendations and the optimization list

### 6.1 Structural recommendations (beyond this review's fixes)

1. **Decompose `director_resolve` (2,589 lines) and `director_interpret`
   (1,108).** A file split buys nothing while one function is the file; the
   phase maps with line ranges are in `review_2026_11_01/agents_director_core.md`
   § Structure notes. Not a verbatim move, so it needs the suite as its net and
   the dead staging payloads deleted first (same report, E1/E2).
2. **Split `web/app.py` by its twenty sections** into route modules behind
   the facade. The auth is a path-prefix middleware, so every route path must
   stay byte-identical; `STATIC_ROOT`, `_RECONCILE_THREAD` and `app` must move
   down into a leaf a sibling may import; `tools/mood_story_drive.py` patches
   `app._detach` and would go inert. Plan notes in `review_2026_11_01/web_app.md`.
3. **Split `llm/schemas.py` (fifteen sections) and `llm/providers.py`** —
   providers only after its 46 mutable module-level "last call" values are
   gathered into one per-call record (the `last_reasoning` leak is what that
   shape costs).
4. **Split the four large JS files** (`chat.js` → 5 files, `lorebooks.js` → 4,
   `settings.js` → 5, `world_browser.js` → 5); the section tables, the globals
   other files call and the parse-time calls are in the three frontend reports.
   Between 15 and 23 test files read each by path.
5. **One answer to "where is this body".** Eleven functions answer it with
   three identity rules and disagree (`world_spatial_truth.md` Q3); one answer
   to "does this text name this place" (five readers, `mind_affect_psychology.md`
   finding 2 — `find_names` in `language_runtime` is now that answer).
6. **Make the `world_entities` projection a consequence of writing the scene**
   — one seam every writer calls, or a projection derived on read — instead of a
   duty each writer remembers (four forgot).
7. **Decide the living-world tiers.** `LIVING_WORLD_APPROACHES = ()` and
   `max_offscreen_actors` forced to 0 make ~1,700 lines of `world/offscreen.py`
   and the ladder tables unreachable while Design.md, AGENTS.md and
   `docs/guides/LIVING_WORLD.md` describe them as live. Retire or revive is the
   owner's call.
8. **The registers are wrong in places the audits measured** (UNBUILT_WORLD
   §1.79 and §1.127, UNBUILT_PIPELINE §1.70 and UNBUILT_WORLD §1.28 naming code
   that no longer exists, UNBUILT_LIVING_WORLD §1.105 and §1.124,
   UNBUILT_IDENTITY §1.0, UNBUILT_PLATFORM §1.58 half-stale, UNBUILT_PLANNING
   §2.35, UNBUILT_PERCEPTION §1.146). Each report's "Docs checked" section lists
   the sentence and the code that disproves it.

### 6.2 Owner decisions the review surfaced (not decided here)

- The hearing table's curtain and glass prices, and the open questions of
  `DESIGN_SOUND_FIELD.md` §§ 11.9–20.6; the pinned test at
  `tests/test_sound_field.py:113` moves if the no-grid stamp lands.
- The charter: the 10 s budget does not hold for creature stories; the 720 h
  catch-up breaks the skip grid; the quarrel idle constant; whether a stretch
  the town never stepped reads as the mind's own.
- Autonomy 0 contested beats ask only the flow reactors.
- Whether the dressing (backdrop, sound bed) must obey the perception floor for
  an unconscious or asleep player.
- Multi-kanji name edges in `name_boundary_pattern` (open edges kept so 田中部長
  still matches); the backdrop strip's remaining any-mention English tables.
- The `auto_dialogue`-class caps the lanes named as constants: `MAX_AIMS`,
  `public_sources[:20]`, `_MEMORY_CONTEXT_POOL` (4 workers vs 8 skip minds),
  the skip's 40-stretch question, the courier slots, guest input size.
- `normalize_speech_volume`: the card says "soft" is normal while the alias
  table folds it to a mutter.
- Furniture placement never backtracks though its docstring says it does.

### 6.3 Optimization list (measured; largest first within each area)

Gate and suite: `make structure` 221–273 s (the 19,635-test suite is 281 s); a
shared parse takes it to ~150 s, the facade check 95 → 19.6 s, a token
pre-check 50.7 → 6.1 s, the map built once instead of four times; sixteen
test files re-parse the whole engine tree (100 s of the slowest forty); the
charter year test is 95.7 s because `_remember_experience` re-sorts the whole
list on every insert (17 s on record); the layout tests are 216 s of the top
forty.

Per beat, engine: perceiver maps built outside a read pass (413 → 92 ms per
stage on chat 175) and payload fields nothing reads (115–375 ms per stage);
the Director's interpret and resolve staging payloads built every beat and
read for one key; the repair pass recomputing `attribute()` 7–18 times a stage
(0.5–1.3 s CPU); three serial decision-model passes before every character
call (0.4 s each) and `active_state` ahead of the static card block, which
defeats provider prefix caching; the scene parsed 3–4 times inside the write
lock and `descriptor_bindings` 4–5 times a beat; the memory keyword search
scoring the whole database before filtering to the chat (53–102 ms, 1–4 times
per character); up to seven serial embedding requests per character; the
output guard's regex at 0.42 s per 20k characters with the GIL held (a slice
test is ~300× faster); a new HTTP connection per step thread; stranger-label
ladders recomputed per observer (0.35 s a beat in a room of six strangers);
sound floods per relation (18% of a sweep) and the listener's ear re-derived
per relation (17%); the merge's deep copies (29%) and `derive_site_plans`
(29%); the shadowcast per `body_visibility` call (409–912 ms per digest in
large yards); `plan_buildings` quadratic in lots; `apply_solved_plans` on every
`get_scene`.

Server and UI: `_stream` joining the pipeline thread on disconnect (every
request stalls until the turn ends); `chat_get` with 62 SQL statements and one
embedding-provider network call per refresh; `turn_branch` holding the global
write lock through a 4.1 s parse; six missing indexes on `turns(id)` foreign
keys (17.6 s → 0.035 s per deleted 1,000-turn chat); the World Browser's
whole-scene lint per request (22–58 ms, three to five times per edit);
`story_view` re-parsing the charter registry per call (76 ms); the guest
page re-downloading the transcript every 10 s; the checkpoint-conversion poll
at one full parse per second (49 s each on the owner's store); seventeen
identical `/models` requests on first open; `boot()` at 24 sites in settings
(1.23 MB each); the translator scanning 446 template rules per miss (about 5 s
per 1,000-card memory list in Japanese).

Which of these landed in this review is recorded in § 5.

## 7. Fix-lane logs

Each lane's log as it stood when this record was written, verbatim but for two
things: its heading levels are demoted three (so a log's own `#` title nests under
its `###` here) and its scratch paths read `<review scratch>/` -- the session's
scratch directory, which does not outlive the review. Audit report paths
(`<review scratch>/audits/<name>.md`) are the copies in `docs/experiments/review_2026_11_01/`.

### L1

*Source: `<review scratch>/fixes/L1_web_app.md`.*

#### L1 — web/app.py fix log (2026-11-01)

Lane: `web/app.py` (except `_remap_cp_blob`, `_deep_remap_ids`,
`_build_world_id_remap` and `turn_branch`'s `chat_char_frames` re-insert, L3's)
plus new tests. Every test below was run on `.venv` with
`-q -n 0 -p no:cacheprovider`, failing before its fix and passing after.

SUMMARY: 24 fixed (16 from the lane's list, 6 relayed requests — L2 x5, L15b x1 — and 2 found in other logs: L15a's two
`guest_state` fields, L8a's R9 raw-card warnings), 0 skipped from the list, 4 requests (FTS orphan sweep -> core/db owner; export
read-snapshot -> persist/chat_archive owner; drive tools wait for idle before `chat_export` -> tools owner; confirm_promotion's raw
sheet depends on L8a's tolerance being committed). 22 new test files, no existing test edited. Final run: the 22 files plus 15
existing suites that pin these routes — 367 passed.

###### persistence_paths_schema#F1 — `turn_del` nested the restore in its own transaction: deadlock with the startup about-backfill
STATUS: fixed
FILES: `web/app.py` (`turn_del`); new `tests/test_a_turn_delete_never_holds_the_write_lock_into_the_backfill.py`
TEST: `tests/test_a_turn_delete_never_holds_the_write_lock_into_the_backfill.py::test_a_delete_finishes_beside_a_backfill_that_holds_its_chat`
(drives the REAL `turn_del` against a thread holding `about_backfill`'s chat lock that then wants the write lock, bounded so a
regression fails instead of wedging the session) and `::test_the_restore_is_not_opened_inside_a_transaction_of_the_routes_own`. Before:
```
E   AssertionError: the startup pass waited out its timeout for the write lock: the delete held it into the restore's backfill
E   assert False is True
E   AssertionError: turn_del opens a transaction around its restore
```
WHAT CHANGED: the restore is now the OUTERMOST transaction, so its post-commit tail (embedding-bank probe = a provider round trip,
`backfill_chat`, `bump_world_epoch`) runs after its commit with no write lock held — the deadlock, the provider call under the lock and
the epoch bump before the outer commit (the report's (a) and (b)) all go. The turn's own deletes (variants, steps,
`delete_turn_memories`, events, turn row) and the "every checkpoint past the newest turn left" sweep run in `_decide`'s SECOND ask,
which `restore_checkpoint` makes inside its own transaction before restoring anything (detected with `db.conn().in_transaction`), so a
failed delete still rolls back with the restore (`test_app_audit_transactionality.py::TestTurnDelAtomicity` passes unchanged — its patch
of `app.delete_turn_memories` still intercepts). The sweep leaves out the carried beats, which `take_back` deletes right after. A turn
with no checkpoint (restore returns before asking) is deleted in a transaction of its own, as before.
BEHAVIOUR CHANGE TO KNOW: the turn's rows now go BEFORE the restore writes (inside the same transaction) instead of after it. A
checkpoint that already holds the deleted turn's OWN memories (a post-turn snapshot filed under the pre-turn idx — what
`refresh_checkpoint`'s no-checkpoint branch writes, persistence F6's class) now makes the delete refuse with
`IntegrityError: FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` and change nothing, where the old order re-inserted those memories and then deleted them
(scratch `l1/fk_edge.py`). Measured read-only on engine.db: 0 of 146 chats' newest turns have such a checkpoint; the one newest turn with
no checkpoint at all (chat 19, turn 347) has 0 memories, so a refresh could not make it one. `take_back` has always had the same order
for the carried beats.
REGISTER: none found naming this deadlock. `persist/checkpoints.py:1314-1317`'s comment "After the commit (an exception above never
reaches here on the rollback path)" is now true for every caller.

###### web_app#F1 — a client that disconnects mid-turn froze the whole server until the turn finished
STATUS: fixed
FILES: `web/app.py` (`_stream`); new `tests/test_a_client_that_leaves_mid_turn_never_stalls_the_server.py`
TEST: `tests/test_a_client_that_leaves_mid_turn_never_stalls_the_server.py::test_a_disconnect_mid_turn_does_not_stall_the_event_loop`
(the audit's repro as a test: the real `_stream` behind a FastAPI route, driven as ASGI, client gone after the first chunk, a 50 ms
heartbeat on the loop). Before:
```
E   AssertionError: the event loop stalled 2.93s after the client left: the stream waited out the turn on the loop
```
After: well under 1 s, and the generator still runs to its end on its own thread (asserted).
WHAT CHANGED: `w()`'s `finally` joins the drain thread only after DONE was dequeued (the run is over and the join is instant). On a
close (client gone, `GeneratorExit` on the event loop) it returns without joining; the run finishes on its drain thread and releases its
slot in `run_pipeline`'s `finally`, the `_detach` semantics. Events after the disconnect still go to the unread queue until the run ends
(bounded by one turn, as the audit noted).
REGISTER: none.

```
WHAT CHANGED: the guard reads the stored card's uid with `authored_uid` (identity block OR the flattened top level — the block-only

###### L2 request 5 (web_routes_extensions efficiency 3) — the guest page re-downloaded the whole transcript every 10 s (server half)
STATUS: fixed (server half; the page must adopt it — see protocol)
FILES: `web/app.py` (`guest_state` gains `since: float | None`); new `tests/test_a_guest_poll_sends_only_the_prose_that_changed.py`
TEST: `::test_a_page_that_sends_nothing_is_answered_whole`, `::test_since_the_mark_only_what_was_written_after_it_is_sent`,
`::test_the_flags_ride_every_row_whether_or_not_its_prose_does`. Before: `E   KeyError: 'mark'` (x3).
WHAT CHANGED / PROTOCOL FOR L15a (static/guest.html): every answer carries `mark` (the `created` of the newest active `narrator_extra`
variant) and every row carries `rev` (its own prose's `created`, or null). `GET /api/guest/state?since=<mark>` returns EVERY row with its
flags (`stale`, `stale_from`, `prose_stale`, `player_input`, `rev`), but `prose` only on rows whose prose was written after the mark (a
new beat, or an older one a rerun rewrote) or that have none. A row without `prose` keeps the held prose when the held `rev` equals the
row's `rev`; a different `rev` (the host re-activated an older rendering) means "read again without `since`". Rows absent from the answer
are gone (a delete). A page that sends no `since` gets exactly the old whole payload, so nothing changes until the page adopts it. The
read uses `active_mappings(cid, "narrator_extra", after_turn_id=...)` from the earliest changed turn, plus one chat-wide `rev` query
(`tests/test_transcript_read_is_one_query.py`'s flat statement count still holds). A timestamp rather than a turn id because SQLite hands
a deleted max rowid out again, so an id cannot say "rewritten".
REGISTER: none.

can still tear it. The report's stronger fix is to read the whole export inside one read transaction (one snapshot); that is the
service's own body.
REQUEST (owner of tools/live_drive.py, tools/quest_drive.py, tools/export_bench.py): they call `web.app.chat_export` in-process at the
end of a paid run, when an off-screen beat the run's last commit scheduled may still be running; it now answers that with
`HTTPException(409)` where it used to export a possibly torn story. Before exporting, wait for the chat to be idle — e.g.
`while agents.runtime.running_frames(cid): time.sleep(0.5)` — or catch the 409 and retry. quest_drive writes `story.json` BEFORE
`transcript.md`, so a refusal there costs both.
REGISTER: none.

###### web_routes_extensions#F3 — extension read surfaces handed out the pipeline's LIVE objects
STATUS: fixed
FILES: extension_runtime/api.py (`_private_copy`, `_turn_step`, `StepView`, `CommitView.step_content`, `PayloadContext/NarrationContext/DirectorContext.step`, `CommittedTurn.step_content`), extension_runtime/__init__.py (`notify_step_saved`), docs/guides/EXTENSIONS.md (§4 view/observers, §8 bullet)
TEST: tests/test_an_extension_reads_a_copy_of_the_turn.py (4 tests). Before:
`{'state_diff': {'positions': {}}} != {'state_diff': {'positions': {'Ash': 'hall'}}}` /
`{'resolved_event': 'nothing happened'} != {'resolved_event': 'Ash crosses to the door.'}` (observer);
`{'prose': 'hi', 'smuggled': 1} != {'prose': 'hi'}` (two observers); same diff via a stage's `view.state_diff` and via every `.step()`/`.step_content()`.
WHAT CHANGED: one helper `_private_copy` (deepcopy, JSON round trip as fallback, None if neither) is the rule `DirectorResult` already kept, now applied at every surface: each `on_step` observer gets its own copy; every `step()`/`step_content()` returns a copy; `StepView` copies once per key per view (so `resolve`/`state_diff`/`dialogue_log` agree and a loop does not re-copy). Cost is paid only when an extension reads.
REGISTER: EXTENSIONS.md §4/§8 edited by me (now true). No UNBUILT entry found for F3.

###### web_routes_extensions#F9 — a hook that edits in place then raises (or Director hook returning None) left an unattributed edit
STATUS: fixed
FILES: extension_runtime/__init__.py (`_run_payload_hooks`, `_changed_keys`; the three `dispatch_*_payload` now call it), docs/guides/EXTENSIONS.md (§4 rerouting paragraph)
TEST: tests/test_a_failed_payload_hook_changes_nothing.py (6 tests). Before: character hook →
`Left contains 1 more item: {'forbidden_fact': 'the vault code is 4471'}`; narration →
`{'perception': {'view': "the thief's face, clearly"}} != {'perception': {'view': 'a hallway'}}`; director raise →
`{'campaign_rule': 'the vault is already open'}` kept; director None → `assert [] == [{'changed': ['campaign_rule'], ...}]`; next hook saw `{'view': 'poisoned'}`.
WHAT CHANGED: one loop for the three seams: each hook gets a private deep copy, adopted only when it returns (dict, or None = the copy as edited, attributed); a raise or a non-dict return drops the copy and changes nothing; a raise is now counted in `observer_failures()` at the Director seam too (it was not). A payload deepcopy refuses falls back to the old live hand-over, and a failure then attributes whatever was left changed.
BEHAVIOUR NOTE: a Director hook that mutated in place and returned None used to have its edit applied silently; it is now applied AND attributed (same as the character/narration seams always did).
REGISTER: none found.

###### web_routes_extensions#F12 — changing the extension set mid-turn could kill the turn ("unknown step ext:...")
STATUS: fixed (runtime side) + request to L1 (409 mapping)
FILES: extension_runtime/__init__.py (`ExtensionBusy`, `_running_beats`, `_refuse_while_a_beat_runs`; enable/disable/install/update/remove), docs/guides/EXTENSIONS.md (§3 enable/disable, hot-loading)
TEST: tests/test_extensions_change_only_between_turns.py (4 tests), plus `test_an_update_is_refused_while_a_turn_runs` in the consent file. Before: `AttributeError: module 'extension_runtime' has no attribute 'ExtensionBusy'`; `DID NOT RAISE <class 'extension_runtime.api.ExtensionError'>` (off-screen slot).
WHAT CHANGED: enable, disable, install, update and remove raise `ExtensionBusy` (an `ExtensionError` subclass, exported) while ANY slot of `agents.runtime.ABORTS` is held (the registry both chat idle checks read; every chat, since the extension set is process-wide); an off-screen-only slot gets the off-screen wording. The check is made under `_lock` (the lock a turn's plan splice takes in `activate()`), so a turn that starts after the check is planned against the new set; install/update check once before the download (fail fast) and again under `_lock` right before the swap + `reload()`. `remove` checks BEFORE its error-swallowing `disable`. Enable is refused too (a turn half of whose payloads met a new hook is run against two sets) — wider than the finding by that one verb.
REQUEST (L1, web/app.py): map `ExtensionBusy` to 409 in the five routes, before the generic `ExtensionError` handler —
```python
except extension_runtime.ExtensionBusy as exc:
    raise HTTPException(409, str(exc)) from exc
```
in `extension_enable` (else 404), `extension_install` (else 400; also before the `except Exception` 502), `extension_update` (same), `extension_remove` (else 404), and wrap `extension_disable` (it has no try today, so until this lands a refused disable is a 500, refused enable/remove a 404, refused install/update a 400 — the turn is protected either way). REQUEST (owner of agents/runtime.py, optional backstop): in `compute_step`, a missing handler for an `ext:` key could yield a warned `{"error": "extension not loaded"}` instead of `RuntimeError("unknown step ...")`, so a stage that vanished anyway (a process-level reload outside these five calls) degrades like an `on_error="warn"` stage; with the guard it is defence in depth only.
REGISTER: EXTENSIONS.md edited by me. The sentence "Enable, disable, install, update and remove are **refused while any beat is running** … `ExtensionBusy`" does not name a status code, so it stays true whether or not the 409 lands.

TEST: tests/test_a_stage_anchor_names_a_real_step.py (9 tests). Before: `DID NOT RAISE <class 'extension_runtime.api.ExtensionError'>` for `after:director_resolv`, `after:mapping`, `before:narrator.establish`; `KeyError: 'typo'` (no disabled reason).

Fail-open if the runtime cannot be imported (the planner's skip stays the backstop). A real key a turn does not run is still "not planned that turn".

Left untouched (not mine; assertion unchanged); worth a rename by whoever owns it.

###### web_routes_extensions#F2 — DNS-rebinding during the unclaimed first-run window
STATUS: request (web/auth_routes.py and `access_control` in web/app.py are L1's)
ASSESSMENT: real in the window (fresh install, or every restart under `FICTION_ENGINE_RESET_HOST=1`): `request_is_local` reads only the TCP peer, so a rebinding page in the host's own browser is "local", claims setup, and gets a same-origin `fe_host` cookie (then `/api/bootstrap` provider keys are readable). Browser-dependent (Chromium's local-network-access checks may block it).
REQUEST (L1): allowlist the `Host` header — `127.0.0.1`, `localhost`, `[::1]` (any port) plus an operator-declared public name via env — at least on `/api/auth/setup`, better for every `/api/*`; refuse otherwise (421/403). A rebinding page cannot choose the Host its browser sends. Mind the tests: FastAPI's TestClient sends `Host: testserver`, so the allowlist needs a test-mode entry or the fixtures a `base_url`.

###### persistence_paths_schema#F3 — archive import never rekeyed character ids held inside `world` (and the checkpoint remap did not either)
- STATUS: fixed (class)
- FILES: persist/chat_archive.py (new table + `remap_world_character_ids`, `remap_row_character_ids`, `world_character_ids`; wired into import's live world, import's world-table payloads, export's resource collection), web/app.py (`_remap_cp_blob` only)
- TEST: tests/test_an_imported_story_keeps_every_character_id_it_holds.py (3 tests). Before (Mora 1 -> 6): `{'relationships': ['relationships:1', 'relationships:99']} != {'relationships': ['relationships:6']}`, `{'binding': 1} != {'binding': 6}`, `{'seat': 'character:1'} != {'seat': 'character:6'}`, `{'routes': ['1']} != {'routes': ['6']}`, `{'cast': {'a': [6, 99]...}} != {'cast': {'a': [6]...}}`.
- WHAT CHANGED: one table in chat_archive names every spelling of a `characters.id` in world state — a `char_id` field anywhere, a seat/handle token (`charter_model.MIND_SEED_PREFIXES`: `character:N`, `cast:N`) as value / dict key / list member, the `relationships:<id>` key family (frame suffix kept), the keys of `character_history_routes` / `character_journey_histories` / `charter_resident_histories`, and couple maps' `cast` lists. Import runs it over the live world and the world-table `payload`/`detail` JSON; `_remap_cp_blob` runs it over every imported checkpoint (only when `char_idmap` is given — a same-install branch passes none). An unmapped id is dropped (key/record/list member) or nulled (field/value), never kept. `_remap_couple_frames` is now handed `char_idmap=None` by both callers because the table already moved the cast (it kept unmapped ids verbatim; that function is L1's and is unchanged). The export also bundles every character the live world references, so the import has a row to map them to.
- COST: the walk is in place and rejects strings by first letter: 0.39 s over a 3.9 MB world vs 0.28 s `json.loads` of it (first version, copying, was 0.71 s). It runs only on cross-install import (live world once, each checkpoint world once); a 41 MB Charter row adds ~4 s per imported checkpoint. Branch is unaffected.
- RESIDUAL (not done, same class, outside world state): `steps.key = 'character:<id>'` and `actor: 'character:<id>'` handles inside variant JSON are copied verbatim by import; `perception_conduct` reads the previous turn's `character:<id>` step by the live id, so the first beat after a cross-install import misses the prior turn's conduct. Persona ids in couple maps (`personas`) still keep an unmapped id verbatim (`_remap_couple_frames`, L1).
- REGISTER: none found for `relationships:` import (`grep -n "relationships:" docs/UNBUILT*.md`).

###### persistence_paths_schema#F15 — capture retention never ran (`prune`, `vacuum_blobs` had no callers)
- STATUS: fixed
- FILES: persist/llm_capture.py (`prune` rewritten; `record_exchange` calls it), persist/chat_delete.py (`delete_chat_data` calls `vacuum_blobs`)
- TEST: tests/test_a_capture_is_kept_to_its_window_and_dies_with_its_story.py (2 tests). Before: `assert [1, 2, 3, 4, 5] == [3, 4, 5]` (RETAIN_TURNS patched to 3) and `assert "the doomed story's prose" not in {...}` after deleting the story.
- WHAT CHANGED: `record_exchange` prunes the turn's chat on that turn's FIRST captured call (seq 1) — when capture is on, off by default — and `prune` is one `DELETE ... WHERE turn_id IN (turns past the window)` statement (it was one DELETE per old turn, every time). `delete_chat_data` runs `vacuum_blobs()` after its transaction commits, so a deleted story's captured text leaves `llm_blobs`; blobs any other capture row names stay.
- NOT DONE / OWNER: blobs orphaned by a PRUNE (not a deletion) wait for the next story deletion's vacuum — running the whole-table vacuum per turn would be a new cost/rate the owner should choose. `RETAIN_TURNS = 200` is unchanged (already a named constant). `vacuum_blobs` reads every capture row; on chat deletion that is O(capture store).
- REGISTER: DATABASE.md:78 ("pruned to `RETAIN_TURNS` per chat") is now true; F20 adds where blobs are collected.

###### persistence_paths_schema#P4 (+P3) — `turn_branch` holds the global write lock while parsing, deep-remapping and re-dumping every checkpoint
- STATUS: partly fixed through my functions (F10); the rest is a request (the loop is L1's)
- FILES: web/app.py (`_build_world_id_remap`, `_deep_remap_ids`, `_remap_cp_blob` — via F10)
- MEASURED (one 4.0 MB checkpoint: 2k entities, a 3k-body charter): `_remap_cp_blob` with the old every-entity remap 1.003 s, with the F10 global-key map 0.002 s; `json.loads` 0.220 s + `json.dumps` 0.221 s remain per checkpoint inside the lock. So the locked cost per checkpoint fell ~1.44 s -> ~0.44 s (a 41 MB charter row scales ~10x both ways).
- REQUEST (L1, web/app.py `turn_branch`): (a) `INSERT INTO variants(step_id,content,created,active,reasoning) SELECT ?,content,created,active,reasoning FROM variants WHERE step_id=? ORDER BY id`, and the same for `steps`/`events`, instead of pulling every variant's content through Python; (b) the remaining loads/remap/dumps of OLDER checkpoints (turn_idx < idx+1) can move out of the main transaction only by splitting it: commit the branch with its branch-point checkpoint, then parse/remap/dump each older one outside the lock and insert it in its own short transaction. That trades the "a failed branch leaves nothing visible" guarantee for older checkpoints only (a branch missing them is playable, just not rewindable past its start) — an owner call, so I did not assume it. Note the shared `world_id_remap` object must still be passed to every `_remap_cp_blob` call (it now mints into it, F9).
- P3 (every restore probes the embedding provider via `start_rebuild_if_needed` -> `embedding_bank_status`): the probe lives in `mind/memory_vectors.py` (L9); `restore_checkpoint`'s call site is mine and unchanged. REQUEST (L9): decide staleness from the stored model keys (or cache the probe per process with a named TTL) so the reroll path makes no provider call.

###### dressing_language_runtime#F5 — backdrop/ambience caches outlive their chat, and a reused chat id inherits them
- STATUS: request (L10) — no clear/forget function exists in `dressing/` to call (checked `dressing/backdrops.py`, `dressing/ambience.py`: only `cancel_backdrops`/`cancel_ambience`, which stop in-flight jobs, and `clear_ambience_pin`)
- REQUEST (L10, dressing/backdrops.py + dressing/ambience.py): add `forget_chat_media(chat_id)` that removes `BACKDROP_DIR/<chat_id>/` and `AMBIENCE_DIR/<chat_id>/` when no live chat lists `chat_id` in its `branched_from` lineage (`backdrops.branch_lineage` reads ancestors' directories in place, so a descendant must keep them), and otherwise writes an owner marker so a NEW chat that reuses the id (`chats.id` has no AUTOINCREMENT: deleting the newest chat hands its id to the next one) does not read them — readers (`room_anchor`, `set_room_anchor`, `cached_backdrop`, `reusable_manifest`) ignore a directory whose marker names a different chat (e.g. the `chats.created` stamp written at the directory's first write). Then I add one call after the commit in `persist/chat_delete.delete_chat_data`, beside `vacuum_blobs()` (or L10/the orchestrator adds it: `from dressing.backdrops import forget_chat_media; forget_chat_media(chat_id)`).

###### persistence_paths_schema#F18 — a database older than v7 cannot be opened
- STATUS: skipped, as the lane brief says. The fix (move every migration-dependent index from SCHEMA to LATE_SCHEMA, or refuse below a named oldest version) changes what versions the engine promises to open — an owner decision; no such database is known to exist (the owner's is v45). Logged only.

###### Coordinator request from L12b — make `planned_entities` per-era
- STATUS: follow-up (not done in this lane; exact change below). Reason: a safe migration depends on persistence F11 (checkpoint blobs carry no schema version), which is not this lane's.
- FACTS CHECKED: `world/planned_entities.py` reads/writes through `wget_for_frame`/`wset_for_frame(cid, "planned_entities", frame_id)`, but the key is in neither `FRAME_SCOPED_WORLD_KEYS` nor `ERA_WORLD_KEYS`, so `_scoped_world_key` returns the bare key for every frame: one ledger for all eras (L12b's finding stands). Carriage needs no code: snapshot, archive and branch carry every `world` row and remap a scoped key's frame suffix generically, and a plan holds no integer row id (uid is a kind+name hash, `filed_turn` an index). `world` is keyed `(chat_id, key)`, so a copy by `INSERT OR IGNORE` is idempotent.
- EXACT CHANGE: (1) core/db.py: add `"planned_entities"` to `FRAME_SCOPED_WORLD_KEYS` (beside `planning_needs`, same comment shape) and to `ERA_WORLD_KEYS` (the author's group), so a causality bubble / couple frame shares its era's ledger and a past/future/other frame has its own. (2) `SCHEMA_VERSION` 45 -> 46, `MIGRATIONS` gets `[]` for v45->v46, and in `init()`'s pass block (inside the stamping transaction, before `executescript(LATE_SCHEMA)` — the F5 position, so it is stamped only when it succeeds): `if not is_fresh_db and current < 46: _scope_planned_entities_per_era(c)` = for every chat holding a bare `planned_entities` row, for every frame of that chat whose `kind` is not in `_SAME_ERA_KINDS`, `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO world(chat_id,key,value) VALUES(?, 'planned_entities' || char(30) || 'fr' || ?, <bare value>)`. The bare row stays the present's. (3) THE HAZARD that blocks doing only (1)+(2): every checkpoint in an existing story predates v46 and holds only the bare row; `restore_checkpoint` deletes the world and re-inserts the blob's, so the first reroll after the upgrade deletes every era's copy and past eras read an empty ledger. Re-copying at restore when a blob holds no scoped row is wrong for a post-v46 era that simply has no plans (it would hand that era the present's — L12b's bug back). The restore (and an import of an old archive) must know the blob's version: F11's fix (stamp `SCHEMA_VERSION` into `snapshot_blob`; run version-gated upgrades on an older blob before restoring it) is the prerequisite, after which (2)'s pass runs on a `< 46` blob too. (4) Test: a story with the present and a past era and one plan; a file stamped 45 -> `init()` -> both eras read the plan; a past-era save leaves the present's ledger alone; a bubble of the present reads the present's; restoring a pre-v46 (unstamped) checkpoint keeps both eras' plans.
- NOTED: L12b's two new scene keys `planned_geometry_laid` / `planned_sounds_laid` ride the frame-scoped `scene` blob — carried by checkpoint, archive and branch with no code (no ids inside to remap, per L12b's note).

F5 F6 F7 F9 F10 F13 F14 F15 F21 F22; offscreen F2), 3 coordinator requests done
(L2's projection sync moved home, L12b's plan marks wired, L9's mood clock), 3
skipped/logged (scene F5, the merged background write, background E2), 7
requests to other owners.** 23 new test files, 78 tests, all green together;
the facade import rule reports nothing for any of them.

Must know before committing: `tests/test_character_continuity.py` has 2 pins
that now fail BY DESIGN (L9's mood clock; fixture request below);
`tests/test_crowds.py`'s five-trigger source pin passes but its docstring is

production path (F10); F7 applies the channel test to exact-name addresses

###### persist_scene_entities#F1 — a refused charter order half-landed inside the commit
- STATUS: fixed (at the commit's call site); request for the class (below).
- FILES: `persist/commit_scene_state.py` (`_apply_charter_orders`).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_refused_charter_order_lands_nothing_inside_a_commit.py`
  (3 tests). Before:
  `test_a_refused_event_leaves_the_registry_as_it_was` —
  `AssertionError: op 0 of a refused event landed: the event half-applied /
  assert {'gate_warden': 'vesk'} == {'cook': 'yusra', 'gate_warden': 'vesk'}`.
  Also ran `tests/test_charter_ops.py` (54), `test_a_charter_op_names_a_body_
  the_way_the_beat_does.py`, `test_an_errand_carries_the_word.py`: green.
- WHAT CHANGED: the registry `author_charter_ops` mutates (inside the commit's
  `registry_session` the session's own copy) is deep-copied before the event
  and restored IN PLACE when the event is refused, so the flush writes the
  pre-event registry; an accepted event lands in that same object, so every
  later writer of the commit sees it. One deepcopy per beat that carries an
  order (32 ms on the largest stored registry, chat 175, 900 KB, measured
  read-only); none otherwise. `author_charter_ops` stays the one seam.
- REQUEST (world/charter_runtime.py, the class): `registry_for_update`'s
  docstring promises mutations "are discarded with the object when the caller
  abandons them, which several writers (carrier ingest, caravan trade, public
  evidence) rely on" — false for every one of them inside a commit. Either
  make `registry_for_update` return a deepcopy inside a session and have
  `save_registry` swap a non-session object into the entry (it already does
  for "a freshly generated one"), or give the session a
  `with registry_trial(cid, frame_id) as reg:` that snapshots/restores the
  way `_apply_charter_orders` now does, and use it in `author_charter_ops`
  and the three named writers. Then `_apply_charter_orders`' snapshot can go.
- REGISTER: UNBUILT_LIVING_WORLD.md:2194-2203 describes the landing as
  all-or-nothing; it now is, at the commit.

###### persist_scene_entities#F10 (+ a wider defect found fixing it) — `known_to` inheritance never ran on the commit path, and read one shape of two
- STATUS: fixed. WIDER THAN THE FINDING: the write-side inheritance was dead

  `test_a_superseding_row_inherits_on_the_commits_own_path` —

###### persist_scene_entities#F4 — the commit's own scene-only mints and folds bypassed the entity projection
- STATUS: fixed (class: the projection is taken against the merged scene).
- FILES: `persist/commit_entities.py` (`commit_world_entities`).
- TEST: `tests/test_the_entity_projection_follows_the_commits_own_mints.py`
  (5). Before: stood-up plan `AssertionError: a planned thing stood up by the
  commit has no row`; healed cast record `assert None is not None`; reclaimed
  garment `AssertionError: a garment reclaimed on re-wear kept its row
  forever`; merge's twin fold `AssertionError: the folded-away id got a
  phantom row`. Then all 29 test files naming `world_entities`/
  `commit_world_entities` (493 passed).
- WHAT CHANGED: with `prepared["scene"]` (the merged blob) in hand, a diff id
  the merge folded away is skipped instead of UPSERTed from the raw diff; every
  merged entity that is new against `prev_scene`, changed against it, or has
  no row is projected (one `SELECT entity_id` per beat); every entity the
  frame's scene held before the beat and does not hold after it loses its row
  (beside `remove_entities`, which now also catches a removal named by display
  name). Direct callers without a merged scene project the diff exactly as
  before. Measured on the six largest stored scenes: an empty merge changes 0
  or 1 entity dict, so "changed" adds no per-beat churn.
- NOTE: the missing-row heal also repairs, on a frame's next commit, rows its
  scene lost earlier (the editor gap F3 measured at 85 of 1,208), and makes a
  chat-wide row deleted by one frame's fold come back on the next commit of a
  frame that still holds the entity.
- REGISTER: DATABASE.md:340 ("derived from the prepared diff") — now derived
  from the merged scene against the prior scene; `commit_world_entities`'
  docstring still says the diff names which entities a beat touched, which
  stays true for the S3-A8 copy-forward check only.

  step metadata and extension channels, so nothing can fall behind (the
  orchestrator's "from the owners table" is met by construction; importing
  the table would have been a narrower list). Dict keys are never renamed (a
  key in a channel is a subject); prose is untouched (exact strings only); an
  entry the rename makes a duplicate of another entry of its list is dropped.

###### persist_background_memory#F3 — the charter overlay welded a different body into a tracked record of the same name (LEAK + CORRUPTION)
- STATUS: fixed (withhold, not the audit's "separate record" — reason below).
- FILES: `persist/commit_background.py` (`_proven_another_body` new;
  `with_charter_presences`).

  (4; the derivation is patched on `world.charter_runtime`, its defining
  module). Before: `assert ['p_hall'] == []` (the hall Mara, bound to m1,
  carried `{'charter': 'millers', 'body': 'b17'}`), and a record holding
  inn/k2 gained millers/b17 beside it. Then 30 files naming the overlay or
  `charter_refs` (779 passed).
- WHAT CHANGED: a name hit in the overlay is refused when the hit is PROVEN
  to be another body — it carries another body's charter ref, or it is bound
  to a scene entity that does not carry THIS body's `charter_ref` — and the
  derived record is withheld for that beat. An unbound record is still
  connected once (as `_resolve_or_mint_presence` does), and a record bound to
  the entity that IS this body's rendering still merges.
- WHY WITHHOLD: a second record keyed on the charter seed makes every later
  name lookup of "Mara" ambiguous, and `_presence_lookup`'s refusal then
  drops the tracked person's own lines unattributed; withholding is the rule
  `background_presence_records` already keeps for two charter bodies sharing
  a name ("withheld until the fiction distinguishes them"). The cost: while
  the names collide the charter's Mara has no voice through the overlay.
- NOT DONE (log): `_mint_missing_presence_names` reserves only registered
  names while drawing from lanes that include the charters', so the engine
  can still mint a presence name equal to a charter body's — the collision's
  source. Reserving charter display names there means a registry walk per
  mint; left for whoever owns naming (UNBUILT_IDENTITY §1.89 family).
- REGISTER: none (UNBUILT_IDENTITY §1.17 is the crowd residual, not this).

###### persist_background_memory#F5 — `promote_background_character` was not atomic
- STATUS: fixed (inside the function, so both callers are covered).
- FILES: `persist/commit_background.py` (`promote_background_character`;
  `transaction` added to its `core.db` import).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_failed_promotion_leaves_nothing_behind.py` (2; the
  bundle and the binding are patched on `world.charter_runtime`). Before:
  `test_a_binding_that_cannot_be_saved_writes_nothing` — `assert
  [<sqlite3.Row ...>] == []` (the `characters` row survived the refused
  binding). The second test pins the successful landing (status active, seed
  memory keyed `promotion:<cid>:<char>:0`, position, ledger cleaned). Then the
  13 test files naming promotion/greeting claims (193 passed).
- WHAT CHANGED: every write that makes the person — `characters`,
  `chat_chars`, both relationship-graph blocks, the scene (position, vitals,
  attire), mutual recognition, the charter binding (whose refusal raises
  inside it) and the ledger cleanup — runs in ONE `with transaction():`. The
  seed/handoff memories (`add_memories_batch`) and the greeting-mind claim run
  AFTER the commit: both embed, which must not hold the write lock, and a seed
  for a rolled-back character would be a memory of nobody. Deviation from the
  audit's "one transaction including memories": I first prepared the batch
  before the write and bound it to the minted id inside, but that reaches
  into `prepare_memories_batch`'s opaque result and broke two existing tests'
  interception of `add_memories_batch(rows)`; the after-commit order needs
  neither, and a failed memory write leaves a whole character without seeds,
  not a half-made one.
- REGISTER: none.

###### persist_background_memory#F7 — `addressed_turns` accrued on a loose word-match with neither the subject rule nor a channel
- STATUS: fixed.

  addressed loop).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_name_talked_about_is_not_addressed.py` (3; the third is
  a pin that a first name said to its holder in the room still counts).

  addressee — `AssertionError: the subject of the question was counted as
  turned toward / assert True is False`; "Nookfeller? Anyone seen
  Nookfeller?" with him at the mill — `assert True is False`. Then the 85
  background files as above (`test_who_you_talk_to.py` green).
- WHAT CHANGED: the Director's flow addressee still counts as before. A
  raw-text address in the player's overt words counts only on the gate's
  terms: the loose significant-word match is dropped when the beat has a
  precise addressee (a flow ref, an exact name, a routed hand-off, or a
  character's line aimed precisely — detected without the hearing test, so
  the subject rule errs toward NOT accruing), and an exact or loose match
  counts only if `address_reaches` says the words arrived, as the gate asks.
  Applying the channel test to the exact-name arm too is a widening of the
  finding's letter (it was "loose"); it is the same class — the gate already
  drops an exact name that does not reach, and the writer now agrees.

###### coordinator request (L9's finding) — the mood clock never advanced in a clocked story
- STATUS: fixed.
- FILES: `persist/commit_memory.py` (`_mood_over_the_beat` new;
  `_beat_span_units` computed beside `_clock_seconds`; the mood keys written
  through it).
- TEST: `tests/test_the_mood_clock_runs_with_the_story_clock.py` (3; the
  third pins that a zero-duration beat moves nothing). Before: `assert 3.0 ==
  13.0` (a ten-minute beat left `mood_clock` where the pass left it) and the
  habit read `{'m1': {'at': 3.0, 'h': 0.6}}` both ways (`assert 0.6 < 0.6`).
- WHAT CHANGED: at commit the carried mood (the pass's, or the stored one on
  a beat the pass did not reach) moves on by the beat's own span — the stored
  clock the beat opened on to the clock it ends on, in psych units, with NO
  turn fallback (an unreadable clock was already counted by the call's turn
  cadence): `mood_clock += span`, `mood_coords` relax toward home by
  `affect_mix.decay` (the pass's own rule, home from `affect_pass.home_mood`),
  `mood_habits` drop what has rested away. Measured from L9 rather than
  `elapsed_units` (prev `affect_seconds` → end) so a gap the call already
  decayed over is not counted twice.
- PINS THAT NOW FAIL, BY DESIGN (request below): `tests/test_character_
  continuity.py::test_the_mood_is_given_in_the_packet_and_the_engine_writes_
  it_back` (`assert state[...]["mood_coords"] == result["_affect_pass"]
  ["mood_coords"]`) and `::test_the_memories_a_beat_mints_keep_what_it_made_
  the_mind_feel` (`== {"tension": 0.3}`): their fixture's resolve claims no
  time, so the unclaimed-beat floor charges a span and the written mood is
  the pass's relaxed over it. The pins assert exactly the copy-unchanged
  behaviour L9 measured as the defect.
- REQUEST (tests/test_character_continuity.py, not mine): in the `story`
  fixture's context builder (~line 102) give the beat no time —
  `ctx.director_resolve = {"dialogue_log": [], "state_diff": {"time":
  {"duration_seconds": 0}}}` — which keeps both tests about WHICH mood is
  written (the engine's, not the model's) and passes against the fix (a
  zero-span beat moves nothing; pinned in my third test).
- REGISTER: if UNBUILT/Design says the mood "decays between beats on the story
  clock", it now does; before this it decayed only on the first beat.

###### persist_background_memory#F9 — salience cues matched inside other words
- STATUS: fixed (start-of-word rule, by the cue's script).

  `assert 0.646 == 0.486` ("familiar"/"compromised"), `assert 0.56 == 0.48`
  ("glove"/"clover"), `assert 0.726 < 0.72` (the audit's unarchivable row).
  `tests/test_language_packs.py` green.
- WHAT CHANGED: a Latin-script cue must begin a word (no spaced-script letter
  before it — `language_runtime.SPACED_WORD_CHARS`); a cue whose first
  character is an unspaced script stays a substring (the ja pack's kanji
  stems sit inside compounds). Only the start is bounded, as
  `_durable_dialogue_category`'s markers are, so "attacked"/"confessed" count.
- RESIDUAL: a cue that OPENS a different word ("dead" in "deadline",
  "secret" in "secretary") still counts; telling those from inflections needs
  a narrower cue in the pack (`_SALIENCE_CUES` written as anchored patterns,
  as `_DURABLE_QUOTE_MARKERS` are) — pack content, not mine. The measured row
  drops from 0.726 to 0.646, under the archive bar.
- REGISTER: none.

###### persist_scene_entities#E1 (part) — the world-pressure domain parsed the scene on every beat
- STATUS: fixed (the lazy parse). The "background domain writes the scene
  twice" half: SKIPPED, see below.
- FILES: `persist/commit_ledgers.py` (`commit_world_pressure`).
- TEST: `tests/test_the_commit_reads_and_copies_the_scene_only_when_it_must.py
  ::test_a_beat_with_no_tick_parses_no_scene` (`wget` counted on
  `persist.commit_ledgers`). Before: `assert 'scene' not in
  ['world_pressures', 'scene']`.
- WHAT CHANGED: the scene is read at the first `tick` op, not unconditionally.
- SKIPPED (the merged background write): the two whole-scene `wset`s in
  `track_background_presences` happen only on a beat with BOTH a departure and
  a handover (≤1 ms at the largest stored scene, 56 KB), and merging them
  changes what a handover that raises part-way leaves behind (today: the
  departures land, the half-applied handover does not; merged: either both or
  a snapshot copy, which costs more than the write it saves). Not worth the
  semantic change.

- STATUS: skipped (partly addressed by F6).

  parsed blob inside `track_background_presences` changes aliasing (the
  departure/handover appliers mutate `live_scene` in place; `_scene_now` is
  read after them for `_thing_name_words`). The expensive repeat is
  `descriptor_bindings` (scene + ledger + charter overlay + plans, 4-5 times a
  beat): F6 now has the commit prefer the gate's bindings, and the REQUEST
  under F6 (carry `demand["bindings"]` on the step) removes three of those
  derivations.

###### persist_background_memory#E4 — belief reconciliation scans the whole bank inside the write lock
- STATUS: request (the computation lives in a file I do not own).
- REQUEST (mind/memory_inference.py): split `reconcile_inference_confidence`
  into `plan_inference_confidence(chat_id, char_id, state, turn_idx,
  elapsed_seconds=None, pending=()) -> [(confidence, memory_id_or_pending_
  index)]` (the SELECT and the `belief_credence` loop, run in
  `prepare_memory_commit` over the settled `st` and the beat's pending rows,
  whose confidence it can set before they are embedded) and
  `apply_inference_confidence(updates)` (the UPDATEs only). Then
  `commit_memory.prepare_memory_commit` appends the planned updates to
  `prepared["belief_reconciles"]` and `commit_memories` applies them inside
  the transaction. Not done here: re-implementing `belief_credence`/
  `_abandoned_confidence` in persist would be a second copy of the rule.

- STATUS: logged, not done (the brief: a pipeline-ordering change).

  false for duplicate mints until this lands.

##### Register sentences that now describe changed behaviour (for the orchestrator)

- docs/guides/DATABASE.md:344 (`world_entities`): "**Which** entities a beat
  touched comes from the post-dedup diff; **what** they now are comes from the
  merged scene" and "A row heals the next time a beat touches that entity" —
  after F4 the commit also projects every merged entity that is new or changed
  against the prior scene or has no row, deletes the rows of entities the
  frame's scene held before the beat and not after, and skips an id the merge
  folded away; a missing row now heals on the frame's next commit, touched or
  not. `sync_world_entities_with_scene` (moved here from L2) is the same rule
  for a host's write.
- AGENTS.md:435: "`pick_voice_demand` qualifies on exactly four demands —
  addressed, owed, acting, emerged" — it qualifies on addressed (flow, routed,
  exact, character, mentioned), place_addressed, owed, owes_act (F13), acting,
  emerged and arrived. Same row: "(the same set gates `mention_turns`/
  `addressed_turns` accrual in the writer and the manager gate)" — the writer
  now also applies the gate's subject rule and `address_reaches` to a
  raw-text address before `addressed_turns` accrues (F7).
- Design.md:280: "`still_owes` writes the figure's aged `pending_act`
  (re-asked until `hands_over` lands ...)" — true on the default path now
  (F13); it was true only under `scene_life: ambient|full`.
- UNBUILT_LIVING_WORLD.md:2194-2203 (charter ops land all-or-nothing) — now
  true inside a commit (F1).
- PIPELINE.md:1144-1156 ("cannot disagree by construction") — still false for
  duplicate mints (F5, logged, not done).

  `:91: AssertionError: assert {'checkpoints...': ['commit']} == {'checkpoints..., 'stale': []}` (the audit's
  reproduction: checkpoints [1,2] -> [1], marker gone, commit stale), and the same for the other three.
- WHAT CHANGED: every `from_key` refusal (key not in the plan, stale interpretation, a stale step the run
  would read back, a key the opening does not hold) is decided against the plan the stored interpretation
  builds -- the plan Resume reports from -- before anything is restored, swept, claimed or marked. The old
  post-restore checks stay as backstops. One deliberate exception, named `_GATED_PLAN_KEYS`: a
  `character:<id>`/`interaction_loop`/`reaction_loop` key the turn HOLDS A ROW FOR but the live world's plan
  lacks is still decided after the restore, because its place in the plan is decided by awareness/presence
  gates the restore rewinds (a reactor the beat knocked out was awake before it) -- refusing it pre-restore
  would refuse a legitimate "Run from here".
- PIN CHANGED: `tests/test_pipeline_audit.py::TestFromKeyMissingInterpret::test_unknown_from_key_raises_instead_of_full_recompute`
  asserted the corrupting order (stale == {"commit"} after a refusal); now asserts nothing is marked (set()).
- REGISTER: `docs/guides/PIPELINE.md` "Resume and rerun" (F16 already flags it omits the restore + sweep) --
  add that a refused rerun is refused before the restore.

###### agents_runtime_loops_mapping#F2 — The skip stage swallows the user's Stop, and the step is saved fresh
- STATUS: fixed (both floors the audit names)
- FILES: `agents/runtime.py` (`_saved_only_if_not_stopped`, `_DURABLE_ON_RETURN`, called in `_step_stream` and
  `_run_parallel_group` after the handler, before the save), `agents/skip_stage.py` (`Aborted` re-raised at
  `ask` x2, the futures loop, `resolve_mind`'s and `judge_what_was_missing`'s decision requests, and the three
  per-mind wrappers in `skip_lives`; `Aborted` imported at module level).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_stop_during_the_skip_is_never_saved_as_a_lived_day.py` (6). Before:
  `:66: Failed: DID NOT RAISE <class 'llm.providers.Aborted'>` (serial), `:83` (group), `:148` (`ask`),
  `:160` (decision request).
- WHAT CHANGED: no stage that FINISHES under a set Stop is saved -- nothing can tell a stage that finished
  just before the Stop from one that swallowed it -- except the commit, whose writes are durable when it
  returns (dropping its row would leave the world holding a commit the turn says it lacks). The skip stage
  re-raises `Aborted` like director/background/skip_work. Behaviour note: a stage that completes in the
  instant a Stop lands is now discarded and re-run on Resume (it was saved before).

###### world_layout_bridge_structure#F8 (tier 2) — the "tail of one" word rule crosses word boundaries
- STATUS: skipped: OWNER decision (the audit says "name it to the owner rather than widen")
- `planned_context`'s tier 2 (`room_words_answer`, in `world/spatial_identity.py`, not this lane's) lets a
  wanted word match the TAIL of a held word: "the hall" -> "Marshall House", "the ford" -> "Stafford Mill",
  "the house" -> "Lighthouse" -- and also the measured live case it exists for, "Cope's yard" -> "Cope's
  Boatyard" (scratch play 2026-09-14, chat 5 turn 27). A candidate rule WITH PRECEDENT in the engine
  (`director_prose.rooms_named_by_part`, measured on chat 166's 107 encoder calls): one word alone must be a
  WHOLE word of the name; a tail counts only inside a phrase of two or more words. It keeps "Cope's yard",
  refuses the three wrong ones, and loses "the yard" -> Cope's Boatyard. It must change `scene_room_id`'s
  identical word tier at the same time, or the plan and the scene answer "the yard" differently.

###### world_layout_bridge_structure#F12 — `max_planned` read per call vs per structure
- STATUS: assessed; REQUEST (L12b, `world/structure.py`)
- Assessment: CONFIRMED by reading. `plant_structure` refuses only a CALL planting more than `max_planned`
  rooms (`len(normalized) > structure["max_planned"]`, ~l.1432), so `expand_lot` (<= `LOT_ROOMS_MAX` 14 a
  call) is never stopped; `prepare_frontier_expansion` counts EVERY live planned spec of the structure
  (`counts[skey]`, ~l.2855-2862) -- a lot's interior rooms (`lot_of`) included -- and from `max_planned`
  (default 200) on retains every axis with no warning. ~14 expanded lots freeze a village's frontier.
- REQUEST: in `prepare_frontier_expansion`'s count loop, skip a spec whose `LOT_OF_KEY` names a lot other
  than itself (`str(spec.get("lot_of") or "") not in ("", uid)`): a building's interior is not frontier, and
  the lot's own entry was counted as the lot. And note (decision or warning) when an axis is retained at the
  cap, so the freeze is visible. The 200 stays the owner's.

`ArrangeCancelled` filed as a refusal), `:143` and `:158: KeyError: 'warnings'` (nothing filed).

###### L12b request 1 — file the solve's warnings on the run record
- STATUS: fixed. `entry["warnings"]` = the report's `warnings` on a laid building, the layout's `warnings` on a
  refused one, only when non-empty (a clean solve's record is unchanged). Bounded by the new named
  `LAYOUT_RUN_NOTES_MAX = 12` -- the 12 the refusals were always cut to (now `reasons[:LAYOUT_RUN_NOTES_MAX]`,
  same behaviour); the log still carries every sentence.

###### L12b request 2 — set `layout_bridge.CANCELLED` around the solves
- STATUS: fixed. `lay_out_buildings` sets `CANCELLED` to `lambda: jobs.is_cancelled(job)` (None with no job --
  the opening's own lay-out) for the whole walk and resets it in `finally`. An `ArrangeCancelled` out of
  `lay_out` is no failure: nothing is filed (it was caught as an ordinary exception and filed as a refusal,
  which would have made the building wait out a retry back-off), and no further building is begun.

###### L12b request 3 — `plan_buildings(only=set(room_ids))`
- STATUS: fixed. The existing `wanted & set(b["rooms"])` filter stays (harmless, and the answer is the same).

- REGISTER: none found naming these.
- NOT DONE (structure note, not requested): `lay_out_buildings`' "Never raises" is still unenforced after
  the solve (`planned_layout`, `write_planned_layout`, `apply_planned_layout` are unguarded).

  owner's engine.db most rooms ARE undeveloped planned stubs (chat 179: 18 of 20; 172: 19 of 22; 174: 19 of
  21), the plan's own rooms built ahead, so dropping them would have left a priest in his own shrine house two
  room names. What a resident knows is taken as: the rooms of its own region (`world.regions.room_region`, the
  structure a planned room belongs to; inherited by minted rooms), plus every room one doorway off or in view
  from where it stands; a scene with no regions keeps every room (the existing

  shrine_under (2), so a groundskeeper now knows the grounds plus doorways, not the house's rooms by name.

###### agents_character_background#E1 — three serial decision-model passes before every character call
- STATUS: skipped (logged) — a MERGE is not behaviour-identical, so per the brief it is not made.
- ANALYSIS: the three requests ask against three different states: `affect_pass.before_call` asks its
  appraisal questions against `affect_pass.state_text` (psychology, mood words, people, events, memories,
  concerns); `character_jev.ask_before` asks disputes against `jev.state_text(h)` and the note/belief/cue
  checks against `jev.moment_text(h)` (two `decide` calls, `mind/character_jev.py:599-612`). Merged into one
  request every question would read one state — a different prompt for each answer — so the merge changes
  behaviour. What IS behaviour-identical is CONCURRENCY: `ask_before` reads nothing `before_call` produces
  (`holding_from` reads the payload's intentions/projects/learned rows/contacts/promises and the memory TEXTS;
  `name_memories` only adds `how_it_feels`, which `_delivered_memories` does not read). Running `before_call`
  in a worker (copy_context, as `_voices_at_once` does) while the payload, notebook view and `ask_before` run
  would save ~0.4 s per call (`docs/experiments/BARE_CARD_REPLAY_2026_09_27.md:113`: 0.4 s median a pass), but
  needs `self.feelings` and `name_memories` filled after the join and before `compact_character_evidence` —
  a restructure of the 1600-line `character_step` worth its own reviewed change, not a fix-lane edit. The two
  `ask_before` requests are L9's file (`mind/character_jev.py`) and are independent of each other too.

###### agents_director_core#F1 — the silent-beat guard left the invention in four other representations
- STATUS: fixed.
- FILES: `agents/director.py` (`_declared_elsewhere`, `_silence_every_copy`, one
  call inside the empty-input guard; the guard's own pinned lines kept verbatim).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_silent_beat_keeps_no_copy_of_what_it_invented.py` (3).
  Before: `AssertionError: the invented rows stood in \`ledgers\`` /
  `assert not ['Kaa Sama, your cooking is too good.']` (the invented line
  COMMITTED to the resolve's `state_diff.speech`) /
  `['persona:primary', 'persona:primary', 'persona:99'] == ['persona:99']`.
  Also green: `test_silent_beat_declares_nothing.py`, both
  `test_a_silent_beat_*` in `test_a_room_in_view_is_built_before_anyone_walks_in.py`.
- WHAT CHANGED: the guard now also empties `ledgers`/`causal_ledger`/
  `_all_causal_sequence`, `movement`, `flow.dice`, and on the prose record
  still on the context: `prose` -> "" (so `already_happened` returns ""),
  `events`, the encoder transforms and extension values (so `declared_moves`,
  the fold and `attach_record` see nothing). An OTHER player's declared rows
  (non-idle, non-empty input) are kept with their transforms. What was
  discarded stays on the record under `silent_beat_discarded`.
- FINDING FOR THE ORCHESTRATOR (not fixed, outside F1): `out["_all_causal_sequence"]`
  is set at director.py ~2148 and then DROPPED by `validate_llm_output`'s
  schema dump (verified: `'_all_causal_sequence' in out -> False`), before
  `_interpret_beat_view` reads it. So the audit's point 6 (the fold writing the
  primary's invented transforms back via it) is not reachable -- but the same
  drop means ANOTHER PLAYER'S rows never become interpret spans at all, so an
  extra player's asserted transforms are never folded at interpret (their
  rows are in `ledgers`/`other_players`, their state is not in the onset).
  Multiplayer WRONG-SCENE, PLAUSIBLE, unregistered as far as I can see.
- REGISTER: AGENTS.md:289 ("a silent beat, which keeps nothing its own prose
  made") now HOLDS for the rows, the record and `already_happened`.
  UNBUILT_PIPELINE §1.216 residual ("a silent beat stays whole with the

###### agents_director_core#F11 — reachability and restraint floors ran only at resolve; the interpret's positions previewed unchecked (assess)
- STATUS: fixed for the RESTRAINT half; the REACHABILITY half assessed and
  skipped (reason below).
- FILES: `agents/director.py` (`_block_restrained_moves` over
  `out["state_assertions"]` right after `validated_player_state_assertions`,
  before the onset copy; holder pool = player + cast, as at resolve).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_bound_body_does_not_walk_off_in_the_onset_preview.py`
  (2). Before: `assert 'The Stranger' not in (({'The Stranger': 'lamp_room'}))`.
  The second test pins that an unbound player's walk is still previewed.
  `program_steps` drops a top-level key removed by a floor out of every
  causal step, so the preview (which executes the steps) loses the move too.
- ASSESSMENT, reachability: at resolve the declared mover is SPARED by the
  physical floor (`_spared`) because the movement backstop has already ruled
  on that crossing and "may legitimately have honoured a contested crossing
  this floor's route test would re-refuse"; under the prose contract a shut
  door on the way is contested-and-asserted, a wall refused. Running
  `_unreachable_position_writes` at interpret would pre-empt the backstop's
  ruling for the declared mover (the commonest interpret position write) and
  needs the charter view, `declared_moves` and `_spared` rebuilt at the
  wrong stage. The window that remains -- a non-mover's position written
  through a WALL at interpret and previewed for one beat -- is narrow; a
  ruling should decide whether the onset preview may show a crossing the
  resolve has not ruled on. Left for the owner.

###### agents_director_floors#F1 — `visibility`/`commitment` copied raw; the firewall reader matched one exact word (LEAK)
- STATUS: fixed (canonical once, at ingest).

  Before: `assert 'Concealed ' == 'concealed'` (and `CONCEALED`, `hidden`,
  `secret`), `public`/`normal`/`visible`/`open` kept raw, and
  `'Contestable' == 'contestable'` failing. After, `concealed_from_observer`
  is True for the listed body.
- WHAT CHANGED: one reading for every reader: `concealed|hidden|secret` ->
  concealed (the dialogue log's own X14 fold), anything else overt;
  `contestable` (any case/space) -> contestable, anything else asserted.
  Measured (owner db, read-only, 3000 recent interpret/resolve variants):
  overt 4877, public 97, normal 87, visible 15, private 8, unobscured 7,
  open 7, concealed 4, low 4, plain_sight 4, plain 3, audible_to_Mirelle 3;
  commitment only asserted/contestable. Failing CLOSED on unknown values
  (the report's suggestion) would have hidden every `public`/`normal` row
  from every observer, so it was NOT done.
  OWNER QUESTION: `private` (9 rows) and `audible_to_<name>` (3) read overt,
  as the firewall always read them; whether `private` joins the concealed
  set is a vocabulary ruling.
- REQUEST (llm/schemas.py owner): the X14 fold (`("concealed", "hidden",
  "secret")`, inline at the dialogue_log coercion) should read
  `agents.director_evidence.CONCEALED_SPELLINGS` (or the constant move to
  schemas and this module import it) so the two folds cannot drift.
- REGISTER: none.

###### agents_director_floors#F11 — the release floor frees the possessor of a thing untied (latent)
- STATUS: skipped (design question) + REQUEST.
- WHY: the report's fix (refuse an object-side name followed by a
  possessive) would break `tests/test_restraint_exits.py::TestReleasePrecision::test_the_japanese_attempt_is_not_a_completion`,
  which pins `サラはヒナミの縄をほどいた。 -> {ヒナミ}` -- untying someone's ROPE
  releases them, and that pin is right. "unties Hinami's hair ribbon" (no)
  and "unties Hinami's ropes" (yes) have the same grammar; the difference is
  WHAT is possessed, which no possessive rule can see. The floor is gated
  off on every beat with rows (UNBUILT_PIPELINE §1.1 item 3), so nothing is
  live. The subject-side rule (`'s`/`’s` only) would extend to Japanese with
  a pack-supplied possessive -- no such key exists in either pack.
- REQUEST (language packs): add `agents.director._POSSESSIVE_AFTER_NAME`
  (en: `^['’]s\b`, ja: `^の`) so the subject-side rule can read it through
  `_ling`; and an owner ruling on the object side (e.g. "a possessed thing
  frees its possessor only when it is a restraint the floor's own
  `_RESTRAINT_KEYWORDS` names").

###### agents_director_prose_movement_rooms#F1 — the walk floor refused bodies the WORLD moves, and dropped the whole event
STATUS: fixed (class), with one residual named below.
FILES: `agents/director_prose.py` (`_walks_refused`, new `_borne_away`/`_bearers`/`_put_in`/`_gone`/`_same`, `_doer_alone` + new `_writes_only_of`, docstring of `a_walk_is_its_owners`).
TEST: `tests/test_a_body_borne_by_what_moves_is_moved_by_the_world.py` (9 tests; 6 failed before):
```
:78  assert [{'event': 'R...e road', ...}] == [...]      (passenger stripped out of the player's drive)
:86  assert ([] == [{'event': 'R...e road', ...}]        (cart driven by `movement`: kept: [])
:138 AssertionError: the boards' breaking is no mind's walk   (event dropped whole)
:151/:159/:169 assert ([] == [...])   (footing removed / footing falls with him / room removed)
```
WHAT CHANGED: (1) A BODY BORNE BY WHAT MOVES IS MOVED BY THE WORLD: a refused mind is let go when something that bears it as the beat begins -- every holder up its `contained` chain, a body/thing a contact joins it to (standing or written this beat, not ended), its pose `support`, its station `at`, its room and that room's `parent_entity` -- is moved by the same event to the room the event puts the mind in, or taken out of the world by it (`remove_entities`, `remove_rooms`, `destruction.target_id`). A registered mind the floor itself keeps bears nobody (fixpoint). Only a move to a ROOM is borne; a boarding by another's declaration is still refused (Hollowmere 4661 unchanged). (2) NEVER DROP AN EVENT WHOLE UNLESS IT IS ALL THAT MIND'S: `_doer_alone` now also requires every write to name that one body (`_writes_only_of`: placing tables, attire/vitals/scales/overlays/entities keyed by it, conditions by `subject_id`, inventory ops to/from it, sounds it is the source of, its follows and contacts) -- a thing placed beside it (the cart), another subject's record (the boards), or any world channel makes the event the declarer's, kept with only the mind's walk taken out. The lighthouse shape (walk + pose/sitting of the one body) still drops whole (`test_his_own_walk_written_under_her_is_still_dropped_whole`, and every existing lighthouse test passes).
RESIDUAL (owner question, not built): the audit's exact fall -- boards written `entities{loft_boards: {state: broken}}` -- still refuses his drop (offered "in The Barn"), because a bearer's new STATE is a word, not a place; only a bearer moved or removed carries its body. Making a state change carry would read prose. The positions card says a body "dropped through a floor ... ends where the prose leaves it": the owner may want the encoder told to write a giving-way floor as `remove_entities` (or the boards' own position), which this floor now honours.
MEASUREMENT: not replayed over the stored corpus (no replay harness in this lane). By construction the change touches only beats where the floor already refuses a walk -- 10 distinct stored beats / 18 refusals per Design.md's row -- and the shapes that row quotes (positions + poses of one body) still read as that mind's own conduct.
REGISTER: Design.md row "A mind goes only where its own declaration takes it": "...unless the world moved it (a `world`/`mechanical` input), it has a declaration of its own at that stage, or a hold, carry or inside joins the two; an event recorded as the moved mind's own conduct is dropped whole." -> add "or what bears it (a holder, a contact, its support or footing, its room) is moved there or taken out of the world by the same event"; and "dropped whole" -> "dropped whole only when everything it writes is that mind's own (`_writes_only_of`); otherwise only the walk leaves it". UNBUILT_PIPELINE §1.227 residual "An event recorded as a cast member's own conduct is dropped whole with any player walk inside it" -- now narrower (an event with another subject's writes is kept).

:71  assert {'Tamsin Reed...': 'low_walk'} == {'Tamsin Reed': 'low_walk'}         (record id passed)
:84  TypeError: a_walk_is_its_owners() got an unexpected keyword argument 'spell'

A spelling two minds answer to names neither (`test_a_spelling_two_minds_answer_to_names_neither`). Stripping a refused mind's writes now matches every spelling of it, not its key/name alone.
REGISTER: none found that states the exact-spelling behaviour.

###### agents_director_prose_movement_rooms#F3 — a walk left under way inside its own destination room was retired as arrived
STATUS: fixed.
FILES: `agents/director_movement.py` (`_travel_continues`, `_travel_in_flight_view`, `walk_within_room`; new `_within_room_end`, `_anchor_name`, `_restationed`, `_walk_within_on`).
TEST: `tests/test_a_walk_under_way_ends_only_where_it_was_going.py` (6 tests; 5 failed before):
```
:63  assert 'Hester' not in ['Hester']                  (retired as arrived, no step walked)
:77  KeyError: 'stations'                                (the last stretch never walked)
:84  assert ([])                                         (a walk to a fixture in its room: nothing advanced)
:97  ValueError: not enough values to unpack (expected 1, got 0)   (the Director told nothing)
```
WHAT CHANGED: a pending leg whose body is in its destination room but which names a `to_cell`/`to_anchor` is walked on by the beat's paces (judged first like any leg: stopped by the passage, carried, restrained, or re-stationed by this beat -> interrupted "this beat moved them itself"), recorded `advanced` (underway or arrived) and warned "Travel continues: ...". A leg to a ROOM is still arrived on entering it. `_travel_in_flight_view` reports the within-room leg (`within_room: True`, `destination_name` = the fixture's description, else the room's name), so the decision model's walk-stop question is asked for it. `walk_within_room` skips a body this beat already walked (it would have re-walked the same steps from the beat's start).
REGISTER: UNBUILT_WORLD §2.15's "room-level `scene.approach` is the only staged-movement memory there is" is stale (the auditor's note); now the approach carries within-room legs AND they are walked on.

   `f"Does the passage enter, open onto or reveal the place called \"{name}\"? Answer no when it is only mentioned or lies beyond a door nobody opens."` -> e.g. `jev_questions/enter_place.txt` with `{name}`.

3. `agents/director_prose.py` `_jev_state` (~638-650): labels `"PASSAGE:"`, `"PEOPLE: "`, `"WHERE EACH PERSON IS BEFORE THE PASSAGE: "`, `"PLACES ALREADY KNOWN: "`, `"ALREADY OWED: "`, `"TIME OF DAY BEFORE THE PASSAGE: "`; also `"PASSAGE:\n"` in `bind_new_places` (~1672) and `pressures_ended` (~5661).
4. `agents/director_rooms.py` `_record_text` (~1778-1801): `f"PLACE: {name}"`, `f"Description: {desc}"`, `"Fixtures:"`, `"Fixtures: none"`, `"Ways out:"`, `f"- to {name}: {what}{side}"` with `side = f", {dir} wall"`, and `f"{key.capitalize()}: {value}"` for light/surface/exposure/quiet.
(Line numbers are as of this lane's edits.)

###### agents_director_floors#E1 — `attribute()` recomputed 7-18 times per stage on the same draft
STATUS: fixed (output-identical).
FILES: `agents/director_repair.py` (`attribute` now reads its answer from the new `_attribution`, an `functools.lru_cache` keyed by the events' texts and the `(sentence id, text)` pairs; new `ATTRIBUTION_MEMO_SIZE = 64`, a memory bound only).
TEST: `tests/test_a_draft_is_attributed_once_per_pass.py` (2 tests). Before the fix, one check-and-repair pass weighed the same (event, sentence) pair 11 times (`_holds` counted: `[(('Odalys sets the brass astrolabe down on the chart table.', 'Odalys sets ... she says.'), 11), ...]`); after, every distinct version of the draft is computed once (`cache_info().misses == distinct versions asked`, with more asks than versions), and a reader that edits its answer edits no other reader's (each gets a fresh list).
WHAT CHANGED: the algorithm is unchanged, moved into `_attribution` over plain tuples; `attribute` builds the key and returns fresh lists, so every caller (`citations`, `battery`, `jev_state`, `declared_gaps`, `bridge`, `_draft`, `call_repair`, `place`, the re-check, `_declared_left`) gets the identical answer it got before. The audit's second idea (an exact trigram bound to skip `SequenceMatcher`) was not built.

  garments; the two-pass rule can only keep MORE than the gate thinks (never removes a garment the
