###### story_attire_import#F8 (lane brief F4) — Japanese garment handles never resolved; the write gate refused undressing
- STATUS: fixed for the four mechanisms the audit lists except the clothing vocabulary — REQUEST R6
- FILES: `story/attire.py` (`_garment_keys`, new `_unspaced_head`, `_script_run_starts`,
  `_whole_phrase`, `_head_noun_counts`, `HEAD_NOUN_FLOOR = 3`; `resolve_garment` tiers 3-4;
  `garments_named_in`; `_attributed_scoped`; `_clothing_sentence`; `wardrobe_change_subjects`;
  `_SENTENCE` gains 。！？｡)
- TEST: `tests/test_a_japanese_garment_handle_finds_its_garment.py` (7). Before:
  `assert None == '白いブラウス'` (resolve), `assert [] == ['白いブラウス']` (licence),
  `assert set() == {'ヒナミ'}` (subject, one sentence and a paragraph), `assert None == '絹の帯'`,
  and the `wearing` restatement removing both garments.
- WHAT CHANGED: an unspaced name's head is its last non-hiragana script run (Japanese is
  head-final, so 白いブラウス → ブラウス, 帯締め → 帯締); containment and head-noun search use
  `\b` at a spaced-script edge exactly as before and `language_runtime.name_boundary_regex` at an
  unspaced edge (帯 found in 絹の帯, not in 帯締め/一帯); the head-noun length floor (unchanged
  value 3, now named) applies only to spaced-script heads; the licence tries an unspaced name's
  script-run suffixes and closes their open edges with the name boundary; the subject reader adds
  unspaced script runs to its word set, unspaced heads to the clothing phrases, and matches names
  with the script-aware boundary; a Japanese paragraph splits at 。. English text takes every
  branch it took before (all 1114 attire-suite tests unchanged).
- NOT FIXED: 「ヒナミは服を着替えた。」 names clothing only by 服, which the English
  `_CLOTHING_CONTEXT` cannot see; a Japanese clothing vocabulary is pack data (R6). The registered
  completion vocabularies (`_DECISIVE` etc.) were not touched.
- REGISTER: none to edit. (UNBUILT_WORLD §1.113 — the English whole-name window floored at two
  words / eight characters — is untouched: unspaced names were already exempt from that floor.)

###### story_attire_import#F10 — `regions_covered` let a modifier set the span; closed coat compounds fell to the torso
- STATUS: fixed
- FILES: `story/attire.py` (`regions_covered`, new `_head_cue`; `_REGION_CUES` and `_SPANNING_CUES`
  gain overcoat/raincoat/trenchcoat/greatcoat/topcoat and petticoat; the false "longest-first"
  comment rewritten)

  `('waist', 'torso', 'groin', 'legs') == ('waist',)` for "dress belt", `('feet', 'torso', ...)` for
  "dress shoes", `('torso', 'legs', 'groin')` for "jeans jacket", `('torso',)` for "overcoat",
  `'torso' == 'legs'` for petticoat.
- WHAT CHANGED: the span comes from the HEAD cue only — the last cue of any region table in the
  part of the name before a placement preposition (`attaches_only`'s cut, `region_of`'s
  last-and-longest rule); the anchor is still `region_of`'s. A closed compound is reached only by
  its own table entry, deliberately not by suffix ("headdress" is not a dress); six entries added

- STATUS: fixed; REQUEST R8 (optional prompt echo)

  `reinterpret_lorebook._source_for`, `_structure_key` docstring); `story/lore_structure.py`
  (`parse_structure` records carry `index`)

  (both halves of a split lost the title and locality), `StopIteration` (no "Great Calamity"
  title to find), `assert not True` (the constant entry was sent to the model).
- WHAT CHANGED: structure is keyed by SOURCE POSITION, and every rewritten entry carries the
  position it came from: a model-echoed `source` if valid; else by position when a batch came back
  one-for-one; else `_structure_key` prefix or exact keys (the old `_source_for` tolerance); else
  the batch source it shares the most distinctive words with (words every source in the batch
  shares are not counted; a tie is no answer). Locked (`constant`) entries are never sent to the
  rewrite and land locked with their structure. `reinterpret_lorebook` uses the carried position
  first.
- REGISTER: `_structure_key` docstring said index alignment cannot be used — corrected (within a
  one-for-one batch it can).

##### agents_narration_planner.md

###### agents_narration_planner#F1 — a clocked operation lands twice; the package store is rewritten whole by racing threads
STATUS: fixed.
FILES: `story/plot_packages.py`.
TEST: `tests/test_the_package_store_has_one_writer_at_a_time.py` (4 tests). Before:
```
test_an_operation_that_landed_is_not_landed_again_when_a_later_one_fails
E       assert 25.0 == 15.0          # op A (+5 grain) landed on all three beats
test_a_clock_pass_does_not_erase_a_package_another_thread_wrote
E       AssertionError: assert [] == ['schedule_event']
test_two_drafting_writers_on_two_threads_both_land
E       assert 0 == 1
test_a_publish_does_not_erase_a_package_another_thread_wrote
E       assert 0 == 1
```
WHAT CHANGED: one helper, `_update_store(cid, frame_id, change)`, reads the store INSIDE `transaction()` (process write lock + BEGIN IMMEDIATE) and saves it there; every store writer goes through it (`new_package`, `edit_package`, `draft_operation`, `remove_operation`, `validate_package`'s three writes, `prepare_package`'s per-op record and re-pin, `activate_due_packages`, `resolve_package`, `retire_package`). `fire_due_clocks` is one transaction: it re-reads the store under the lock, lands each clocked operation in a SAVEPOINT of its own (whole or nothing), records ANY exception on the op as `refused` (+ history note + `logger.warning`) instead of aborting the pass, and saves the marks with the writes; `advance_region_waves` lands each wave in its own savepoint and records a failed one in `op["refused_waves"]` (it now returns `(landed, refused)`; its only caller is `fire_due_clocks`). A pre-check outside the lock returns at once when no published/active package has a clock or a pending wave, so an ordinary beat takes no write lock. `publish_package` decides outside the lock (checks, authority, rebase preview -- seconds, so not under the lock a beat's commit waits on), then re-reads the store under the lock and lands only if neither the package (revision/status) nor the world (latest turn, registry revision) moved since the decision; otherwise it decides again, at most `PUBLISH_ATTEMPTS` (3, a named constant) times, then refuses "publish it again". The grant is re-checked under the lock. A conflict is recorded through `_update_store` only while the package is still the previewed revision. `prepare_package` runs each long op OUTSIDE the lock and writes its record onto the op as the store then stands (`_mark_prepared`, matched by index + content).
REGISTER: AGENTS.md row for plot packages says "publish is ONE short transaction" -- still true; the audit's "not for clock fires" is now false: `fire_due_clocks` is one transaction with a savepoint per operation and per wave. ANP "Structure notes" ("two persistence boundaries ... `fire_due_clocks` (no transaction, F1)") is stale.

Before the story's first beat there is no turn row and the retirement is still skipped (the same rule that function states).

FILES: `story/plot_packages.py`, `world/region_events.py`.
TEST: `tests/test_a_package_reads_its_fields_as_the_author_wrote_them.py` -- `test_the_cases_are_every_declared_list_field` (guard: the test table == every field `OPERATION_FIELDS` declares with `[`), `test_one_item_written_bare_is_one_item[*]` (12), `test_a_planned_rooms_lists_read_a_bare_item_as_one`, `test_a_region_events_lists_read_a_bare_word_as_one_item`, `test_a_clock_waiting_on_one_event_kind_written_bare_waits_on_that_kind`. Before (with only the helper added):
```
[plan_entity-aliases]       AssertionError: ['O', 'l', 'd', 'T', 'o', 'm']
[plan_creature-hunts]       AssertionError: ['c', 'e', 'l', 'l', 'a', 'r']
[presimulate-charters]      AssertionError: ['h', 'a', 'l', 'l']
[file_lore-knowledge_locations] AssertionError: ['q', 'u', 'a', 'y']
[plan_creature-active_phases] AssertionError: []      # "night" read as ANY hour
[incident-harms]            AssertionError: []        # a single harm object dropped
[set_vistas-vistas]         ValueError: set_vistas lists one or more vistas ...
plan_rooms frontier         ValueError: room 'shed': frontier 'e' is a direction ...
advance_on                  [{'event_kind': 'f'}, ...] == [{'event_kind': 'fire'}]
region_event rooms          ['q', 'u', 'a', 'y'] == ['quay']
```
WHAT CHANGED: one public helper `plot_packages.as_list(value)` -- a bare string or single object is ONE item, a list/tuple is itself, anything else is none -- read by every list field: `plan_rooms` frontier (both reads) / adjacent / over (replacing the one-instance fix), `plan_entity` aliases / known_by, `plan_creature` hunts / prey / active_phases (`_phase_names`), `file_lore` knowledge_locations / circles, `set_vistas` vistas, `presimulate` charters, `charter_ops` ops, `incident` harms, `director_note` rooms, a clock's `advance_on`, the package's own list fields (`_LIST_FIELDS`: a bare truth/clock was dropped) and `scope.locations`; `region_events.normalize_region_event` reads footprint rooms/structures and effects artifacts through it (lazy import, `world -> story` as that module already does).
REGISTER: none.

##### story_room_carriers.md

###### story_room_carriers#F2 (lane F1) — no consciousness gate anywhere in the carrier network
STATUS: fixed (the deterministic floor). The "audibility" half (a whisper copying a report to every named listener in the room) is NOT done: it needs the listener's own perception_outcome lines as evidence, which is wider than the finding (see SKIPPED).
FILES: `story/carriers.py`, `story/couriers.py`, `story/artifacts.py`.
TEST: `tests/test_a_mind_that_is_not_awake_takes_in_no_carried_word.py` -- `test_an_unconscious_body_acquires_nothing_where_a_surface_lands`, `test_a_knockout_resolved_this_beat_closes_the_channel_this_beat`, `test_a_telling_to_a_sleeper_or_from_one_is_refused`, `test_a_sleeper_reads_no_bill_and_is_handed_no_letter`. Before:
```
E       AssertionError: assert [{... 'claim': 'the reeve accused the miller aloud' ...}] == []   (x2)
E       assert 1 == 0     # telling applied to the sleeper
E       assert 1 == 0     # the sleeper read the bill
```
WHAT CHANGED: `carriers.awareness(ctx)` is perception's own map -- `apply_awareness_diff(awareness_map(cid), <this beat's resolved state_diff>)` -- and `carriers.not_awake(amap, entry)` answers the `NON_AWAKE_GATED` level under any name the carrier answers to (a crowd is never gated). A non-awake body still carries what it holds where its body goes, and takes in nothing (`advance_carriers`); a telling naming a non-awake speaker or listener is refused ("Mora is unconscious and hears nothing"); a courier is not sent by, questioned by, stopped by or handed to a sleeper (an addressed one WAITS at the gate until they come round; a place-addressed one skips sleepers); a bill is not posted, read or torn down by one. Each refusal names the level. No new failure mode: `awareness_map` raising propagates exactly as it does in perception.
REGISTER: AGENTS.md:443 / the carriers module note "only physical holders at that location acquire it" -- now also "and only an awake one" (the module note says so).

###### story_room_carriers#F3 (lane F2) — a newcomer acquires a passing act as an eyewitness
STATUS: fixed on the consumer side; producer half REQUESTED (below).
FILES: `story/carriers.py`, `story/couriers.py`.
TEST: `test_a_newcomer_files_a_standing_surface_as_standing_not_as_an_eyewitness`, `test_a_body_present_on_the_beat_is_still_the_eyewitness` (pins the unchanged half), `test_a_surface_its_producer_says_does_not_stand_is_not_offered_to_a_newcomer`. Before:
```
E       AssertionError: assert 'witnessed_surface' == 'standing_surface'
E       AssertionError: assert ['gate_barred', 'accusation_2'] == ['gate_barred']
```
WHAT CHANGED: what the arrival window offers (`standing_surfaces_reader`) is filed with provenance `standing_surface` (`carriers.STANDING_PROVENANCE`), never `witnessed_surface`, for a body (`advance_carriers`), a crowd (`_crowds_acquire`) and a caravan's cargo (`couriers._exchange_stops`); a surface seen on the beat it fired keeps `witnessed_surface`. Seeing replaces meeting-it-standing, both replace hearsay (`_sight_rank`). The window never offers a surface whose payload says `stands: false`. `standing_surface` is NOT in `charter_news.FIRSTHAND_PROVENANCE`, so a charter body that walks in reads it as unattributed news, not as its own eyes.
REQUEST (producer, `world/charter_runtime._scheduled_row` -- L11 -- and `world/charter_news.py`'s owner): beside `WITNESSABLE`, say per kind whether its surface STANDS (the state of a place: `upkeep_*`, `stock_*`, `incident`, ...) or PASSES (an act: `accusation`, `apology`, `aid_given`, `harm_done`, `goods_exchanged`, `report_*`, `commitment_*`, `institution_order_*`, `mobilisation_*`), and write `payload["stands"]: true|false` on every row with a `witnessed` surface. Until then every surface is offered as before (under the honest provenance), and a passing act still reaches a newcomer's ledger as `standing_surface` text.
REGISTER: AGENTS.md:443 "Later arrivals at still-standing surfaces" -- now filed as `standing_surface`, and a surface marked `stands: false` is never offered.

A result that fits is byte-for-byte what it was. Measured on synthetic towns: 12 institutions -> rows 1, 6 shown + 6 named, 11,677 chars, 0.08 s; 40 -> 3 shown + 37 named, 11,569 chars, 0.31 s. `fit_result` itself is unchanged (still the last resort for every tool).

###### story_room_carriers#F1 — a charter event's engine body keys reach minds word for word (consumer side)
STATUS: request (producer, L11) -- consumer change deferred until the producer's shape exists; assessed.
ASSESSMENT: every carrier stores `payload["witnessed"]` verbatim (`advance_carriers`, `_crowds_acquire`, the caravan's cargo, `couriers`/`artifacts` copies), and `degradation.degrade` redacts only names it is handed (registered + charter display names), never `{post}:{NNNN}` keys -- so the producer is the only place the key can be kept out. The consumer can then do one more thing the producer cannot: name a party the OBSERVER knows and label one it does not, at acquisition (as `tellers_as_known` does for tellers). For that the carrier needs the parties structurally, not the producer's rendered sentence.
REQUEST (L11, `world/charter_runtime._event_surface` / `_scheduled_row`): (1) build `witnessed` from what a bystander perceives -- the post/role nouns `carrier_entries` already uses (`_post_words`/`_member_namer`), through the story's language pack -- never a body key; (2) keep the parties on the payload as `payload["witnessed_parts"] = {"template": <compositor key>, "parties": {<slot>: {"name": <display name or "">, "label": <the stranger's word for them, in the story language>, "charter": <key>, "body": <key>}}}`. With (2) in place `story/carriers.py` can render each acquisition per observer -- `name` where the holder's `known` ledger recognises them, else `label` -- via `language_runtime.compositor_text`, and fall back to `witnessed` when `witnessed_parts` is absent. That consumer is a ~30-line change in `advance_carriers`/`_crowds_acquire`; I did not write it against a shape that does not exist yet.
REGISTER: none.

- story_room_carriers#F8, the grounding half (a send/post by a sender who declared nothing this beat -- "the dispatch itself is a decision the mind never declared"): needs a reader of the sender's own declaration (character step intents / the player's declared acts) that the carriers do not have; it touches the owner's 2026-10-20 ruling ("the Director writes undergoing, never deciding") -- an owner decision on what counts as the declaration.
- story_room_carriers#F20, the bounded wait for an addressee who EXISTS and never comes (and a route held by a door that never reopens): a wait in story time is an owner's number (`MAX_COURIERS` and any wait are the owner's). Suggested shape if wanted: `COURIER_WAIT_SECONDS` (named), after which an ARRIVED courier ends `undelivered` exactly as the addressed-to-nobody case now does.

`AssertionError: 'I watched the city fall to the invaders and fled north.' reached a mind ten years before it` (the past-frame native also got the thread, the origin window, the earlier window and the hearsay summary); the split test failed on the missing reader.
WHAT CHANGED: every window is a present-era fact (consolidation refuses any other frame), so each is now gated by `frames.is_memory_visible(char_id, None, viewer_frame, end_turn_idx)` -- the rule the raw rows obey -- after the turn cutoff and the couple-frame resolution `visible_memory_rows` applies. A traveller keeps the summaries, a later era reads them, an unmerged spatial split reads only windows that closed by the split. Unframed chats: no lookup, no change. Ran 161 existing tests over summaries/frames/the seam: green.

###### mind_memory#F5 — `update_lore` cleared the gate fields a caller omitted; a list of places raised (CORRUPTION, CRASH)
STATUS: fixed (in `update_lore`) + request (two callers)
FILES: mind/memory_lore_entries.py (`update_lore`, imports `_UNSET`).
TEST: tests/test_a_lore_edit_changes_only_what_it_states.py::test_a_rewording_keeps_every_gate_field, ::test_a_list_of_places_is_stored_as_json, ::test_a_stated_none_still_clears_a_gate_field. Before:
`AssertionError: assert None == 'Gate'` (title/tag/range/locations all NULL after `update_lore(eid, keys, content)`), `sqlite3.ProgrammingError: Error binding parameter 7: type 'list' is not supported`.
WHAT CHANGED: one convention -- a field not passed is left alone; for the five nullable gate fields (`title`, `knowledge_tag`, `knowledge_range`, `knowledge_locations`, `circles`) an explicitly passed None is written (that is how the host editor's `knowledge_tag: null` clears a tag, and how a restore writes NULL back); the other fields keep None = leave (they have no NULL meaning). `knowledge_locations` goes through `_storage_json`, so a list is stored as JSON. The host route (`web/app.py:4195`, passes every field explicitly) and the restore keep their behaviour; 150 existing lore/restore/overlay tests green.
REQUEST persist/commit_mapping.py (~:705, the authoring `op == "update"` arm) -- the audit's live scenario still clears the tag there because it passes `o.get(...)` (None) EXPLICITLY. Pass only what the op states:
```python
update_lore(
    o["id"], o.get("keys", row["keys"]), o["content"], cat,
    **{k: o[k] for k in ("title", "knowledge_tag", "knowledge_range") if k in o},
    **({"knowledge_locations": kloc} if "knowledge_locations" in o else {}),
    source_notes=o.get("source_notes"),
    embedding=o.get("_embedding"),
)
```
REQUEST story/importers.py (~:3110, the plan's `update` op) -- same class, and it also resets importance/aliases/scope/relations/source_notes to defaults when the op omits them:
```python
update_lore(
    entry_id, entry_op.get("keys", ""), entry_op.get("content", ""),
    category=entry_op.get("category"),
    **{k: entry_op[k] for k in ("title", "knowledge_tag", "knowledge_range",
                                "knowledge_locations") if k in entry_op},
    importance=entry_op.get("importance"), aliases=entry_op.get("aliases"),
    scope=entry_op.get("scope"), relations=entry_op.get("relations"),
    source_notes=entry_op.get("source_notes"),
)
```
(`update_lore` now serializes a list for `knowledge_locations`, so the crash is fixed without the importers change.)

###### mind_memory#F11 — the embedding repair backed off after rounds that SUCCEEDED (SILENT-NOOP)
STATUS: fixed
FILES: mind/memory_write.py (`_repair_loop`).
TEST: tests/test_the_embedding_repair_waits_only_on_refusal.py::test_a_productive_round_never_lengthens_the_wait, ::test_a_refusing_provider_still_backs_off_and_is_given_up_on, ::test_a_pass_that_raises_is_a_refused_round_not_the_end. Before: `AssertionError: the whole backlog is finished / assert not {385, 386, ...}` (500-row backlog left 116 queued), `assert 10 == 12` (rounds counted success), and a raised pass ended the thread with 100 rows queued.
WHAT CHANGED: a round that fixed rows (or found them already healed) resets the delay to `_REPAIR_DELAY` and does not count; only a refused round (fallback, nothing fixed, or a raised pass) doubles the delay and counts toward `_REPAIR_MAX_ROUNDS` -- the bound's documented meaning ("a provider that has refused for this long is gone"). Constants unchanged (30 s, 300 s, 12). A raised pass (`database is locked`) is now a refused round instead of ending the thread.
REGISTER: none (docs/guides/MEMORY.md's repair-lane description, if it says "backs off each round", now reads "backs off each refused round").

###### mind_memory#F4-addendum — the repair's UPDATE was not compare-and-set (CORRUPTION)
STATUS: fixed (the restore-side id reassignment race stays L3's)
FILES: mind/memory_write.py (new `_write_repaired`, `_REPAIR_DOCUMENT_COLUMNS`; `repair_pending_embeddings` writes through it for all four tables).
TEST: tests/test_the_embedding_repair_waits_only_on_refusal.py::test_a_host_edit_inside_the_embed_window_keeps_its_vector (before: `AssertionError: the repair wrote the OLD text's vector`), ::test_an_edit_that_also_fell_back_is_not_stamped_with_the_old_text (before: `assert 'test:live' == 'cheap:crc32:256'`), ::test_an_untouched_row_is_finished (control).
WHAT CHANGED: the repair's vector lands only `WHERE id=? AND embedding_model='cheap:crc32:256' AND <every column its document is built from> IS <the value embedded>` (NULL-safe); an overlay compares its merged keys/content, parent included. A row that changed inside the embed window is not written and STAYS QUEUED, so the next pass embeds whatever it holds then (or drops it if the editor finished it). `fixed` now counts rows actually written. As a side effect the CAS also refuses a write onto an id a restore re-minted onto a different row (different text) -- the restore-side race itself is L3's. 72 existing repair-lane tests green.
REGISTER: none.

###### mind_memory#F16 — `without_names` was English-only (SILENT-NOOP / WRONG-SCENE, language pack)

WHAT CHANGED: each form of the name (whole name, then the words `named_in` would match, longest first) is found with `language_runtime.name_boundary_pattern` (script-aware edges, L10's). The question's own script -- read off its words with the names taken out -- decides the replacement: spaced words get "them"/"their" exactly as before (the measured 48th->15th gain); a question in a script without spaces loses the name where it stands and gains no word of another language. "When did I first meet ヒナミ?" stays an English question ("...meet them?").
REQUEST language_packs (owner of `language_packs/*/cards/linguistics.json`): the remaining English assumption is the two words "them"/"their" used for every SPACED-script question; a third (spaced) pack would inherit them. Add `mind.memory._UNNAMED_PERSON` (en "them") and `mind.memory._UNNAMED_POSSESSIVE` (en "their"; ja may carry "その人"/"その人の" if the owner prefers a pronoun to removal) and then in `without_names` replace `("them", "their")` with `(_ling("_UNNAMED_PERSON"), _ling("_UNNAMED_POSSESSIVE"))` (memory_jev would import `_ling` from `mind.memory_common`). Not done here: `_ling` raises on a missing key and the about lane calls this outside its try.

###### NEW (found while measuring E3) — the mood's psych clock never advances in a clocked story: no mood decay toward home, no habit rest, no fading of lived memories' feelings
STATUS: request (cross-file: agents/character.py + persist/commit_memory.py; a design choice about the mood clock's time base -- not in my brief, not fixed)
EVIDENCE (engine.db, mode=ro): chats 175, 176, 177, 178 -- every mind with habits has `mood_clock: 1.0` after 12-35 turns while `simulation_clock.elapsed_seconds` reached 440-1143 s; chat 178 char 83: all 80 habit entries stamped `at: 1.0`, `affect_seconds: 1143.0`, `affect_turn: 35`.
MECHANISM: commit stamps `active_state.affect_seconds = _clock_seconds`, the clock at the END of beat N (persist/commit_memory.py:~1959). Beat N+1's character call computes the affect pass's psych units as `elapsed_psych_units(active.affect_seconds, _sim_clock.elapsed_seconds, ...)` (agents/character.py:~4380), and `_sim_clock` there is the clock at the START of beat N+1 -- the same number, so units = 0 ("time that did not pass decays nothing"). `carried()` adds 0 to `mood_clock`, commit copies that `mood_clock` back. The interval each beat itself spans (start N -> end N) is never added: only the very first beat (no stamp yet: fallback 1 unit) moves it. Consequences: `mix.decay` toward the card's home never runs between beats; `recall_lands` never rests a habit (so every re-delivered recent row climbs to the habituation ceiling in ~3 beats and stays); lived rows are all stamped at the same clock so `memory_fade` never ages them; F15's origin mismatch is moot while the clock is stuck.
PROPOSED FIX (owner/L6/persist): advance the mood clock by the beat's own elapsed units at commit (commit already computes `elapsed_units = elapsed_psych_units(prev affect_seconds, _clock_seconds, turns_since)` at ~:1852 -- add it to the copied `mood_clock`), or anchor `mood_clock` to the story clock in minutes for clocked stories (which also fixes mind_affect_psychology F15's origin mismatch). Either needs a test that two clocked beats 10 minutes apart decay the mood and rest a habit.

###### mind_memory#F15 — Japanese keyword search sees only extracted key phrases; dialogue rows never match; the lore keyword term is always 0
STATUS: request (L3: the FTS tokenizer / index text is core/db.py's)
WHAT THE INDEX MUST DO: today `memory_retrieval_fts` and `lore_fts` tokenize with `unicode61`, which keeps an unspaced run ("古い地図はどこ") as ONE token, while the query side (`memory_retrieval._memory_fts_query`, the pack's `_WORD_RE`) asks for kanji runs and katakana runs ("地図", "オレン"). The index must hold each SCRIPT RUN of an unspaced script as its own token -- exactly `language_runtime.fold_words` (L10's: splits where an unspaced script changes; for ASCII it is `re.findall(r"[a-z0-9]+", text.casefold())`, i.e. unicode61's own tokens) -- and keep spaced-script words as unicode61 does. NOT `tokenize='trigram'`: it cannot match a term under three characters, and most Japanese content words here are two kanji ("地図", "灯台").
PROPOSED REALIZATION (no custom tokenizer): segment at WRITE time, `" ".join(fold_words(x))` for each FTS column. Memories: in `mind/memory_write._replace_memory_fts` (MINE -- I will make that edit on request, it is four lines). Lore: `lore_fts` is filled by the `lore_ai`/`lore_au` triggers in core/db.py, so either register `fold_words` as an SQL function on the connection and call it in the triggers, or move the lore FTS writes into Python. Plus ONE reindex migration of both tables (existing rows were tokenized whole). Paired query-side change (MINE, lands with it): `memory_common._fts_query` (lore) still uses `[A-Za-z0-9]{3,}`; route it through the pack's `_WORD_RE` as `_memory_fts_query` does (on its own it changes English lore ranking and helps no Japanese row, so not done alone).
TEST to land with it: a Japanese dialogue row and a query sharing one kanji word -> a lexical hit; the English twin's ranking unchanged.

### L10

*Source: `<review scratch>/fixes/L10_language.md`.*

#### L10 — language runtime and dressing (fix log)

Lane L10, 2026-11-01, working tree on top of `3d7cdcde`. Python only as
`.venv/bin/python`; tests `-n 0 -p no:cacheprovider`. Every new test was run
and seen failing before its fix; the failing lines are quoted per section.

##### The helpers other lanes should use (exact import lines)

```python
from language_runtime import fold_key, fold_words, fold_text, script_of
from language_runtime import name_boundary_pattern, name_boundary_regex, find_names
from language_runtime import split_sentences, join_sentences
```

* `fold_key(text, sep="")` — NFKC + casefold, letters/digits/marks of EVERY
  script kept, everything else removed; with `sep`, each removed run becomes
  one separator and none stands at an end. For ASCII input `fold_key(x, "_")`
  IS `re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", "_", x.casefold()).strip("_")` and
  `fold_key(x, " ")` IS `re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9]+", " ", ...).strip()` (pinned by
  test), so ids already minted keep matching. `fold_words(text)` is the
  drop-in for `re.findall(r"[a-z0-9]+", x.casefold())` (identical for ASCII)
  and splits an unspaced name by script (`["佐藤", "ヒナミ"]`). `fold_text` is
  the NFKC+casefold half for callers with their own token regex. `script_of`
  answers hiragana/katakana/han/hangul/thai/"". Class bodies:
  `HIRAGANA KATAKANA HAN HANGUL THAI SPACED_WORD_CHARS UNSPACED_SCRIPT`.
* `name_boundary_pattern(form)` — same signature and return type;
  `story.character_schema.name_boundary_pattern` is now a delegate, so all
  ~80 existing call sites get the new rule unchanged. `name_boundary_regex
  (form, flags=0)` is cached here (E7). `find_names(text, forms,
  flags=re.I) -> [(start, end, form)]` is the SET matcher: longest form wins
  at a position, no span inside another ("Great Hall" is not also "Hall").
* `split_sentences(text, maxsplit=0, *, language_id=None)`,
  `join_sentences(sentences)`.

Counts: **13 fixed, 7 skipped (owner / out of scope), 14 requests.**

---

###### dressing_language_runtime#F7 + deliverable 2 — `name_boundary_pattern` matched inside longer kana/kanji words
- STATUS: fixed (rule moved to `language_runtime`, `character_schema` delegates).
- FILES: `language_runtime/__init__.py` (`name_boundary_pattern`,
  `name_boundary_regex`, `find_names`, `JA_NAME_FOLLOWERS/LEADERS`, script
  classes), `story/character_schema.py` (the function `name_boundary_pattern`
  only, now a delegate).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_name_is_whole_in_an_unspaced_script.py` (60 cases, both
  spellings). Before: `AttributeError: module 'language_runtime' has no
  attribute 'name_boundary_pattern'`, and through the old character_schema
  spelling e.g. `not inside a longer katakana name / assert True is False`,
  `test_the_disguise_scrub_keeps_an_angular_jaw` failing on `'ばった顎の…'`.
- WHAT CHANGED: an edge is a boundary at the text edge, whitespace or
  punctuation, or a script change (kana ↔ kanji ↔ Latin ↔ digit). Katakana:
  strict both sides (ヒナミコ, アヒナミ, プレイヤー no longer match).
  Hiragana: strict except a particle beside it (`JA_NAME_LEADERS` before,
  `JA_NAME_FOLLOWERS` after — particles, copula, honorific/plural suffixes,
  a closed class owned in code because the boundary is the NAME's script, not
  the story's). ONE-character kana/kanji: no kanji before; a particle, copula,
  honorific, punctuation, other script or the end after (角が ✓, 角ばった ✗,
  一角/頭角 ✗). Latin: byte-identical pattern strings (pinned by test).
- DEVIATION FROM THE BRIEF, deliberate: a kanji name of TWO OR MORE
  characters keeps open edges (as before). "Within one script class there is
  no boundary" would make `田中部長`, `佐藤隊長` (title compounds) and
  `山田花子` (given name inside an unspaced full name) unmatchable, and the
  identity scrub would then LEAK the name — the engine-failure direction. No
  multi-kanji over-match was measured by any audit; every measured one
  (ヒナミコ, アヒナミ, プレイヤー, 角ばった) is fixed. Strict kanji edges are an
  owner decision (recall vs precision for kanji names). Hangul/Thai keep open
  edges (no closed class says where their words end).
- Acceptance (all pass): ヒナミ in 「ヒナミは…」✓ 「ヒナミ、」✓, not in 「ヒナミコ」;
  レイ not in 「プレイヤー」; 田中 in 「田中さん」; 角 not in 「角ばった顎」.
- Verified wide: every test file that carries Japanese text or names a
  touched function, plus the dressing/time/room suites — final run 219
  files, 5107 passed, 2 xfailed (serial, after all edits).
- REGISTER: `docs/guides/LANGUAGE_PACKS.md:221-223` "Use
  `character_schema.cue_boundary_pattern` for alternations and
  `name_boundary_pattern` for names — both exclude only spaced-script
  letters, so Japanese matches after a particle" — now true of
  `cue_boundary_pattern` only; `name_boundary_pattern` is per-script (above)
  and lives in `language_runtime`.

###### dressing_language_runtime#F1 + deliverable 5 — Japanese room text reached the image prompt with people in it (LEAK)
- STATUS: fixed for names and pronouns; residual (population nouns) is a request.
- FILES: `dressing/backdrops.py` (`_setting_only(text, bodies)`,
  `_scene_bodies`, `_names_a_person`, `_pack_person_patterns`, `place_desc
  (room, scene=None)`, threaded through `_walls_of`, `_openings_of`, look,
  `visual_signature`, `room_projection`); `dressing/ambience.py` passes the
  scene.
- TEST: `tests/test_a_backdrop_strips_people_in_every_script.py`; before:
  `TypeError: place_desc() takes 1 positional argument but 2 were given`.
- WHAT CHANGED: sentences split by script (`split_sentences`), rejoined by
  `join_sentences`; a sentence goes if a BODY the scene holds stands as the
  SUBJECT of it or of one of its clauses (`scene_names_body` decides who is a
  body; forms = key, entity name, aliases, and the script-run parts of an
  unspaced name), or a person named by any installed story pack's pronoun
  recogniser (`agents.common._NPC_PRONOUN_RE`, boundary-checked: 彼方 is not
  彼) is, beside the English tables (unchanged, still any-mention). SUBJECT,
  per script (`_is_subject`): where the name runs straight into an unspaced
  script, its particle says so — は/が/も, after an optional honorific or
  plural suffix (`_JA_SUBJECT`, a closed class); in a spaced script, its place
  does — it opens the sentence or a clause (after `, ; : ( — –`). A body named
  in the English POSSESSIVE ("Tamamo's shrine") never counts; in Japanese the
  particle decides (ヒナミの / ヒナミに do not count). Every body the scene
  holds, never only those in the room, so the key cannot move because someone
  walked in.
- COORDINATOR'S ADJUSTMENT (after the first report): the rule was any-mention
  (with the possessive exception) and is now subject-limited, by the owner's
  rule that a guard firing on valid output is cut back once its rate is
  measured. Test first: `test_a_body_is_stripped_where_it_is_the_subject_not_
  wherever_it_is_named` (real kept sentences from the measurement + ja
  particle cases); before: `AssertionError: The room smells of old paper,
  aged wood, incense, silk, and Tamamo herself.`
- MEASURED (owner DB, 156 live scenes, 1,237 rooms, read-only, implemented
  rule, `<review scratch>/l10/dropped.py`): any-mention dropped 19 sentences, 15
  wrongly (before the possessive exception: 43, 38 wrongly); subject-limited
  drops 5 — the 4 that put a person in the frame and that the English verb
  table missed ("Hinami lies unconscious on the floor, bleeding."; "Tamamo is
  preparing a simple meal … at the hearth"; "Subject Nine has pressed
  eastward …"; "Subject Nine is stalled in the narrowing duct …") and 1 that
  does not ("Yusuf Kessler passed through this corridor on the first day …",
  history). The measurement sits in the `_names_a_person` docstring.
- What the narrowing gives back: a body mentioned but not the subject ("…
  that Hinami cannot decipher", "and Tamamo herself", ヒナミの古い本) now reaches
  the prompt; where such a sentence does put a person in the picture, the
  "no people" instruction is the only half left — the module's own
  belt-and-braces argument, now traded for the measured precision.
- The body predicate's own false positive (chat 118 `lamp`: two entities
  named "lamp"/"Lamp", so no unique record, and the bare position key counts
  as a body by tier 3) no longer costs a sentence ("A brass lamp …" — the
  lamp is not a subject), but is still a finding for the spatial lane.
- RESIDUAL (request): '非番の乗組員や民間人がここに集まる。' (off-duty crew and
  civilians gather here) still reaches the prompt — Japanese population,
  speech/posture and body words need pack data (`dressing.backdrops` module in
  BOTH packs; owner decision 2, UNBUILT_WORLD §1.65). I own neither en
  linguistics nor new ja keys.
- CACHE COST (subject-limited rule): `visual_signature` moved for 28 of
  1,237 live rooms (28 descriptions: the 5 sentences above plus the splitter
  no longer breaking decimals and titles — "2. 5 meters" is "2.5 meters"
  again); `acoustic_fingerprint` for 1; the day-phase bucket moved 0 scenes.
  Each moved backdrop is one paid redraw on next view. (Any-mention moved 65
  and 6.) Poll path +~0.9 ms per
  room key on ~50-subject scenes (1.0 → 1.9 ms, `visual_signature` +
  `acoustic_fingerprint`; module's own poll total ~5.4 ms).
- REGISTER: `Design.md:1107-1121` (backdrops "structurally excludes
  occupants") and AGENTS.md:528 ("a whitelist with no concept of an
  occupant") — the people strip on `desc` is now script-aware and name-aware.

  town's history). The planner's `presimulate` package (`story/plot_packages._prepare_presimulate`, which passed

  the first in-play beat after it.

  `people` (`AssertionError`).

  holds several) — no key is rewritten, so no identity changes for existing data. `transfer_person` lifts from
  `from_charter` when given; a bare key held by two institutions is refused ("say which institution they leave");
  a hermit employed again is POPPED from `people`; the transfer's `place` goes through `set_place`.
- REQUEST (`world/charter_ops.py` is mine and needs nothing: `depart` names its charter; `arrive` of a bare key
  employed twice is now refused by `transfer_person` with the reason above). None outside the lane.
- NOT DONE (not in the lane's list): core F9 — `walked` is not in `PERSON_STORES`, so a transfer drops it. Adding it
  changes the stored person shape; `_join_person_state` resets every PERSON_STORE before filling it, so an old
  registry's institution-level `walked` would be lost on first read unless migrated. Needs its own migration.
- REGISTER: AGENTS.md "The Charter/body dict key is permanent identity" — holds now for "/" keys and transfers.

###### world_charter_behaviours#E2 — the evidence pass scans every body per row, twice per beat
- STATUS: skipped. (a) The prefilter to `rooms_in_frame(actor room)` is the VISUAL frame; speech is graded by
  `hear_level` over `spatial_rel_between`, and I could not show that no "full" hearing reaches past it (a loud
  line through open ways), so it could change who hears. (b) Passing the appraisal's plan into the apply is unsafe
  inside a commit session: the plan is computed on the cached pre-commit registry and the apply runs on the
  session copy, which earlier commit domains (placements, leases) may have moved bodies in. Needs its own
  equivalence proof.

###### world_charter_core#E2, E3 — `normalize_charter` per step copies every experience row; ~7 normalizations per advance
- STATUS: skipped. `step` returns an un-normalized state (diagnostic fields `normalize_charter` drops, caps it
  applies), so "skip re-normalization of state step produced itself" is not behaviour-identical as written; and
  `advance_snapshot`'s deepcopy protects callers (tests among them) that reuse the registry they pass. Both need a
  measured equivalence harness; E1 removes the superlinear term the year test showed.

###### OWNER DECISIONS (logged, not decided)
1. **The 10 s beat budget does not hold for creature stories.** `advance_snapshot`'s `together` arm
   (`charter_predation.run_registry`) takes no deadline — "a group begun is a group finished"
   (DESIGN_INSTITUTIONS_AND_UPKEEP §7a). The budget bounds that path at entry only.
2. **The 720 h catch-up vs the skip grid (core F10).** A creature registry's `time_skip` beat is prepared for the
   `together` arm BEFORE the `context == "time_skip"` branch, so it is stepped on equal windows and the epoch seed
   (plus predation), not the `skip_windows` grid and `skip_seed` the cast lived; and any skip longer than
   `MAX_CATCHUP_HOURS` carries its remainder into later ordinary beats. AGENTS.md's "the town the story stores is
   the town every asked mind lived in" fails in both cases. Options: run the skip grid/seed through
   `run_registry`, or have `live_in_charter` run the predation rounds; carry the skip context with the shortfall.
3. **The quarrel idle constant** (`charter_practice.IDLE_CLOSE_HOURS` = 2.0) is unchanged; only where idleness is
   measured moved (G-F6). The `reconcile` 12 h gate and the empty-effect chooser (G-F6 residual) are tuning.
4. **Creature `sight` default (B-F3)**: honoured when written `false`; undeclared reads as eyes. Declared-not-
   defaulted would blind every existing creature.
5. **An errand for a leased (on-screen) body (B-F14)** is refused by name. Releasing the lease on dispatch is the
   alternative; it needs the commit to drop the entity's scene rows too.
6. **Name novelty floors per script (S-F9)**: `NAME_RUN_FLOOR`/`NAME_PREFIX_FLOOR` (4) are code points in every
   script; the engine's own unspaced precedent (`_display_floor`) is 4 -> 2.
7. **Key-named bodies (C-F14)**: rename on the next normalization, or keep (now warned).

###### world_charter_core#F7 — `save_registry` outside a commit is last-writer-wins (assess: read token or transaction)
- STATUS: fixed in my files as an opt-in seam + REQUEST (the race lives in callers outside the lane)
- FILES: `world/charter_runtime.py` (new `registry_update(cid, frame_id)` context manager)
- TEST: `tests/test_a_registry_update_outside_a_commit_loses_no_write.py` (2 tests: two overlapping updates from two
  threads both land; an update that raises saves nothing). Before: `ImportError: cannot import name
  'registry_update'`.
- ASSESSMENT: of the two fixes the audit offers, a transaction is the smaller and certain one: `transaction()` holds
  `core.db._write_lock` for its whole body, the lock `land_snapshot`/`land_presim` already take, so a read-modify-
  write inside one cannot interleave with a landing. A read-token check would need the token threaded from the
  read to the save and a refusal path in every caller. Cost: the lock is held across the registry parse (about
  0.1 s at 0.9 MB, seconds at the 41 MB registry measured) and blocks a turn commit for that long.
- REQUEST (callers, not my files): replace the unguarded `registry_for_update(...)` ... `save_registry(...)` pairs with
  `with registry_update(cid, frame_id) as registry:` in `agents/director_lots.deal_berths` (~306-331, the
  lot-expansion job), `world/region_events.apply_wave` (~321-388, reached from `plot_packages.fire_due_clocks` after
  the commit transaction), and the charters editor's post in `web/app.py` (~6142).
- REGISTER: none.

  connective list is kept for Latin names as it was (no other language's added — the rule against word lists).
- REGISTER: UNBUILT_WORLD §2.44 (dealt sibling names vs an unlanded planned room elsewhere) — the broader, generic-
  name class is now closed for rooms that carry a structure.

  test mentioning a charter (1,621 tests) — and the ops/generation/transfer group (734) are green on `.venv`.

###### T#F1 — `parent_entity` spelled by the holder's name splits structural from permissive readers (LEAK)
STATUS: fixed

WHAT CHANGED: every room's `parent_entity` is folded at merge onto the unique entity KEY its spelling names (name, alias, case); ambiguous or unknown spellings are left as written. Stored scenes heal on their next merge (the merge is the only caller of `normalize_scene_subjects`, so the fold was not duplicated there). Note for F15 (not this lane's): `normalize_scene_subjects` still folds `contained.in` toward the display NAME, so the ledger form and the place form now systematically spell one holder as name vs key; readers comparing the two must use `same_subject` (F15's own fix).

Before: `assert None is True` for `rel.get('air')` on a walled gridless pair (rel was `{'barrier': 'wall', 'distance': 'near', ...}`), and the rooms-away chain came back `{'barrier': 'separated', 'distance': 'far', 'same_room': False}` with no `far_loss_db`.

The frontier-join special case is subsumed (same far stamp). The device rescue's earned case (rooms no edge joins: no `air`) is pinned unchanged.

One existing fixture assumed the old row: tests/test_a_declared_follow_carries_the_player.py `_trail` put the 'out of earshot' Far Glade two NEAR open trail segments away, which the far field prices as carrying a normal voice (25 dB loss vs a 30 dB floor). The room registry needs nothing: `_prepare_room_registry` retires rooms by diffing pre/post scene, so a retired interior is retired there too. The follower test passes before and after (apply_following_ops already pruned it) — kept as a pin.
NOTE: destruction (`persist/commit_destruction.py`) routes doomed entities through `remove_entities`; their unoccupied interiors are now retired by the merge itself, which is what destruction intends (it already does the book-scoped version).

###### SL#F2 — the room fold (and every other `[a-z0-9]` slug in L12a's files) is ASCII-only (CORRUPTION, latent in ja)
STATUS: fixed
FILES: world/spatial_identity.py (`normalize_room_id` = `fold_key(name, "_")`; `room_name_words` via `fold_words`, hiragana runs dropped as the unspaced script's function words), world/spatial_barriers.py (barrier-word and fastening separator folds), world/spatial_geometry.py (window id slug; entity `state.position` words), world/spatial_containment.py (`_label_tokens`), world/spatial_contacts.py (`_part_identity` = MS#F2; `_contact_motion_from_text` tokens)
TEST: tests/test_a_room_is_found_by_its_name_in_every_script.py (6). Before: `normalize_room_id("台所") == ''`, `scene_room_id(sc, "台所") == ''`, `normalize_room_id("Café Rouge") == 'caf_rouge'` (4 failed; the ASCII and legacy-accented-id pins passed).
WHAT CHANGED: L10's `fold_key`/`fold_words` replace the ASCII classes; for ASCII input they are the same string, so every minted id keeps matching. Accents are kept (L10's ruling: "café" is not "cafe"), so the audit's "Café and Cafe fold together" is NOT done; an id minted under the old fold ("caf_rouge") still answers by its key and by its name. Left on purpose: bearings (`spatial_orientation`), distance/speed units (`spatial_routing`, `spatial_transit`) — protocol tokens; the room-size name hint (T#F18, owner's); the contact-migration tables (SL#F3, English-only and fail-safe); the shed-object helper in spatial_containment (L4's).
PIN MOVED (audit showed it wrong): tests/test_a_place_name_fills_the_grammars_slot.py — two assertions pinned the H1 workaround key "planned_room"/"planned_room_2", which existed only because the ASCII fold gave a kana name the empty id. The kana stub is now keyed by its own name: "長屋", "長屋_2" (the file's invariants — no "" key, kana compared by its own letters — still asserted). 19/19 green.
WIDER: `normalize_room_id` has ~25 callers outside L12a (director_prose.reserve_places, common_cast, director_lots, commit_room_registry, commit_mapping, structure.mint_frontier): every one now gets non-empty, script-preserving slugs for non-ASCII names — the fix the audit asked for, but it reaches ja stories' room ids, registry keys and lorebook dedup at once. 283 tests across every file naming the room-identity functions green.

###### MS#F1 — nearly a fifth of written postures read as STANDING; a body laid down behind a sill is seen over it (LEAK)
STATUS: fixed in L12a's files + REQUESTS (the closed class must be asked of the encoder)
FILES: world/spatial_fov.py (`POSTURE_CLASSES`, `UNREAD_POSTURE`, `UNREAD_POSTURE_TOP`, `posture_class`, new `target_rank`, `_tokens` via `fold_words`; `body_visibility` uses `target_rank` for the target), world/spatial_geometry.py (`_clean_pose` keeps a valid `posture_class`)
TEST: tests/test_a_posture_the_engine_cannot_read_is_not_standing.py (7). Pre-fix, checked against the committed module (`git show HEAD:world/spatial_fov.py` loaded in a scratch copy): `posture_class(reclining) = standing`, `body_visibility(..., Keeper) visible = True, hidden_below 'waist'` — the audit's leak. (The test file was written after the code, so this is the recorded failure.)
WHAT CHANGED / WHAT I CHOSE (the lane asked me to log it): (1) the engine's own closed classes are read first — a pose's `posture_class` (standing|sitting|kneeling|crouching|lying), then the class words in the text in any script; (2) a posture that IS written and that none of that can read answers `unread` (nothing written still answers `standing`; an entity's `state.position`, which is as often WHERE as HOW, never makes an answer unread); (3) for the TARGET of a line of sight an unread posture stands at the LOWEST class (`UNREAD_POSTURE_TOP = "lying"`, a named owner-visible constant), so a fixture between hides it; the OBSERVER's eye keeps the standing default, and every other reader keyed by class (`EYE_M.get(cls, standing)` in site_plan, vistas, landscape; `_EYE_RANK.get(cls, 2.0)`) is unchanged by the new word. THE TRADE (owner's): a written-but-unreadable upright posture ("leaning on the bar") is now hidden behind a waist-high fixture until its pose states `posture_class`. 1,603 tests across 52 files naming posture/sight functions green.
REQUESTS: (a) llm/schemas.py `PoseDef`: add `posture_class: str = ""` (or a closed enum) so it survives validation; (b) both packs' `encoder/poses.txt`: ask for `posture_class`, the engine's closed class, beside the free `posture` text; (c) world/site_plan.py `plan_sight` (the measured window-over-the-garden case is the cross-storey `plan` path, which reads `posture_class` through `EYE_M.get(...)`): take the TARGET's `unread` at `spatial_fov.UNREAD_POSTURE_TOP` the way `target_rank` does — until then the plan path still treats an unread target as standing.

###### SND#F9 — card acuity/channel/range, event `kind` and edge `material` read with English token lists
STATUS: partial (tokenisation) + REQUEST (the class fix is a card contract this lane does not own)
FILES: world/spatial_senses.py (`_sense_channel`, `sense_acuity_offset`, `sense_range_class` tokenise with `fold_words`)
TEST: none new — ASCII-identical by construction (L10's `fold_words` contract); 1,257 tests naming the senses readers green. No failing-first test is possible for a tokeniser swap that changes no English answer.
ASSESSMENT: "route through the pack" would mean Japanese synonym tables for free prose in `linguistics.json` — the word-list class CLAUDE.md warns against, in files this lane does not own. The audit's own class fix is the better one and needs no ja vocabulary at all: closed protocol tokens on the cards, as `barrier` and `level` already are, normalised at ingest. The readers here already answer the closed tokens exactly (absent→None, dulled→-1, ordinary→0, keen→+1, extraordinary→+2; hearing|sight|scent → channels). The misreads the audit measured ("high-frequency hearing loss" -> +1, "deaf in one ear" -> deaf, "ordinary, nothing lost" -> deaf, 聴覚/鋭い -> ordinary, event `kind: 音` dropped from the field) are free-text answers and are NOT fixed here.
REQUESTS: (a) both packs' character-card senses note: state `acuity` as one of absent|dulled|ordinary|keen|extraordinary and `channel` as sight|hearing|scent (the fiction's own word may go in `notes`); (b) `story/character_schema.character_card_warnings` (and the card normaliser): warn on / fold a free-text acuity onto the closed set; (c) both packs' `encoder/sensory_events.txt`: `kind` is one of hearing|sight|scent|touch (protocol tokens, untranslated, as `barrier` is in ja); (d) planner/room cards: edge `material` as the engine's material words (the `_MATERIAL_SOUND_STEPS` keys). Once (c) lands, `normalize_sensory_event` could fold `kind` at ingest — not done here, since without the card it changes nothing for ja.

  before: ImportError (`PROJECTED_SOUNDS_KEY`); behaviour before, `lb_audit/emission.py`: `beat 2, rendered and switched off: {'level': 'loud'}`.

Because the room's projection now stops once the plan is rendered, the thing the engine stands up carries the plan's declared emissions (`light_source`, `light_shape`, `light_height`, `steadiness`, `sound_source` -- the same closed fields `plot_packages._plan_sources` validates); before, an engine-minted generator was audible only through the never-cleared room projection. `test_it_mints_the_bare_fact_and_the_plan_s_own_look` (a plan with no sources) still holds its key set.
REQUEST (persist/commit_scene_state.py, L4): `PROJECTED_SOUNDS_KEY` into `_ENTITY_RENAME_SKIP` (above).

###### world_misc_causal#F1 — a look at a far place told the mind the place's real name, learned or not (LEAK)
STATUS: fixed
FILES: world/landscape.py (`looked_far`)
TEST: tests/test_a_far_look_names_only_what_the_mind_knows_and_what_is_there.py::test_a_stranger_s_look_by_id_is_told_no_name, ::test_no_person_s_name_reaches_a_look_through_a_place_s_name
  before (the new tests run against HEAD's landscape.py): `['To the east, you can see Gale Green.']`; `["Toward Widow Marsh's Orchard, the buildings around you stand between you and it."]`
WHAT CHANGED: a place is labelled by the name this mind knows (`known`), else by the look's own word -- never the room's own name. A look word that is a room id is the encoder's pointer ("its id where world_index gives one"), not the actor's word: a place SEEN by one carries no label, so the standing line describes it as it describes any unknown place (ground, height, look); a place HIDDEN is "toward" the word as written (underscores as spaces), because the hidden templates need a label; every word said back goes through the observer's people gate (`_through_gate`).
PINNED TESTS CHANGED (the audit showed the pins were the leak; each now carries a CHANGED note and a known-name variant asserting the old line): tests/test_what_stands_between.py::test_a_look_at_a_storey_is_a_look_at_its_building_and_is_answered and ::test_a_look_at_a_hidden_place_names_what_stands_between (the two the lane named), plus ::test_a_look_from_a_closed_room_meets_its_walls and ::test_a_room_that_declares_no_ground_is_seen_where_its_building_is -- the same leak, a stranger's look by id answered with the room's own name.
RESIDUAL: a hidden place looked at by id says the id back ("Toward chapel, ..."); where the Room minted an id from a person's name, that word reaches the gate as written. The class fix is upstream: REQUEST (encoder card owner, language_packs/*/cards/system_prompts/encoder/core.txt `look`): keep the actor's own words for a far place beside the id. REQUEST (agents/composer_landscape.py + both packs' compositor.json): an unnamed variant of the `far_look_hidden_*` templates, so a stranger's hidden look needs no label at all.
REGISTER: Design.md:580 ("named by the look's word where the mind knows no other ("a two-storey building where the bell loft is")") -- now: named by the name the mind knows; a stranger's look by a room's id is described, unnamed, and a hidden one is told the look's own word.

###### world_misc_causal#F6 (the lane's "F5") — a failed look named an occluder that was not there
STATUS: fixed for the far layer; request for the horizon (vistas) and for a wall with no name
FILES: world/landscape.py (`far_places` gains `aimed=` and `stopped=`; `looked_far`; new `_what_stopped_the_walk`)
TEST: ::test_a_place_past_the_graded_cap_is_answered_as_seen, ::test_an_eye_that_sees_nothing_is_told_no_occluder, ::test_a_named_wall_is_what_a_look_is_told, ::test_no_person_s_name_reaches_a_look_through_a_place_s_name (no "buildings around you" on all-open ground)
  before (against HEAD): `[{'room': 'spoke39', ..., 'hidden_by': {'kind': 'around', ...}}]`; the blind look answered; the named wall unsaid.
WHAT CHANGED: a sightless body's look returns nothing (the cause is its eyes, which no template says); a place a look aims at is graded past `PLACES_GRADED_MAX` (the standing view's cap and walk unchanged when nothing is aimed); the walk records where and why it stopped (a mass that ended the line, a wall or shut gate between open grounds with its edge's own name, another locale, past `REACH_HOPS`); a target it never reached is told the looker's own walls (a walled room), the buildings round open ground only where a mass ended the walk, a wall or gate by its own name -- and nothing otherwise (past the reach, another locale, an unnamed wall, no way at all). Caps unchanged: `PLACES_GRADED_MAX` 32, `REACH_HOPS` 12, `LINES_MAX` 4.
REQUEST (agents/composer_landscape.py + both packs' compositor.json): a `far_look_hidden_wall` template ("Toward {label}, a wall stands between you and it." / ja "{label}の方は、塀に遮られている。") and the composer's kind list to accept it; then `_what_stopped_the_walk` returns `{"kind": "wall"}` for an unnamed wall instead of None. (Returning an unknown kind today renders the false "around" line: the composer maps it there.)
REQUEST (agents/perception_standing.py + composer_landscape.vista_percepts): a sightless body's look at a vista -- `vistas.what_a_look_finds` falls through to "unseen_nearer" ("nearer ground rises between you and the Grey Range"). Skip the look for a body whose card sees nothing (or add a template that says its eyes); `what_a_look_finds` returning None today would render as SEEN, so it was not changed here.

### L12c

*Source: `<review scratch>/fixes/L12c_world_time_misc.md`.*

#### L12c — world time, off-screen, weather, paradox, causal, misc (fix log)

Lane L12c, 2026-11-01, working tree on top of `3d7cdcde` (+ L10's
`language_runtime` helpers). Python only as `.venv/bin/python`; tests
`-n 0 -p no:cacheprovider`. Every new test was written first, run against
the unfixed tree from the scratchpad, and seen failing; the failing lines
are quoted per section. Finding ids are the AUDIT's own
(`world_offscreen_time#Fn` = OT, `world_misc_causal#Fn` = MC); where the
orchestrator's lane text numbered an item differently, it is noted.


##### Summary

**Fixed 18** (15 with a new failing-first test; 3 comment/dead-code only),
**verified 1** (scene_memo/beat_ledger), **skipped 5** (owner), **requests 7**.
Files: `world/{mechanics,weather,day_cycle,skip_itinerary,offscreen,living_world,
routines,paradox,degradation,causal_program,causal_verification,causality,comfort,
place_purpose,subjects,gaps,beat_ledger}.py`; 14 new test files; two pinned test
files changed (`test_the_cast_lives_through_a_skip.py`, `test_weather.py`) --
each change is argued in its section.

Behaviour changes wider than their finding, to know before committing:
1. EXPOSURE (OT F3): a room's DESCRIPTION can no longer put it under the
   open sky -- only its name can; measured on 671 unauthored live rooms: 53
   records change, all toward lit (12 indoor names fixed, 6 outdoor names now
   lit at night), none darker.
2. SKIP COMPANY (OT F1): two minds at the same off-map label share company
   only when each names the other in `with` (the design note's label rule
   changes).
3. PARADOX WOUND (MC F4): the wound now heads whichever prose field the view
   reads (notes, else desc, else description) instead of being appended to
   notes; `_HAZARD_WOUND_NOTE` lost its leading space.
4. DEGRADATION (MC F2/F3): "score"/"couple"/"few"/"handful" are no longer
   counts; "a dozen" -> "several"; clock readings keep their digits.

###### world_offscreen_time#F14 — a non-finite time claim was adopted as the story clock (CORRUPTION)

  `time_diff_claims`, `time_diff_duration` read every claimed number through it).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_non_finite_time_claim_is_refused.py` (9 tests). Before:
  six parametrized cases failing at `assert math.isfinite(elapsed)` /
  `elapsed == approx(100 + FLOOR)` (e.g. `{'duration_seconds': '-inf'}` was read
  as a claimed 0 and charged nothing), `{"start_seconds": "NaN", "end_seconds": 130}`
  displaced a readable end (`max(0.0, nan)`), and
  `assert inf == 0.0` for `time_diff_duration({"duration_seconds": "inf"})`.
- WHAT CHANGED: NaN/±inf parse but measure nothing, so they now raise inside
  the readers' existing `except (TypeError, ValueError)` and land exactly where
  a word lands: refused, reported, and charged `UNCLAIMED_BEAT_SECONDS` --
  the rule `beat_time_from_spans` already applied to spans. `time_diff_claims`
  keeps agreeing with `read_time_diff` (pinned). The STORED clock is not
  re-read (`clock_elapsed` unchanged): no live clock is non-finite, and this
  closes the way one could become so.
- REGISTER: none.

- TEST: `tests/test_a_room_keyword_is_read_only_as_a_whole_word.py` (6 tests).
  Before (draft run on the unfixed tree, and the new assertions checked
  against `git show HEAD:world/weather.py` loaded as a module):
  `assert 'open' == 'enclosed'` (Tavern Common Room / "Wooden tables"),
  Washroom / "the window faces the mountain" -> `open`, Tavern Room / "street
  outside" -> `open`, `assert 'sheltered' == ''` (a brazier "Uncovered"),
  `_matches(... "A mineral spring" ..., _DEEP_WORDS)` -> `True`.
- WHAT CHANGED + MEASURED (read-only, `engine.db?mode=ro`, script
  `<review scratch>/l12c/exposure_measure.py` / `exposure_variants.py`):
  * whole words ALONE moved 44 of 671 unauthored room records but was a wash
    in the dangerous direction: it fixed 6 indoor names read open by a
    substring and newly darkened 5 indoor names (Hinami's Room, Tavern Room,
    Ten Forward, The Green Door Inn, West Deep Passage) whose descriptions
    name what their windows look out on ("mountain", "street", "a bank of
    viewports") once the substring hits of enclosed words ("hold" in
    "holding", "tunnel" in "tunnels") stopped shielding them.
  * so UNDER THE SKY IS NOW THE NAME'S TO SAY: a description may still take a
    room indoors (enclosed/sheltered/deep words read over name+desc) but
    never out under the sky. Of the 14 distinct rooms the description alone
    read `open`, 9 were indoors.
  * Final, against the substring reading: 53 records change, ALL toward lit:
    52 open->enclosed (12 distinct indoor names put right; 6 outdoor names
    left lit at night -- Boatyard, Exterior Grounds, Ferry harbor apron,
    Inland route to the ferry port, Moonlit shoreline, Outskirts of the ferry
    port: unlisted or compound words, the direction rule 2 accepts), 1
    sheltered->enclosed (a station's Command Deck). No room reads darker.
- PINNED TEST CHANGED: `tests/test_weather.py::test_two_scenes_sharing_a_room_id_do_not_share_an_answer`
  -- its outdoor fixture was a room NAMED "Hall" described "An open
  courtyard", exactly the desc-only open reading the measurement above shows
  darkening indoor rooms; renamed "Courtyard" (the test's point, a memo not
  keyed on the room id, is untouched).
- OWNER QUESTIONS (not decided here): "Street -- lined with shops" reads
  `enclosed` (the plural "shops" is an enclosed word; it read enclosed
  before too, so unchanged) -- letting a name's open word outrank a
  description's enclosed word fixes it but measured 2 bunker rooms named
  "Shelter" turning `sheltered`; compound outdoor names (boatyard, shoreline)
  read `enclosed`. Both fail lit, never dark.
- REGISTER: UNBUILT_PERCEPTION §1.18 and UNBUILT_WORLD §2.28 say the
  fallback's "failure direction is ... never the reverse" -- now true by
  construction for descriptions; §1.18's "the fallback does the work for 86%
  of rooms" stands (the registered fix, storing the derived exposure at
  commit, is still open).

###### world_offscreen_time#F1 — two minds each "home" were told each other's doings (LEAK)
- STATUS: fixed. PINNED TESTS CHANGED (the audit showed the pinned label
  rule is the leak).
- FILES: `world/skip_itinerary.py` (`_names_mind`, `_kept_company`, new;
  `share_company` asks `_kept_company` before writing company; the module and
  function docstrings restated), `tests/test_the_cast_lives_through_a_skip.py`
  (two assertions, below).
- TEST: `tests/test_an_off_map_label_is_no_shared_place.py` (7 tests). Before:
  `test_two_minds_each_at_home_are_not_told_each_others_doings` (Alice saw
  `{'who': 'Bob', 'doing': 'counts the stolen coins'}`), the same in 家/家,
  and one-sided `with` at "the pub" sharing both ways -- 3 failed.
- WHAT CHANGED: a ROOM the world holds is one place for everyone in it, as
  before. An OFF-MAP LABEL is a mind's own words, and the same words from two
  minds are no evidence of one place (a place named relative to its speaker
  is a different place for every speaker), so off the map two minds keep
  company only where they name the same label AND each names the other in
  its own `with` (its whole name, or a whole name within it -- "Pell" for
  "Pell Hardacre" -- script-aware: `fold_key`, `find_names`; never the name
  inside a longer entry, so "Nell's brother" is not Nell, pinned). Never
  folded prose alone. The player row is
  always a room, so the player's company is unchanged.
- PINNED TESTS CHANGED, both asserting the label rule the audit measured as
  the leak:
  * `test_company_reaches_each_observer_as_its_doing_never_its_why` -- D and E
    at "The Harbour Pub"/"the harbour pub" now name each other in `with`
    (the test's subject, doing-never-why per observer, is unchanged).
  * `test_the_stage_shares_company_by_each_observers_recognition` -- Pell and
    Nell, strangers at "the harbour pub" who never named each other, were
    asserted to see each other's doings; now asserted to see none.
  299 tests over the skip stage/itinerary/memories green.
- REGISTER: `docs/design/DESIGN_THE_CAST_ACROSS_A_SKIP.md` § "Company" --
  "Two minds in the same room, or at the same off-map label, over overlapping
  time each receive the other's `doing`" -- now: "...or at the same off-map
  label where each names the other in its own `with`". (A design note, not a
  register; quoted for the orchestrator.)

###### world_misc_causal#F2 + #F3 (lane "F2") — rumour degradation did nothing in Japanese and garbled English counts (WRONG-SCENE)
- STATUS: fixed in `world/degradation.py`; REQUEST for the pack words.
- FILES: `world/degradation.py` (`_replace_phrases` matches a name/place
  through `language_runtime.name_boundary_pattern`; `_language(name)` reads
  `COUNT`, `ARTICLE`, `VAGUE_COUNT`, `VAGUE_PLACE`, `VAGUE_PERSON` from the
  story pack's `linguistics["world.degradation"]`, else the English defaults,
  logging once per language that lacks them; `_PLURAL_NUMBER_WORDS` loses
  "score", "couple", "few", "handful"; `_COUNT` swallows an indefinite article
  and leaves a clock reading's digits alone).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_rumour_loses_its_details_in_every_script.py` (9 tests).
  Before: `'門の通路' is contained here: ミラ・ホルトが門の通路で衛兵を三人殺した`
  at TIER_PLACES; "Mira settled the several with the miller"; "a several of
  the guards saw a several of riders"; "a several of them went home"; "They
  met at several:several by the well"; "a several guards fell"; and the pack
  table was never read. Existing `tests/test_degradation.py` (19) green.
- WHAT CHANGED: in any script the caller's name and place are matched whole
  and lost at hops 3 and 2; English counts are numbers only. With the
  REQUEST below a Japanese rumour degrades into Japanese ("三人" -> "数人",
  the place -> "どこか", the name -> a pack word for a stranger); until then
  it degrades with the English defaults (a firewall floor first: the name is
  lost), and the log says so once.
- REQUEST: `language_packs/en/cards/linguistics.json` and
  `language_packs/ja/cards/linguistics.json` -- add a `"world.degradation"`
  section. en (the module defaults, so the en pack owns its words):
  `VAGUE_COUNT "several"`, `VAGUE_PLACE "some place"`, `VAGUE_PERSON "a
  stranger"`, `ARTICLE "(?:\\b(?:the|a|an)\\s+)?"`, `COUNT` = a
  `{"$type":"regex", ...}` of `world.degradation._COUNT.pattern` with
  `re.IGNORECASE` (2). ja: `VAGUE_COUNT "数"`, `VAGUE_PLACE "どこか"`,
  `VAGUE_PERSON "見知らぬ誰か"` (or the pack's `unknown_actor_fallback`
  wording), `ARTICLE ""`, `COUNT` = `{"$type":"regex","pattern":
  "(?:[0-9０-９]+|[二三四五六七八九十百千万]+)(?=[人名匹頭羽個本台隻棟軒枚])",
  "flags":0}` (a numeral before a counter; 一 alone excluded, as English
  excludes "one"). A pack author should own the counter list.
- RESIDUAL (English, not fixed): "No. 7 Mill Lane" and "quarter past six"
  still lose their number -- a number in an address or a time read without
  digits; the en pack's `COUNT` is where that refinement belongs.
- REGISTER: none.

###### world_misc_causal E1 — `canonical_subject_map` rebuilt twice per `_same` comparison (PERF)
- STATUS: fixed.

  `_build_subject_map`, `_subject_map`, `_canonical`, new; `_same` folds
  against a map built once per world; `verify_patch` opens the memo for the
  call and closes it in `finally`, the body moved verbatim to `_verify_patch`).

  tests). Before: `assert 148 <= 2` map builds for one 12-entity call.

  aliases 645.5 ms -> 4.7 ms; 100 entities 18,124 ms -> 22.9 ms; receipts
  identical (asserted).

  its worlds) and holds the scene object beside its map so a recycled id
  cannot answer for another world; outside a call nothing is kept (pinned:
  `test_nothing_is_kept_once_the_call_returns`). A thread-local, not a
  ContextVar: a first cut used a ContextVar and
  `project_check.check_turn_scoped_contextvars_are_cleared` rightly flagged
  that a job's copied context would inherit it; a per-call memo has no
  business riding anywhere.

###### llm_providers_quality#F1 — a mind could read the previous mind's private reasoning
STATUS: fixed
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_a_mind_never_reads_the_trace_of_the_call_before.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_a_mind_never_reads_the_trace_of_the_call_before.py (5 tests). Before the fix all 5 failed:
`AssertionError: assert 'A private: I will betray B at dawn' is None` (blocking Anthropic, streamed Anthropic,
a call dying before the wire, the CLI with no thinking) and `assert 'A private: I...ray B at dawn' == "B's own reading"`.
WHAT CHANGED: `_chat_complete_once` (and the dead async twin) now clears `last_reasoning` with the other three
"last call" records at its start; `_claude_cli_complete` clears it at its own start and always writes it
(`reasoning or None`); `_sse_anthropic` now reads `thinking_delta`s into the trace (and the reasoning sink, never
the player-facing token sink) and writes `last_reasoning` (None when none arrived); the blocking Anthropic read
writes the thinking blocks' text or None. Engine-side only: L6/L9 own the reader-side resets in `agents/character.py`.
REGISTER: `providers.py:70-76` ("diagnostic only") and `mind/thoughts.py:17-20` ("no other reader ever read
`last_reasoning`") are now true again on the engine side; no UNBUILT entry found for F1.

###### llm_providers_quality#F2 — aborting one turn degraded every embedding caller queued behind it
STATUS: fixed
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_an_aborted_turn_never_degrades_another_turns_embeddings.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_an_aborted_turn_never_degrades_another_turns_embeddings.py (4 tests). Before the fix:
`AssertionError: chat B never aborted: its vectors must be the provider's, not crc32` (B got
`model_key='cheap:crc32:256' ... error='generation aborted by user'`); the leader's ledger got `[1, 3]` (the
followers' round trip booked to the leader's step); two runs' callers were merged into one request
(`[['a', 'b'], ['lead']] == [['a'], ['b'], ['lead']]`).
WHAT CHANGED: `_EmbedWaiter` keeps a copy of its caller's context (cancel event + ledger sink); a group takes only
callers of ONE run (same cancel event, or none), and `_serve_embed_group` runs the request inside that group's
context, so an abort closes only its own run's request. The request's ledger entries are collected and shared out
to each caller's own ledger (`texts`, a proportional share of `in`, new key `shared_by`; shares sum to the bill).
An aborted leader whose own answer is in hand hands leadership to the next queued caller instead of serving other
runs' requests on the aborted turn's thread. Coalescing within one run (parallel character retrievals) is unchanged;
callers of different runs no longer share a request (cross-chat concurrency is the rare case).
REGISTER: none found naming F2.

###### llm_providers_quality#F4 — the research fetch read any http(s) address, local network included
STATUS: fixed (engine side) + request
FILES: llm/research_providers.py; tests/test_research_reads_only_public_pages.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_research_reads_only_public_pages.py — before the fix every private address was SENT
(`KeyError: 'http://127.0.0.1:8181/v1/embeddings'`, `'http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/'`, `[::1]`,
`localhost`, a name resolving to 10.x, a name resolving to one public + one private address, `0.0.0.0` — the fake
session was asked for each), a public page's 302 to 169.254.169.254 was followed (`Failed: DID NOT RAISE
ResearchError`), and the streamed response was never closed (`assert False ... .closed`).
WHAT CHANGED: `_fetch_html` resolves the host and refuses unless EVERY resolved address is public
(`ipaddress.is_global`, IPv4-mapped IPv6 unwrapped; loopback/private/link-local/shared/reserved refused);
redirects are no longer followed by requests (`allow_redirects=False`) but by hand, each hop re-checked, at most
`FETCH_REDIRECTS_MAX = 5` hops (NEW NAMED CAP — requests' default was 30; flag for the owner); after connecting, the
socket's peer address is checked too where urllib3 exposes it (best-effort DNS-rebinding guard); the response is
closed on every exit; the returned `url` is the final hop's (the page the text actually came from).
REQUEST (story/room_research.py, owner of `fetch_page`): enforce the docstring "Read one page the search returned"
— before `_disclose`/`rp.fetch`, refuse a `url` that is not among `store["searches"][*]["results"][*]["url"]`
for this chat (e.g. `if url not in {r["url"] for s in store["searches"].values() for r in s.get("results") or []}:
raise ValueError("only a page a search returned can be read")`). The engine-side refusal above holds without it.
REGISTER: Design.md:400 (research disclosure) still holds; the UNBUILT_PLANNING "Research is grantable" bullet does
not name F4.

###### llm_providers_quality#F9 — research pages without a charset header decoded as Latin-1
STATUS: fixed
FILES: llm/research_providers.py; tests/test_research_reads_only_public_pages.py (TestTheCharsetIsThePagesOwn)
TEST: `test_utf8_without_a_charset_header_is_read_as_utf8` failed before: `assert 'æ\x97¥æ\x9c¬...aã\x83¼ã\x82¸' ==
'日本語のページ'`; `test_a_meta_charset_is_honoured` failed (`'\x93ú\x96{\x8cê' == '日本語'`).
WHAT CHANGED: the header's charset is used only when the header names one; else a BOM, else the page's
`<meta charset>`/`http-equiv` declaration, else UTF-8 (incremental, so a page cut at `FETCH_BYTES_CAP` mid-character
still decodes), else cp1252.

###### llm_providers_quality#F6 — an unset `embeddings` role inherited the default CHAT model
STATUS: fixed
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_an_unset_embeddings_role_is_the_local_hash.py (new)

'cheap:crc32:256'` and `an unset embeddings role sent a request ... ['https://ope...1/embeddings'] == []`.

to `default` for those roles, so an unset row raises "No model configured for role 'embeddings'",
`embedding_model_key()` returns `cheap:crc32:256` (the documented unset state) and no HTTP request is made. Every
