###### llm_providers_quality#F8 — a json_schema 400 re-imposed as a fresh 24 h suspension at every restart, never pruned
STATUS: fixed (prune/heal); two parts skipped
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_a_grammar_rejection_heals_across_restarts.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_a_grammar_rejection_heals_across_restarts.py (6 tests, real settings rows on `temp_db`, a fake
clock, `_restart()` clearing the in-memory state). Before: `AssertionError: a restart renewed the window ... Obtained:
1800090000.0 Expected: 1800086400.0` and `assert ['glm-b'] == ['glm-a', 'glm-b']` (a second 400 dropped the first);
the prune-on-answer hook did not exist.
WHAT CHANGED: new `_SCHEMA_REJECTED_AT` (key -> epoch of the 400). The `providers_no_json_schema` row is now
`{"<prov>\u0000<model>": epoch}`; a legacy bare-list row is stamped with the load time ONCE and written back.
At load each entry becomes a suspension until `rejected_at + _SCHEMA_SUSPEND_SECONDS` (not now + 24 h); a lapsed
entry is pruned from the row. `_persist_schema_blacklist` writes the merged dict (loaded + this process's), and
`_note_json_schema_rejected` loads first so it never overwrites. New `_note_json_schema_answered` removes the entry
when a grammar-bearing stream returns (wired in `_chat_complete_once`). Unchanged: in the process that saw the 400
the verdict stays permanent (`test_a_400_still_never_lapses` still passes).
SKIPPED: (a) keying the verdict on the schema hash as well as the model ("one step's oversized grammar costs every
role on that model") — a design change to what the verdict is about, beyond prune/heal; owner's call.
(b) `_SCHEMA_STALLS` never decaying / resetting on success — changes the measured "two stalls" threshold semantics
(a model stalling 1-in-3 would never be suspended); owner's call.
REGISTER: memory note "The grammar blacklist never heals" and `providers.py`'s own docstring ("A REJECTION ... is
permanent") — the docstring is updated in place; the note (owner's memory) is stale for 400s as well as stalls now.
UNBUILT_PLATFORM §1.186 (json_object drop does not survive a restart) is a different item, unchanged.

###### llm_providers_quality#F7 — `request_timeout` raised only the socket, `patient_stream` only the clock
STATUS: fixed (assessed: the two knobs now do what their docstrings say; the default path is unchanged)
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_both_patience_knobs_reach_both_deadlines.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_both_patience_knobs_reach_both_deadlines.py (5 tests; two on a real local SSE socket at test scale).
Before: under `request_timeout(2.0)` a first token at 1.0 s -> `llm.providers.ProviderSilent: provider silent for 1s
(before any token)`; under `patient_stream(2.0)` -> `requests.exceptions.ConnectionError: ... Read timed out.`; the
control (no knob, same silence) was cut before and after.
WHAT CHANGED: new `_stream_deadlines(patience, read_override)` returns ONE `(first token, between tokens)` pair —
`max(socket first-token deadline, patience, read override)` and `max(PROVIDER_SILENCE_SECONDS, patience, read override)`
— read by `_sse_openai` (socket AND `_ActivityClock`), `_sse_anthropic` and `_claude_cli_complete`;
`_between_tokens_limit` is defined through it. With neither knob set the deadlines are exactly 30 s / 10 s as before.
A knob below the defaults no longer LOWERS the clock (`patient_stream` is documented as "raise"; no caller passes
less than 180). Behaviour change to flag: `request_timeout(...)` callers (the lorebook generator only) now also get a
patient clock between tokens (was 10 s), as `_between_tokens_limit`'s docstring promised.
REGISTER: AGENTS.md:554 ("The lorebook generator's `timeout` is the per-call knob") is now true of the clock too.

###### llm_providers_quality#Efficiency-1 (lane brief: "F12 PERF") — the guard's short-loop regex was ~80% of its CPU
STATUS: fixed (verdicts identical)
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_the_short_loop_rule_reads_as_the_regex_did.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_the_short_loop_rule_reads_as_the_regex_did.py — 24 tests: every window of a 1,500-text fuzz corpus
(random alphabets, injected loops of period 1-26 x 60-100 at every phase, prose with loops spliced in, two start
offsets) gets the regex's verdict; the 80/81-repeat boundary at every period 2-23 and several phases; and the whole
guard fed in random chunk sizes raises at the same stride with the same reason as the regex-driven guard. Before the
change the tests failed only on the missing `_short_loop` (AttributeError) — they are an equivalence oracle, the
old regex kept in the test as SPEC.
WHAT CHANGED: `OutputGuard.feed` calls new `_short_loop(text, start, end)` instead of `_SHORT_LOOP_RE.search(tail)`:
sample every 79p characters for each period p in 2..23, and grow a repeating sample into its maximal p-periodic run
by binary-searched slice comparison (`_periodic_run`). Exact, not the audit's end-anchored test: the end-anchored
version misses a loop that reached 81 repeats and stopped between two strides (a `"Ha"x100` passed 81% of the time),
which the regex flagged — so it would NOT have been verdict-identical. Measured in the scratchpad prototype: 3.57 ms
-> 0.050 ms per 4 KB check on prose (~70x); 0 mismatches in 40,000 fuzzed windows. `_SHORT_LOOP_RE` stays defined as
the rule's statement.
REGISTER: none.

###### llm_providers_quality#E2 (Efficiency 2) — every step thread opened a new connection
STATUS: fixed
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_step_threads_share_one_connection_pool.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_step_threads_share_one_connection_pool.py — before: two threads' sessions carried two different
`HTTPAdapter` objects (`assert <...HTTPAdapter at 0x..b3b0> is <...HTTPAdapter at 0x..9400>`).
WHAT CHANGED: `_session()` still keeps one `requests.Session` per thread, but mounts ONE shared `HTTPAdapter`
(`_shared_adapter()`, built once under a lock) for http and https, so urllib3's thread-safe pools reuse kept-alive
connections across step threads. NEW NAMED NUMBER: `HTTP_POOL_MAXSIZE = 32` idle connections kept per host — not a
concurrency ceiling (the pool never blocks; a burst past it opens extra connections and urllib3 logs "Connection pool
is full" when discarding them); requests' default was 10 per session. Flag for the owner.
REGISTER: none.

###### llm_providers_quality#F12 — the degenerate-output guard never saw reasoning
STATUS: fixed (false-positive rate measured first: 0)
FILES: llm/providers.py; tests/test_a_looping_trace_is_stopped.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_a_looping_trace_is_stopped.py — 2,000 identical reasoning fragments before an answer; before:

trace and an answer that each repeat a little are judged apart.
MEASUREMENT (owner's rule for a guard on valid output): the guard's battery run over every stored trace in
`engine.db` (read-only): 0 fires on 3,036 per-call traces (`llm_capture` ⋈ `llm_blobs`); 5 fires on 7,174 step
traces (`variants.reasoning`) — two distinct texts (one stored in four variants), each a 5,950/7,116-character block
said twice back to back and running to the 20,000-character storage cap (variant 29851 interaction_loop, variant
41959 et al.): loops, not prose. Script: scratchpad `l13/reasoning_fp.py`, hits in `l13/reasoning_hits.json`.
WHAT CHANGED: `_sse_openai`, `_sse_anthropic` and `_claude_cli_complete` feed reasoning deltas to a second
`OutputGuard` per response (the same battery, kept apart from the content guard). A looping trace now raises
`DegenerateOutput` (retryable; the ladder treats it as a runaway: fresh ask with varied sampling) instead of billing to
`max_tokens`. The dead async readers are untouched.

###### llm_schemas_prompts#F2 — `normalize_speech_volume` folded any unknown word to "normal" silently; alias table vs card
STATUS: fixed (warn + follow the card); two pinned assertions changed (see below)
FILES: llm/schemas.py; tests/test_a_volume_outside_the_set_is_said.py (new); tests/test_a_line_pitched_to_its_addressee.py
and tests/test_speech_concealment.py (one assertion each — pins of the old soft->mutter fold)
TEST: tests/test_a_volume_outside_the_set_is_said.py (20 tests). Before: 8 failed — no warning for `whispered`, `low`,
`projected`, "pitched low, only for Hinami" (`AssertionError: []`), `soft`/`softly` -> `'mutter'`, `excited`/`enthusiastic`
-> `'loud'`.

but now emits a step warning naming the word (`_note_drop`). The alias table now FOLLOWS THE CARD
(`encoder/core.txt`: volume "is how far the speaker means the words to carry, never how they sound: a soft, breathy,
shaky, murmured or moaned line ... carries as any voice does, at normal"): `soft`, `softly` -> normal (were mutter);
`excited`, `enthusiastic` -> normal (were loud — how a voice sounds, which the card says volume is not); the card's
own `breathy`, `shaky`, `murmured`, `moaned` -> normal, listed so they do not warn. `quiet`/`quietly` -> mutter
unchanged (reach, not sound). New `_VOLUMES` tuple is the closed set.

`tests/test_a_line_pitched_to_its_addressee.py::test_the_sixth_word_is_a_volume` `normalize_speech_volume("soft") ==

`DialogueLogEntry(..., volume="softly").volume.value == "mutter"` -> `"normal"`.
NOT DONE (not asked; owner): folding a member's inflections (`whispered`->whisper, "pitched low..."->pitched).
`world/spatial_senses.py:1379` `_volume_word` has its own unknown->normal fold at hearing time (not this lane's file).

###### llm_schemas_prompts#F3 — the first JSON mend that validates was accepted even when it moved a member
STATUS: fixed (prefer the least-lossy mend; name the loss loudly)
FILES: llm/llm_quality.py; tests/test_a_mend_that_loses_what_the_model_wrote_says_so.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_a_mend_that_loses_what_the_model_wrote_says_so.py (4 tests; the audit's `scene_life` case — the `}`
closing `entries[0].speech` deleted). Before: `_mended`'s report carried no warning (`AssertionError: []`); the ladder
said only "the answer's JSON was mended locally (closed an object left open before its next sibling); no model call";
a lossy candidate yielded before a lossless one won (`['closed an o...next sibling'] == ['swapped two closers']`).
FINDING WHILE FIXING: in all three of the audit's worked cases (`director_establish` doubled `}`, `scene_life`
missing `}`, `resolve_repair` doubled `}`) `mend_candidates` yields exactly ONE candidate — the lossy one — so there
is no better mend to prefer there; naming the loss is what lands for them.
WHAT CHANGED: `_mended` computes, for each validating candidate, the outermost key paths it carried that the validated
output no longer does (`_mend_lost`; None-valued keys are not content). A lossless one is returned at once (as before
when the first was lossless); otherwise the least-lossy one is returned with `MEND_LOSS` "the mend validated only by
dropping what the answer wrote at <paths> -- a closer in the wrong place may have moved it into an object that has no
field for it" appended to its report's warnings, which `_accepted` says on the step (main rung, rebuild-mend and
fresh-mend paths). `_read_answer`'s goes-on path adds the same note when the loss is below the top level (it already
named top-level losses). The brief's "top-level keys match" wording was generalised to key paths because the
`scene_life` loss is nested (`entries.0.speech.action`) and top-level keys match there.
REGISTER: AGENTS.md (`llm/json_mend.py`) "a mend is used only if it validates" — still true; now also "and a mend
that validates by losing content says what it lost".

###### llm_schemas_prompts#F7 — a decision answer missing a key read as a confident "no"
STATUS: fixed (engine side: never silent) + request (caller-side fail-open)
FILES: llm/decisions.py; tests/test_an_unanswered_decision_is_said.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_an_unanswered_decision_is_said.py (3 tests). Before: `AttributeError: module 'llm.decisions' has no
attribute 'unanswered'` (the missing key was silent), and `_post` on a JSON-list body raised
`AttributeError: 'list' object has no attribute 'get'` instead of `DecisionError`.
WHAT CHANGED: `decide` (the real path; `OVERRIDE`, used by 51 test files, is left alone) now says on the step how
many questions came back unanswered and which; new public `decisions.unanswered(questions, answers)` names them for a
caller; `probability`'s docstring says an unanswered key is not a no; `_post` treats a non-object body as no reply
(the typed `DecisionError`). `probability(None)` still returns 0.0: which way an unanswered question should fail is
the caller's ("is this channel touched?" fails open; "does she strike?" must not), so the engine names it.
REQUEST (agents/director_prose.py, `select_channels`, owner of the decision-model read for channels): after
`answers = decisions.decide(...)`, treat every channel in `decisions.unanswered(battery, answers)` as granted
(fail-open, as AGENTS.md says the decision model does) and add a warning naming them — e.g. before computing
`probabilities`: `missing = set(decisions.unanswered(battery, answers)); for ch in missing: answers[ch] = {"type":
"noul", "noul": 1.0}` plus `ctx.add_warning(f"{stage}: decision model left {sorted(missing)} unanswered; granted
(fail-open)")`. Same review for `director_repair` (:759, :1250), `skip_memories:959`, `story_planner:2812`, each in
its own direction.
REGISTER: AGENTS.md "the decision model FAILS OPEN -- every channel granted -- when it cannot answer" holds for raised
errors; for a partial answer it holds only once the request above lands.

###### llm_schemas_prompts#F5/F6 — the repair example asked for `sources`, which `UnifiedEvent` drops
STATUS: fixed (example) + request (card text)
FILES: llm/schemas.py; tests/test_every_output_example_keeps_what_it_shows.py (new)
TEST: tests/test_every_output_example_keeps_what_it_shows.py — every `OUTPUT_EXAMPLES` entry validates and drops no key
path it shows. Before: `[director_repair]` failed: `assert ['answers.0.events.0.sources'] == []` (the only example
any schema dropped, as the audit measured).
WHAT CHANGED: `OUTPUT_EXAMPLES["director_repair"]` no longer shows `"sources": ["s3"]`; a comment says why. The field
was not added to `UnifiedEvent`: nothing reads a model-written `sources` — `director_repair` credits each recovered
event with its job's sentences (`event.setdefault("sources", ...)`) and recomputes attribution by trigrams
(`attribute`) — so asking for it pays for an answer that is discarded either way (the `held_by` class, resolved by
removing the ask).
REQUEST (L10/L14, card owners): `language_packs/en/cards/system_prompts/prose_contract/encoder_repair.txt` line 4:
"Return in `events` only the missing events, in the order they happen, each with `sources`; set `after` ..." -> drop
", each with `sources`"; ja `encoder_repair.txt` line 4: "...起こる順に、それぞれ `sources` を付けて返してください" ->
"...起こる順に返してください". (Line 1's description of the DRAFT's `sources` stays: the draft does carry them.)
REGISTER: memory rule "a field a card asks the model to WRITE must be on the output schema" — satisfied once the card
request lands.

###### llm_schemas_prompts#E2 — the `_plain` walk
STATUS: fixed differently (a faster walk, same output by construction); `model_dump(mode="json")` REJECTED
FILES: llm/schemas.py; tests/test_validation_reads_each_field_shape_once.py
TEST: equivalence tests (the old walk kept in the test as the reference): every OUTPUT_EXAMPLE's python-mode dump and
edge values (tuples, int keys, IntEnum, str-Enum, NaN, bytes, sets, dict/list subclasses) give identical output with
identical types — passing before and after, as an equivalence oracle should.
WHY NOT JSON MODE: measured in the scratchpad: identical on all 16 examples, but NOT byte-identical in general —
NaN becomes null (`ser_json_inf_nan`), tuples become lists, non-string dict keys become strings — so it fails the
brief's "only if byte-identical" condition.
WHAT CHANGED: `_plain` dispatches on the exact type first (dict, list, then str/int/float/bool/None returned as is)
and falls back to the old `isinstance` chain in the old order. Measured: 3.14 -> 2.21 ms on the 163 KB resolve;
with E1, 18.2 -> 12.6 ms a validation.

###### tooling_gate#F1 — `make check` and `make run` ran a stack no player has
STATUS: fixed
FILES: Makefile, tools/project_check.py (`--stack`: `constraint_pins`,
`installed_versions`, `stack_differences`, `report_stack`, `ALLOW_UNPINNED_ENV`),
.github/workflows/ci.yml (a `make stack` step)
TEST: tests/test_the_gate_runs_on_the_stack_players_run.py (drives the real
Makefile in a scratch dir against a stand-in interpreter);
tests/test_the_stack_check_reports_what_differs_from_the_pins.py.
Before: `AssertionError: ['uvicorn web.app:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8008
--reload ...', 'python tools/project_check.py', 'python tools/generate_code_map.py']`
(no `.venv/bin/python`), `AssertionError: uvicorn web.app:app ...` (no
`python -m uvicorn` fallback), `AssertionError: ('check', 'python -m compileall
...')` (no stack step first); `AttributeError: module 'project_check' has no
attribute 'constraint_pins'` / `'stack_differences'` / `'installed_versions'`.
WHAT CHANGED: `PYTHON ?= $(if $(VENV_PYTHON),$(VENV_PYTHON),python)` with
`VENV_PYTHON` the first of `.venv/bin/python`, `.venv/Scripts/python.exe`; `run`
and `serve` are `$(PYTHON) -m uvicorn` (`check_asgi_targets` still reads them).
New `make stack` = `project_check.py --stack`: prints the interpreter and each
`constraints.txt` pin's installed version, exits 1 when an INSTALLED pin
differs (a pin not installed, e.g. Playwright outside the browser env, is no
difference) unless `ALLOW_UNPINNED=1`. `check` and `check-fast` run it first.
On `.venv` it reports all twelve pins matching; on the system `python3` it
lists pydantic 1.10.14, pytest 9.1.1, requests 2.31.0, starlette 0.31.1,
uvicorn 0.27.1 and refuses.
REGISTER: CLAUDE.md:48 "**`make check` runs whatever `python` resolves to,
which is very unlikely to be the stack that ships.**" — now `.venv` when the
checkout has one, and `make stack` says which stack; CLAUDE.md:42-46's
`check-fast`/`check` lines now begin with the stack step (`make stack` could
join the Commands block). docs/guides/ENGINEERING.md:797-798 ("`make
check-fast # the same, minus database-backed slow tests`") was already stale
and is more so: both run every test; `check` regenerates the map, `check-fast`
verifies it. TESTING.md was updated (owned).

###### tooling_gate#F2 — the facade rule could not see relative imports
STATUS: fixed (+ notice to owning lanes)
FILES: tools/project_check.py (`resolve_relative_import`, `_import_nodes`,
`_facade_violations_in`, `facade_import_violations(..., module=)`,
`facade_dependencies`, `check_facade_import_direction`); IMPORT LINES ONLY:
agents/character.py (the two `from .director_floors`/`.director_views`
imports -> `from .director import ...`), agents/perception_stream.py:87 (the
audit's `agents/perception.py:864`, moved there verbatim by the perception
split: `from .director_evidence import ...` -> `from .director import ...`),
world/charter_creature.py (`from .spatial_fov` -> `from .spatial`). All four
are function-level imports of names the facade re-exports (each checked `is`
the sibling's object).
TEST: tests/test_a_relative_import_is_held_to_the_facade_rule.py — before: 7
failed (`TypeError`/`AttributeError: module 'project_check' has no attribute
'facade_dependencies'`, and the repo-level test).
WHAT CHANGED: relative imports are resolved against the file's dotted name and
judged like absolute ones. `agents/director_prose.py:70,71,408` were NOT
changed: `agents/director.py` imports `director_prose` as a module at module
scope (`from . import director_prose`, before `director_fanout` is bound), so
the facade spelling at module scope is an import cycle, and the names at 408
(`QUOTED_LINE_CARRIED`, `_QUOTED_SPAN`, `_carried_share`, `_line_words`) are
not re-exported at all. The rule now names such a module a facade DEPENDENCY
(`facade_dependencies`: a `<stem>_*` module the facade imports as a module at
module scope) — upstream of the facade, held to the outside-in rule for
nothing, still importable directly by its own callers (57 import lines name
`director_prose`). `tools/` is deliberately NOT scanned (stated in the
docstring): its drivers patch and introspect siblings, nothing imports them,
and 39 of their imports name siblings.
NOTICE TO OWNING LANES: agents/character.py (L6), agents/perception_stream.py
(perception owner), world/charter_creature.py (charter owner) — one import line
each, nothing else.
OPEN, NOT MINE: the gate (the OLD gate too) reports ABSOLUTE outside-in
imports added by other lanes' in-flight work -- 11 to 20 while this lane
worked; the final list is under "The gate's verdict on the tree as it stands".
Each should import the facade (`agents.director`, `agents.common`,
`mind.memory`, `world.spatial` re-export every name), or name the sibling only
to patch it. `make structure` stays red until they do. None is a relative
import: after this lane's four edits the relative reading finds nothing the
absolute one does not.
REGISTER: CLAUDE.md "`tools/project_check.py` enforces that for `world.spatial`,
`agents.director` and `persist.commit` alike: a caller imports the facade" —
now true for relative spellings too, and the seven families are
`agents.common`/`composer`/`perception`/`director`, `persist.commit`,
`mind.memory`, `world.spatial`.

the test at teardown if the engine swallowed it; `@pytest.mark.network` stands
it down. Ran green with the guard: every test file naming a real host
(listed under "Verification") and the causal-stress harness, whose own
`no_network()` stacks on it.

reached `embed_texts_meta` through `prepare_memory_commit` under the composer
file's autouse `forbid_model_calls`; it now stubs that door
(`patch_provider_seam`) and says why. Assertions unchanged.

now fails (by design). Not run: the whole suite (forbidden); see Verification.

###### tooling_gate#F6 — the contextvar check read 2 modules of 9
STATUS: fixed (check + per-test reset) + request
FILES: tools/project_check.py (`BACKGROUND_SAFE_CONTEXTVARS` +4 with reasons;
`_contextvar_names` text gate; the check reads `engine_python_paths()`,
reports a var name defined in two modules), tests/conftest.py
(`_fresh_engine_context` replaces `_fresh_provider_context`), tests/helpers.py
(`engine_contextvars`)
TEST: tests/test_every_engine_contextvar_is_cleared_or_declared.py — before:
`AssertionError: []` (a var in story/ was never read; a duplicate name never
reported), `ImportError: cannot import name 'engine_contextvars'`. The repo-level
`tests/test_repo_hygiene_source_roots.py::test_turn_scoped_contextvars_are_cleared_or_declared_safe`
now FAILS on three vars until core/jobs.py clears them (below).
WHAT CHANGED: declared safe, each with its reason: `active_frame_id` (every job
producer sets and resets its own — jobs.py's own 2026-08-19 reasoning),
`answered_by` (an output slot: written on acceptance, its one reader sets ''
first), `current_language_id` (inherited on purpose; tests/test_jobs_queue.py),
`_room_scope` (inherited on purpose, per persist/llm_capture.py's own comment).
The per-test reset now returns all 23 engine ContextVars (12 beyond
`llm.providers`) to their declared defaults and restores them after.
REQUEST (core/jobs.py, owner of `_clear_turn_scoped_context`): add the vars
that name something ending with the turn, reply, commit or job, inside the
function so the check sees the names. Three were in the audit; a fourth was
added by another lane while this lane worked (`world/layout_bridge.py`
`CANCELLED`) and the widened check caught it on arrival (so did it catch
`agents/director.py` `_SPEAKER_LABELS`, since turned into a `threading.local`
by its lane, which a job does not inherit):
```python
    from story.room_citations import _rows_read
    from world.charter_runtime import _REGISTRY_SESSION
    from extension_runtime.api import _COMMIT_SCOPE
    from world.layout_bridge import CANCELLED
    ...
    for var in (token_sink, reasoning_sink, generation_event_sink,
                call_ledger_sink, cancel_event, current_warning_sink,
                current_step_key, current_decision_sink,
                current_exchange_sink, beat_onset,
                _rows_read, _REGISTRY_SESSION, _COMMIT_SCOPE, CANCELLED):
        var.set(None)
```
(The check reads the bare names inside the function, so import each var
UNALIASED -- an alias or an attribute such as `layout_bridge.CANCELLED` is not
seen. Reasons: `_rows_read` is a Room
reply's mutable citation set — a job's reads would count as the reply's;
`_REGISTRY_SESSION` a commit's private registry copy — a job would mutate a
session the commit already flushed; `_COMMIT_SCOPE` the extension commit
scope — a job would be allowed `state.set()` outside the commit; `CANCELLED`
one layout job's stop predicate — a job started under it would stop when that
job is stopped. None is the "outside" value of all four.) Update its docstring's
"a NEW contextvar in llm/providers.py or core/pipeline_context.py" — the check
now reads every engine module. Until this lands
`tests/test_repo_hygiene_source_roots.py::test_turn_scoped_contextvars_are_cleared_or_declared_safe`
and `make structure` are red on these four.

never fail a request. The pinned `localStorage.getItem("chime.muted")`/`setItem` strings (test_frontend_turn_signals) are kept.

`_freshRunPending` and the sliced refresh follow `ok` (so a failed run now really refreshes whole, as the openChat comment says). The composer refill

resumable turn keeps it -- refilling would play it twice) and only in the story/frame it was typed in. The pinned strings in the runStream block

###### frontend_core#F6 — a dropped or reloaded stream was never re-attached
STATUS: fixed (assessed: the server contract is clear -- `turn_new` writes the row before the stream opens; `GET /api/turns/{tid}/status` gives
running/committed and 404 for a discarded stepless turn)
FILES: static/js/chat.js (followRun, newestTurnOfRun, followEnded, followRunningTurn; openChat/switchFrame call followRunningTurn),
static/js/utils.js (`apiOrNull`: a read whose 404 is an answer; `streamPost(url, body, onEvt, onOpen)`)
TEST: tests/test_a_run_ends_the_way_its_stream_says.py (dropped stream followed through an offline poll to commit; closed-without-ending followed;
404 -> line back; stopped partway -> line kept; a running turn found on open is followed; a finished one is not) -- before: `assert False is True`,
`ReferenceError`-exit for followRunningTurn; browser_tests/test_a_turn_stream_that_breaks_is_followed.py::test_a_stream_that_breaks_is_followed_to_the_finished_turn.
WHAT CHANGED: once the server accepted the run (`onOpen`), a broken read or a stream closing without a terminal event no longer ends the run on the
page: Stop stays bound, Send stays held, the status line says "Connection lost — following this beat on the server…", and the run is polled at
`/api/turns/{tid}/status` (tid = run.turnId, or for a composer run the newest turn past the held one, preferring the run's frame) until it ends;
then the normal finish (refresh, chime only on commit; "This beat stopped before it finished." otherwise). Opening a story (and switching frames)
probes the newest beat of the frame on screen when it has no prose and follows it if it is running -- the reload / other-tab / `?detach=1` case.
NUMBERS (named, owner's to tune -- flagged): `RUN_FOLLOW_POLL_MS = 2000`, doubling to `RUN_FOLLOW_POLL_MAX_MS = 30000` while the server does not answer.
No give-up timeout: like a live stream, the follow lasts until the server says the run ended (a reload always frees the page).
NOT DONE: a `running` flag on the chat payload (the audit's other suggestion) -- unnecessary with the probe; `?detach=1` submission -- would lose
the live log/early narration, not adopted. Known interaction: web_app F1 (thread.join on disconnect freezes the server) makes the first status poll
hang until the turn commits; the follow still ends correctly.
REGISTER: none.

if that story is still open.

###### frontend_core#E1 — the translator scanned all 446 template rules per miss; the observer re-translated catalog values
STATUS: fixed
FILES: static/js/i18n-core.js (new i18nRuleIndex / i18nCandidates / i18nIsTranslation; i18nTranslate uses them)
TEST: tests/test_a_translator_miss_tries_few_rules.py (node: the indexed translator answers exactly what the full linear scan answers over every
pack x keys, values and every template filled 7 ways; rule attempts per miss <= 20; a ja value is not re-translated) -- before:
`AssertionError: ('en', 408.4166666666667)` attempts per miss, and the ja value "set_vistas には名前と方角（n, ne, e, se, s, sw, w, nw）…" came back
garbled as "s, ne, e, nw）を持つ眺めを一つ以上挙げてください s, sw, w, nw）…". Existing browser_tests/test_ui_translator.py (rule order + one-pass
match equivalence) passes unchanged.
MEASURED (node 24, 10k misses, HEAD -> now): en prose 1472 -> 204 ms, en names 1342 -> 105 ms; ja prose 1432 -> 197 ms, ja names 1325 -> 121 ms
(7-13x). Scripts: <review scratch>/l15a/bench.js.
WHAT CHANGED: candidates are looked up by the literal a rule must begin with (probing only lengths for the source's first character) or, for a rule
that begins with a placeholder, the literal it must end with; the 11 rules with both edges empty, and any regex rule, are always tried; candidates
are tried in the rules' own weight order, so the first match is the full scan's. A string that is a catalog VALUE but not a key is returned as is
(it is el()'s output coming back through the observer). The index lives in a WeakMap beside the rules array, so `i18nCompileTemplates`' result is
unchanged (the browser test compares it to the old regex rules). The audit's "skip the scan for the identity pack" was not needed once misses are cheap.

###### frontend_core#E3 — the whole transcript was re-rendered after every beat

###### frontend_core#F11 — composed English the catalog can never match; two catalog entries that could never fire
STATUS: fixed + request (catalog keys)
FILES: static/js/chat.js, static/js/app.js, static/js/components.js, static/js/utils.js
TEST: tests/test_interface_text_is_composed_through_the_catalog.py (24 sites: the joined form is gone and the translated-before-joining form is
present; plus `stepLabel` builds a character's step label from its key) -- before: 25 failed.
WHAT CHANGED: every probed site translates BEFORE anything is joined. Two forms: a sentence with the value inside it is one `{var}` key with the
value filled in after the lookup ("Summary through turn {n}", "{n} memories recalled", "How {name} currently feels…", "Rebuilding {name}'s earlier
eras", "⇉ ran concurrently with {steps}", "Reading {file}…", "covers: {regions}", "NSFW: ON"); a complete phrase with a value appended -- or a name
before it, where the catalog's entry was written for exactly that ("Hinami 何をすべきか決めている") -- keeps its EXISTING key and appends the value
("Map unavailable:", "Load error:", "Search error:", "Error:", "Turn", "last: turn", "Source:" + ". Saving stores…", "Could not reach the server.",
"is deciding what to do", "hasn't formed any tracked opinions…", "zones still covered:"). That second form keeps 12 translations working with no
catalog change and keeps login.html's coincidental "Could not reach the server." translation alive. A character's step label is rebuilt as
`t("Character · {name}", { name })` from the step key (`stepLabel`) in the live log, the ✓ line and the drawer heading, which now also carry
`t("(stale)")`; the elements opt out so the translated label is not looked up twice.
REQUEST (language packs): see CATALOG below.

##### CATALOG — request for the language packs (this lane's keys only)
Exact data: <review scratch>/fixes/L15a_catalog_request.json
(computed by <review scratch>/l15a/my_catalog_diff.py with every non-lane file held at HEAD).
1. After all lanes land, run `.venv/bin/python tools/extract_ui_catalog.py` (en/ui.json is regenerated from every file; `make structure` fails until then).
2. language_packs/ja/ui.json: add the 26 `ja_add` entries (translations supplied, placeholders and protocol tokens checked with
   `project_check.canonical_language_tokens`: no drift), remove the 34 `ja_remove` keys.
3. language_packs/ja/translation_exceptions.json: add `"Tab": "keyboard key name, matched against KeyboardEvent.key"` (same wording as Escape /
   ArrowLeft); remove the entries for '${mems.length}', 'NSFW:', 'chat-${S.chatId}-debug.json' (their keys leave the harvest).
Until 1-3 land, the new `{var}` keys render in English under ja (the old joined strings did too, so nothing that was translated stops being
translated, except the sidebar aria-labels "X を開く" etc., which are screen-reader text).

##### REQUEST (tools, optional class fix) — inline scripts of static/*.html are never harvested
tools/extract_ui_catalog.py feeds a whole `<script>` body to `_message`, which rejects it as code, so every string guest.html and login.html set
from script is outside the catalog (pre-existing: "Waiting for this beat to resolve…", "Connection lost —", login's lockout and error lines). Fix:
in `_HTMLMessages`, track `<script>` (no src) and run its data through `_javascript_strings` + `_unescape_js` + `_message`. Then ja needs (proposed):
guest.html -- "Waiting for this beat to resolve…": "このビートの解決を待っています…"; "Superseded — this text was written before part of its beat
was re-run.": "無効化されました — このテキストは、ビートの一部が再実行される前に書かれました。"; "Partly out of date — part of this beat was re-run and
the rest was not recomputed.": "一部が古い — このビートの一部が再実行され、残りは再計算されていません。"; "Queued for the next beat. Sending again replaces
it.": "次のビートを待っています。もう一度送信すると置き換わります。"; "Connection lost — retrying.": "接続が切れました — 再試行しています。"; "Your session has
ended — ask the host for a new join code.": "セッションが終了しました — ホストに新しい参加コードを依頼してください。"; "— playing as": "— プレイ中:";
exceptions: "HTTP ${res.status}", "turn queued", "turn stale" (status template / CSS classes).
login.html -- "First-run setup": "初回セットアップ"; "Passwords do not match.": "パスワードが一致しません。"; "Username and password are required.":
"ユーザー名とパスワードが必要です。"; "You can try signing in again now.": "もう一度サインインを試せます。"; "One attempt at a time — try again in a moment.":
"一度に一回ずつです — 少し待ってから再試行してください。"; "No host account exists yet — reload this page to reach first-run setup.": "ホストアカウントがまだ
ありません — このページを再読み込みして初回セットアップを開いてください。"; "Could not reach the server. It may be stopped or restarting — this page is fine,
the connection is not.": "サーバーに到達できませんでした。停止中か再起動中の可能性があります — このページは正常で、問題は接続です。"; "Too many failed sign-in
attempts. Sign-in unlocks by itself in" / "s — no refresh needed." (a joined fragment pair, the F11 shape on the login page): "サインインの失敗が
続きました。自動的に解除されるまで残り" / "秒です — 再読み込みは不要です。".

###### (request from L15b) frontend_lorebooks_editors#F11 — a UI message with a line break was never translated
STATUS: fixed (in this lane's static/js/i18n-core.js, at L15b's request via the coordinator)
FILES: static/js/i18n-core.js (i18nLookup split out of i18nTranslate; new i18nCollapse; rules carry their `key`)
TEST: tests/test_a_line_broken_message_is_still_translated.py -- before: the lore placeholder ("…generate.\n\nExample: …") came back in English,
and `[' Delete', 'Delete\n'] != [' 削除', '削除\n']`; after: ja translates both real instances (lore placeholder, 🎧 tooltip), en returns them
byte-for-byte (line breaks kept), padding is re-attached, a padded translation is not re-translated, and a value written across lines is never
collapsed into a template. Existing browser_tests/test_ui_translator.py (incl. `test_a_long_line_costs_one_pass`, which pins "no placeholder
matches across a line") passes.
WHAT CHANGED: on a miss, the translator retries once with whitespace collapsed exactly as the extractor collapses keys, and answers only with a REAL
translation (an entry mapping the message to itself -- all of en -- leaves the string as written) wrapped in the original padding. Whitespace is
collapsed only in the catalog's own text: a template's captures are mapped back (`i18nCollapse`'s index map) and filled with what the page wrote,
and a capture that spans a line break refuses the rule, as before. The E1 equivalence test now compares `i18nLookup` (index vs full scan) so the two
layers are pinned separately. Miss cost unchanged (re-benched: en 155/54 ms, ja 185/61 ms per 10k prose/name misses).

###### frontend_settings_world#F1 — Undo re-issues a whole-field snapshot over later card edits and onto another room
- STATUS: fixed
- FILES: `static/js/world_browser.js` (`wbUndoStack`, `wbWrite`, the map's `writeParts`/`writeExtent`/`writeAnchors`/`dropDoorway`/`dropBody`/`dropCharter`/`dropThing`, `ctx.patchRoom`/`ctx.patchDoorway`/`ctx.showRoom`, `selectTab`, `renderMapBar`)
- TEST: `browser_tests/test_the_world_browser_writes_only_what_it_read.py::test_a_card_edit_after_a_drag_takes_undo_away`, `::test_undo_does_not_follow_the_host_into_another_room`. Before the fix (unchanged file):
  `AssertionError: Locator expected to have count '0' - unexpected value "1"` (both: `.wb-map-bar .wb-undo` still offered after a card write, and after following Nathan's room link from the Bodies tab into the Study).
- WHAT CHANGED: `wbWrite` clears the Undo stack on every successful write (a drag clears and then remembers its own inverse); each remembered inverse records the room it was made in and re-issues through THAT room (`ctx.patchRoom`/`patchDoorway` take an explicit room; the bar offers Undo only for the selected room); `showRoom` now goes through `selectTab("rooms", id)` -> `loadRoom(id)`, so leaving the room clears its pending forms and Undo (it used to set `state.selected` first, which made `loadRoom`'s reset test answer "same room"). The contract comment was rewritten (it said a card write cleared Undo; nothing did).
- REGISTER: UNBUILT_PLANNING §2.26 says "a write from the card clears it" — now true (any write clears it, and so does a room change); the same section's "Undo is ONE step and only for a drag" still holds.

###### frontend_settings_world#F2 — the structured editors write whole lists from the card's last read
- STATUS: fixed (client-side re-read guard; one request below for the server half)
- FILES: `static/js/world_browser.js` (new `WB_ROOM_LISTS`, `wbPatchRoomList`, `wbAttireEntryOf`; `wbRoomFields` parts, `wbExits`, `wbWindows`, `wbAnchors`, `wbAttireEditor`, `ctx.patchRoomList`, `ctx.addAnchorAt`, the map's parts/anchors drops and their Undo)
- TESTS:
  - `browser_tests/test_the_world_browser_writes_only_what_it_read.py::test_an_anchor_edit_is_refused_when_the_room_changed_since_the_card_was_drawn`, `::test_an_exit_edit_is_refused_when_a_beat_built_a_doorway_since`, `::test_a_garment_edit_is_refused_when_the_body_changed_since_the_tab_was_drawn` (+ `::test_an_unchanged_room_still_takes_the_edit`, which passes before and after). Before the fix: `AssertionError: Locator expected to contain text 'This room changed since the card was drawn' ... unexpected value "✓Saved.✕"` (the stale list was PATCHed; same shape for the attire PUT).
  - `tests/test_a_room_write_answers_what_the_next_read_reads.py` (2 tests, pass): the guard's premise on the REAL routes — the answer of `PATCH /rooms/{id}` (exits, anchors, parts) and of `PATCH /doorways/...` equals a following `GET /rooms/{id}` byte for byte per list, and `body_rows`' attire equals `GET /attire`'s entry. If that ever drifts, every second card edit would be refused as "changed", so it is pinned.
- WHAT CHANGED: picked the approach the server already supports for ALL four list kinds — a re-read (`GET /rooms/{id}`, `GET /attire`) immediately before the whole-list write, compared with the list the edit was made against; a moved room/entry is refused with a toast ("This room changed since the card was drawn — ... Nothing was saved; the card now shows what the room holds.") and `wbWrite`'s error path redraws from the server. Per-item doorway routes were considered for exits and NOT used: `_apply_doorway_fields` writes a `one_way_window` onto BOTH edges and mints a passage record, where `_apply_exits` keeps that barrier per edge — switching writers would have changed doorway semantics beyond the finding, and the existing pin `test_adding_an_exit_shows_the_doorway_from_both_rooms` (full list) stays true. Undo's inverse for anchors/parts is guarded the same way (against the list the drag left).
- REQUEST (`web/world_routes.py`, owner of the room routes): close the one-request read-to-write gap with a precondition — accept an optional `"expect": {"<field>": <list as read>}` (or a hash) on `PATCH /rooms/{id}` and answer 409 "this room changed since you opened it" when the stored list differs; the client can then send `before` instead of re-reading. Not needed for correctness of this lane's fix.
- REQUEST (`browser_tests/test_world_browser.py`, not owned): `test_a_side_handle_resizes_the_room_and_the_shape_is_chosen_on_the_map` reads `writes` immediately after clicking "Add part" with no wait, which only worked while the PATCH left during the click; the guard puts a GET first. Exact change — move the line
  `expect(modal.locator(".wb-room-map .wb-m-part[data-part='0']")).to_have_count(1)`
  up to directly after `add.get_by_role("button", name="Add part").click()` (before `patches = [...]`). Nothing it asserts changes; it waits for the redraw that follows the PATCH. Until applied that one test fails with `IndexError: list index out of range` at line 1477.
- REGISTER: AGENTS.md:532 / the file header's "exits, anchors and parts as this room's full list" still holds (they are still full lists); add "written only over the list the card was drawn from (re-read and compared at the write)".

The 7 existing `test_checkpoint_compaction_ui.py` tests still pass.

###### frontend_lorebooks_editors#F2 — "Update" runs an extension's new code with no consent
- STATUS: fixed (client); the server half is lane L2's
- FILES: `static/js/settings.js` (new `extensionEnableConsent`, used by the Enable toggle and by `update.onclick`)
- TEST: `browser_tests/test_the_settings_panels_say_what_happened.py::test_an_extension_update_asks_first_and_runs_again_only_when_enabled`. Before: `AssertionError: Locator expected to contain text 'switched off' / element(s) not found` (no dialog; the update POST went out on the click).
- WHAT CHANGED: Update now asks first ("The new version is fetched from its repository, and nothing has reviewed it. It is left switched off until you enable it again, after seeing what the new version asks to do."). After the update the old browser half is unloaded; if the answer still says `enabled` (a server from before L2's change) the client POSTs `disable`; then, for an extension that had been enabled, the Enable dialog is shown over the NEW version's `disclosures`, and only a confirm POSTs `enable` and loads the new UI script. The toast says which happened ("updated and enabled" / "updated and left switched off — enable it to run the new version"). An answer of `updated: false` (the remote had not moved) says "Up to date." and asks nothing, since nothing new was fetched. Whether to offer the Enable dialog is `enabled || disabled_for_consent` (L2's flag).
- NOTE for L2: the client reads the update route's answer as the listing row (`id`, `enabled`, `disclosures`, ...), which `update_extension` already returns; leave it disabled there and this needs nothing more. Until then the new code has run `register(api)` once server-side before the client switches it off — only the server can close that.
- REGISTER: AGENTS.md "capabilities are DISCLOSURE for the consent dialog, never enforcement" — still true; Update now has the consent moment.

###### agents_common#F5 (brief F1) — two word-list guards matched SUBSTRINGS of free prose and ended beats
- STATUS: fixed (the cue tables unchanged; a third substring test of the same tables, the reaction-phase gate's `_REACTIVE_VERBS`, fixed with them — same class, same file).
- FILES: `agents/common_acts.py` (new `_names_a_cue`, `_whole_cue_regex`, `_CUE_INFLECTION`; `acts_awaiting_resolution`, `_classify_action_commitment`, `_requires_reaction_phase` read through it).
- TEST: `tests/test_a_cue_is_a_whole_word_never_a_piece_of_one.py` (33). Before: 19 failed, e.g.
  `AssertionError: eats breakfast by the window / assert not True` (`_requires_director_resolution`),
  every `IN_WORD_ATTEMPT` row (`'contestable' == 'asserted'`), the stealthily reaction-phase row, and the
  ja row's `breakfastを食べる`.
- WHAT CHANGED: a cue is a whole word: an edge in a spaced script needs no spaced-script letter beside it
  (`SPACED_WORD_CHARS`), the right edge after an optional inflection — the `(?:e?s|ed|ing|d)?` shape the
  pack's own verb alternations carry, plus the doubled consonant ("grabbing"); an edge in an unspaced script
  stays open, so Japanese is byte-for-byte as before (a ja verb carries its inflection on the same run). Every
  new match is a match the old `cue in text` made (asserted on the corpus below). The measurement sits in
  `_names_a_cue`'s docstring.
- RE-MEASURED (owner db, read-only, `<review scratch>/l16/measure_cues.py`; today's 2,500 most recent active
  interaction_loop variants hold 1,273 distinct action elements, not the audit's 2,554): asserted, overt
  conflict-verb exits 76 -> 51 (all 25 removed are in-word: center 15, stable 3, restraint(s) 4,
  entertaining, forceful, interleaved, established); attempt-cue `contestable` 56 -> 25 (all 31 removed
  in-word: brush/brushing, pastry, recharge, claim/claiming, "reach forward" for "reach for", entry/entrance).
  The word-level remainder (breaks 10, force 10, break 9, breaking 7, grabbing 7, casts 2 …) still fires;
  whether "breaks eye contact" / "casts a glance" belong in the table is the next measurement (owner).
  The reaction-phase whitelist (`_REACTIVE_VERBS`) on the same corpus's 56 contestable acts: 12 -> 11 (the one
  removed: "charge" inside "power recharge").
- NOT CHANGED (same class, logged as residual): `_is_autonomous_response`'s `phrase in low` over
  `_AUTONOMY_PHRASES` ("give in" inside "give instructions") and `_reaches_inward`'s `state in low` over
  `_INTERIOR_STATES` ("lust" in "cluster", "rage" in "garage"). The second is a firewall-direction floor (an
  interior act is never delivered): a whole-word rule drops "fearful"/"guilty"/"hopeful", which would DELIVER
  private cognition — needs a measurement and an owner call first. Japanese compound verbs (気に入る for 入る,
  見入る) still match: no script boundary tells 部屋に入る from 気に入る (pack data, owner).
- REGISTER: none found that states the substring behaviour.

###### agents_common#F1 (brief F2) — LEAK (ja): a Japanese stranger's label carried their given name
- STATUS: fixed
- FILES: `agents/common_names.py` (new `_name_parts`, `_unspaced_name_parts`; `_summary_label`'s head-final branch strips the name's parts after its forms).
- TEST: `tests/test_an_unspaced_name_is_withheld_by_its_parts.py` (11, shared with F2). Before:

  `見知らぬヒナミさんは…`, and `見知らぬハルトは衛兵の制服を着た若い男` (role case).
- WHAT CHANGED: the head-final/unspaced branch strips, after the whole name and aliases, every script run of
  the name (`_name_tokens`: 佐藤 | ヒナミ for 佐藤ヒナミ and 佐藤・ヒナミ) at its script's identifying length
  (`_name_token_floor`), exempting titles, generic heads, function words and the role's own runs — the

- RESIDUAL (request, pack data): ja `agents.common._NAME_TAIL_PATTERN` takes a particle but no honorific, so
  ヒナミさんは… now labels 見知らぬさんは狐耳の… (no leak, ugly). Request to the pack owner: let the ja tail take
  an optional honorific before the particle, e.g.
  `(?:['’]s|(?:さん|様|さま|ちゃん|くん|君|殿)?(?:は|が|を|に|と|も|へ|で|の|から)?)` (the honorific set is
  `language_runtime.JA_NAME_FOLLOWERS`' own).

###### agents_common#F4 (brief F4) — LEAK (ja, channel): a Japanese stage direction was delivered as speech
- STATUS: fixed
- FILES: `agents/common_sequence.py` (`split_stage_directions` counts a span with `common_names._word_count`;
  the orphan-mark strip after the cut also takes `、，。；：`); `agents/common_names.py` (new `_word_count`).

  `AssertionError: 身を乗り出して彼女の肩に手を置く 座ったほうがいい。 / assert '身を…置く 座ったほうがいい。' ==
  '座ったほうがいい。'` (and through `norm_sequence` no action was promoted); `*彼女の手を取って*、行こう。` came
  back whole (`('彼女の手を取って、行こう。', [])` on the pristine copy).
- WHAT CHANGED: a span's words are counted in its own script: a spaced span by `.split()` (byte-identical,
  "*well-known*" pinned as one word); an unspaced span by its CONTENT runs (`fold_words` less hiragana runs —
  the use `fold_words`' own docstring names), so 本当 / 本当に / はい stay emphasis as "*really*" does and a
  phrase of several content words is promoted. NOT `_countable_word_units` as the audit suggested: one unit per
  character would make 本当 two words and cut an emphasised word out of the line (`*本当*に？` -> `に？`).
  Parity residual, same as English: a one-content-word direction (頷く, 微笑む, like "*nods*") stays spoken.
- REGISTER: none.

###### agents_common#F3 (brief F5) — LEAK (ja): the presence-knowledge channel floor could not fire in Japanese
- STATUS: fixed
- FILES: `agents/common_authority.py` (`_check_presence_knowledge_channel`; new `_unspaced_definite`,
  `_DETERMINER_MODIFIERS = 2` (the English `{0,2}`, named), `_CLAUSE_EDGE`, `_PHRASE_ENDING_PARTICLES`);
  `agents/common_names.py` (new `_word_count`, shared with F4).
- TEST: `tests/test_the_presence_knowledge_floor_reads_japanese.py` (9). Before: 4 failed, e.g.
  `AssertionError: その奇妙な硬貨のことは知っている / assert []`, `その硬貨はここでは使えない。 / assert []`.
- WHAT CHANGED: (1) the form length floor is `_display_floor(p, spaced=3)` (硬貨 counts at two); (2) "is this
  a phrase" is `_word_count(p) >= 2` (content runs in an unspaced script: 奇妙な硬貨 = 2); (3) an unspaced noun
  is bounded by `name_boundary_pattern`, and a determiner of an unspaced script binds the noun it is written
  onto across at most two modifiers in the same clause and no phrase-ending particle (`JA_NAME_LEADERS` less
  の, so この町では硬貨 is "this town", never "these coins"). Spaced-script forms keep their exact old
  patterns, and every determiner still goes through the old spaced shape too (a superset of old behaviour).
  The copper-and-silver rule is pinned in both languages (generic 硬貨 talk is never flagged).
- RESIDUAL: an adverb that begins with a determiner (そのまま硬貨を渡した) still reads as definite — the
  English shape has the same class ("this area's coins"); the determiner list is pack data.
- REGISTER: none.

  would re-segment ~32% of texts under every guard that reads sentences — an owner call (UNBUILT_PIPELINE 1.69).

  this is the A2 skeptic's "window unit needs thought" deferral, decided as: the engine's one unspaced
  language puts its predicate last. Spaced heads byte-identical (pinned). The ja verb alternations hold
  dictionary forms only (言う, not 言った) — pack data; the checks now fire on what the pack lists.

###### C#F1 — `observations_from_render` told a mind a line it could not hear was said to it (LEAK)
STATUS: fixed
FILES: agents/composer_speech.py (new `_said_to_you`; `speech_percept`, the seen-speaking-unheard percept, `communication_percept`, `_names_kept`)
TEST: tests/test_an_unheard_line_is_never_said_to_you.py (6). Before: 5 failed, e.g. `AssertionError: the observation told the listener a line it could not hear was said to it: [{... 'observed': {'text': 'A voice — the words do not carry.'}, ... 'directed_at_self': True ...}] vs [{... no directed_at_self ...}]` (voice, fragment, seen-unheard, fragmentary communication, and the own-name case all differed by addressee).
WHAT CHANGED: the flag is decided where the percept is minted, by what the page carries: at `full` it is the addressee test as before; below `full` (fragment, voice, seen-speaking-unheard, a communication "speaks indistinctly") it is exactly `own_name` -- the one self-directed fact such a page says ("You catch your name in it.") -- and `_names_kept`, which moves a voice group's `own_name` onto its first line, moves the flag with it. Two percepts differing only in `intended_target`/`targets` now project byte-identical rows. The full-fidelity addressee keeps the flag (pinned).
RUN: the new file + every test file naming speech_percept/communication_percept/_names_kept/observations_from_render/directed_at_self/own_name (81 files): 2044 passed, 2 xfailed.
REGISTER: UNBUILT_WORLD §1.28 (observation metadata "consumed by nothing") — the audit notes the model reading the payload is the consumer; nothing to edit, but CLAUDE.md's "the second representation cannot expand the information budget" now holds for `directed_at_self`.

###### C#F3 — `ambient_percepts` handed a roomless observer every room's sounds (LEAK)
STATUS: fixed

Each now passes the event's own room (`"entrance_hall"`); every channel assertion is unchanged.

REGISTER: none.

###### P#F1 — the sense card gated bodies and lines only: a blind card was shown the room, a deaf card heard it (LEAK)
STATUS: fixed
FILES: agents/perception_senses.py (new `_CARD_CUTS`, `_channels_the_card_cuts`, `_where_without_eyes`, `_card_admitted`; `_sight_detail` refuses conduct to an absent sight), agents/perception_standing.py (new `_heard_without_eyes` on the sound shape), agents/perception_opening.py / perception_onset.py / perception_outcome_views.py (one call each, before `_composer_company`), agents/composer_things.py (`room_content_percepts` marks a thing in the observer's own keeping `kept`), tests/test_perception_card_senses.py (extended, as the lane brief asked)
TEST: tests/test_perception_card_senses.py — 6 new tests (`test_an_ordinary_card_is_given_the_kitchen` baseline, `test_a_blind_card_is_not_shown_the_room` [outcome], `..._at_onset`, `..._at_the_opening` [establish], `test_a_deaf_card_hears_no_room`, `test_an_anosmic_card_smells_no_room`). Before: `AssertionError: an authored-blind card was shown 'You can see': 'You are in Kitchen. A warm kitchen with a scrubbed table. You can see the long oak counter within arm's reach. Through the back door is The Yard. A muddy yard. The noise from the generator is a din at the long oak counter. ... There is the silver coin at the long oak counter. Hinami crosses the hall toward the window. ...'`; deaf: `'generator' not in ...` failed; anosmic: `'the smell of burnt toast' not in ...` failed; the opening test was confirmed failing with the floor patched out.
WHAT CHANGED: one floor per stage, asked after every builder has spoken (`_card_admitted`, at the three IR assembly points): a percept on a channel the card says is ABSENT (sight/hearing/scent, `sense_acuity_offset` None) does not reach the mind -- this covers every `_gated_ambient_percepts`/`heard_events`/`distant_sound_percepts` site, the environment/things/openings/notices/chatter/soundscape/weather/body-region builders, the unseen-act "Something moves behind you" and the silence percept for a deaf card. Never more than the card says: own-body percepts (`source_label` "you") and things in the observer's own hands (`kept`) are proprioception/touch; a sightless body keeps WHERE it stands (`_where_without_eyes`: room name + the room's own description, which the dark leaves a sighted body too, on channel `mixed`); touch, interoception and `mixed` are no card's to take; dulled/keen cards are untouched (the builders that grade still grade). Two earlier-stage fixes for the same class: `_sight_detail` returns "none" for an absent sight (same-room acts were seen by a blind card: "Hinami crosses the hall toward the window" was in the blind card's outcome view, channel sight), and the soundscape a sightless ear gets drops the shape's eye-placed `groups`/`openings` ("a din at the long oak counter" -> "Where you are, you are in the din."). Every withholding is recorded (`note_step_decision("sense_card", ...)`).
RUN: the 12 card tests + every test file naming the perception stages/senses/sound_shape/room_content_percepts (213 files): 3684 passed.
OWNER QUESTION (kept, flagged): a sightless body keeps the room's authored description ("A warm kitchen with a scrubbed table."), on the precedent that the dark keeps it for a sighted one; free prose cannot be split by sense. Drop it too if the owner reads room notes as sight.
RESIDUAL: a soundscape heard by a sightless ear no longer names its source (the origin clause rides the anchor-graded sentence); a same-room act a blind card cannot see is refused, not converted to a heard movement (the rear-arc branch's "Something moves" is the only heard-act form, and an occluded same-room act already gets nothing).
REGISTER: `composer._sense_graded`'s docstring ("an authored-blind card saw the room in its onset view ... and was correctly blind for the length of one micro-round") now holds for the room; Design.md:336 ("a player whose card says sight is absent was handed a body in a lit hall" -- fixed) now holds for the environment half too; tests/test_perception_card_senses.py's docstring (bodies and lines) is extended in the file.

###### P#F2 — a heard tell was graded by none of the rules the line beside it uses (LEAK + its inverse)
STATUS: fixed
FILES: agents/perception_stream.py (`_delivered_manifest` gains `sound=`/`voices=`; new `_TELL_VOLUME`, `_tell_volume`, `_loudest_voices`), agents/perception_outcome_stage.py (passes the perceiver's own field and the beat's loudest voice per speaker)
TEST: tests/test_a_tell_is_heard_as_a_line_is.py (9). Before (5 failed): deaf card in a dark cellar got `['a ragged breath catches in the dark']`; a breath carried 28.5 paces down a dark hall (`whisper: none, normal: full`) and was delivered; a voice tell across an open doorway was `{}`; a blind card in a lit room was handed `{'surface_demeanor': 'outwardly calm', 'cues': ['a glance at the door']}`.
WHAT CHANGED: sight is graded THROUGH the card (`composer._sense_graded`, as every other sight reader in the module), and hearing by the line's own rule: `hear_level` on the relation built with this perceiver's field, at the loudness the tell carries (a breath at a whisper's rung -- "a whisper is breath with no voicing"; a voice tell at the source's loudest line this beat, else a normal voice; any other heard channel at a normal voice, as before), through the card's ears, with no `same_room` precondition; a deaf card hears no tell. A tell is caught by the sense that reached it: seen-only at the sight acuity, heard at the hearing acuity (`_tell_acuity(sheet, channels)`), so a poor eye no longer borrows an ordinary ear's reach (`test_a_tell_is_caught_by_the_sense_that_reached_it`). The pinned source line `cues.append(t.get("cue"))` (tests/test_tells_reach_the_page.py) is unchanged.
RESIDUAL: the field passed is the perceiver map's, built before the beat's own sound events are known (the outcome derives them inside `_composer_outcome_views`), so a `drowns` event does not yet mask a tell -- P#F4's class at the manifest; the full fix is building the beat's sound records before the perceiver loop (a refactor of `_composer_outcome_views`' prelude).

###### P#F5 — `_tell_acuity` read a card with its own vocabulary
STATUS: fixed
FILES: agents/perception_stream.py (`_tell_acuity`, new `_TELL_ACUITY_BY_OFFSET = {-1: 0.2, 0: 0.4, 1: 0.65, 2: 0.9}`)
TEST: tests/test_a_tell_reads_the_card_as_the_world_does.py (15). Before: 10 failed, e.g. `assert 0.4 == 0.65` for `sight: keen`, `eyes: keen`, `vision: acute`, `vision: sharp`, `audition: expert`; `0.4 == 0.9` for `hearing: extraordinary`; `0.4 == 0.0` for a blind+deaf card.
WHAT CHANGED: one reader: the world's `sense_acuity_offset` per channel, on the owner's 0.4/0.65/0.9 scale (dulled 0.2 = the old "impaired"; absent 0.0).
BEHAVIOUR CHANGE TO NOTE (measured read-only on the owner's db, 2026-11-01): 22 vision/hearing card entries grade higher (`enhanced` x10, `expert` x6, `extraordinary` x5, `sharp`, `acute`, `high`, `superior`); three `heightened` entries grade 0.65 where the old table said 0.75; `exceptional` (0.9 in the old table, +1 in the world's) is keen (0.65) -- no live vision/hearing card uses it; `vision: failing` 0.4 -> 0.2. `general` (the legacy default card's channel, always `ordinary` in the corpus -- 0 non-ordinary entries) is no channel the world reads.
REGISTER: mind/character_jev.py's MISS comment ("A keen eye (0.65) counts as one advantage and an exceptional one (0.9) as two") now reads "extraordinary" for the 0.9 rung.

WHAT CHANGED: an observation is a `change` only on the observer's own evidence: the renderer's `beat` mark (stamped from the observer's ledger), an appearance `delta` (attached only for an observer who held the body), or -- on the character tier, which marks no beat -- `force` on a body this observer HELD at full sight last beat (`held`, perception's `saw_before`). A first sight on the beat a body's form moved is `state`. Pinned: tests/test_composer_poses.py::...test_a_forced_change_is_never_filed_as_standing (force+delta stays a change) is green.

FILES: agents/perception_conduct.py (`_declares_rapid_movement`, new `RUN_PACE = "run"` -- the engine's word, `director_movement.RUN_PACE`; the director facade does not re-export it, so it is spelled once more here with a pointer)

###### P#F10 — seven of twelve `agents.perception` pack tables have no engine reader
STATUS: noted (+ request) -- the tables live in `language_packs/*/cards/linguistics.json`, not this lane's files
EVIDENCE (repo grep, .py, docs excluded): `_AMBIGUITY_CUES`, `_INTENSITY_CUES`, `_SUDDENNESS_CUES` -- no reader at all; `_SELF_DIRECTED` -- read by three tests only (test_observation_derivation.py, test_continuous_contact_sensation.py, test_firewall_across_scripts.py; the code mentions it in one comment); `_RAISING`/`_LOWERING` -- read by tests/test_perception_sentence_split.py and tools/build_japanese_pack.py, and in code only by the comment on the cut tripwire (perception_scrub); `_CHANNEL_CUES` -- read only by `perception_common._atom_channel`, itself read only by tests/test_firewall_across_scripts.py.
REQUEST (language-pack owner): delete `_AMBIGUITY_CUES`, `_INTENSITY_CUES`, `_SUDDENNESS_CUES` from `agents.perception` in both packs' linguistics.json (no reader anywhere). The other four go only with their test pins (and `_atom_channel` with them).

###### C#E2 — `_episode_sentence` ~3x per act per observer per beat
STATUS: skipped -- a percept is not hashable (its `data` is a dict), so a memo needs either an identity-keyed cache (ids are reused after collection) or a memo threaded through `perception_onset`'s `said`, the outcome's dedupe and `render_episode` by hand; the audit measured ~80 ms on a busy beat. Not worth an unsafe cache.

###### C#E1 — stranger label ladders recomputed per observer at every call site
STATUS: fixed
FILES: agents/composer_figures.py (new `_ladder_of`, `scene_memo` under `("stranger_label_ladder", language, name, appearance, aliases, role, noun)`; `assign_stranger_labels(bodies, scene=None)`; `observer_display_map` passes its scene)
TEST: none new (no behaviour change); 27 test files naming the display map / stranger labels / pack tables / tell gate: 487 passed. Measured (6 observers x 6 mutual strangers, inside a read pass, loaded machine): 37.7 ms -> 7.3 ms, identical labels. Outside a pass, or with no scene (a direct caller), it runs as before; the memo dies with the pass, so there is no cap.

###### C#E3 — pack tables rebuilt per call
STATUS: fixed
FILES: agents/composer_common.py (`_pack_words` cached per (active language, table), cleared through `language_runtime.on_packs_reloaded`), agents/composer_figures.py (`_label_core` reads `_pack_words("articles")` and a module-level `_ENGLISH_ORDINALS`)
MEASURED: `_pack_words` 5.5 us -> 0.33 us; `_noun_phrase` 7-23 us -> 8.7 us; `_label_core` 24 us -> 16.8 us.

###### Tell gate card reading (follow-on to P#F2)
`_delivered_manifest` read the card with `_sense_card` and `_tell_acuity` with its own dispatch (which also accepts a bare `{"senses": [...]}`); both now read one `_card_of(sheet)`, so the gate's sight, hearing and acuity cannot answer from two different cards.

No template added.
- L12b, unnamed `far_look_hidden_*` / `far_look_hidden_wall` templates: NOT DONE -- not in this lane's list, and a new kind in `composer_landscape` plus four pack strings is a design addition; orchestrator's call.
